# AGENTS.md — dotfiles handbook for AI agents

Read this first. It describes the machine, the repo, the conventions, and what has already been built, so you can continue without re-deriving it.

## Working rules (mandatory)

### 0. Public repo: never write real setup details

This repo is **public on GitHub**. Never write anything identifying the user's real setup into it: in code, comments, docs, tests, notes, commit messages or screenshots. That means:
- no real hostnames / server or machine names, Tailscale or tailnet names (`*.ts.net`, MagicDNS short names); <!-- privacy-ok -->
- no real URLs or IP addresses (LAN/RFC1918, Tailscale `100.64.0.0/10`), MAC addresses, device serials; <!-- privacy-ok -->
- no usernames, accounts or emails (the user's or anyone's), tokens or keys, personal channel / server / library names.

Use placeholders instead: `jellyfin.example`, `example-host`, `192.0.2.10`, `user@example.com`, `<token>`. Real values live outside the repo (`~/.config/<tool>/...`, `~/.local/state/...`) or in git-ignored files (`config/hypr/.secrets`). Notes for future agents describe mechanisms, not the user's values. Agents may mention real values to the user in chat, never in repo files.
Enforced by `.githooks/pre-commit` (`git config core.hooksPath .githooks`, set by `install.sh`): it blocks staged lines matching generic patterns plus a private deny-list at `~/.config/dotfiles-private/denylist` (outside the repo). A line containing `privacy-ok` is skipped; `--no-verify` only on the user's explicit decision.

### 1. Global rules

Subagent delegation, change documentation and the no-attribution rule live in the global `~/dotfiles/CLAUDE.md` (symlinked to `~/.claude*`). They apply here too; they are not repeated in this file.

### 2. Safety rules learned the hard way (repo-specific)

- **Kill Quickshell only by exact PID.** The user's shell is the bare `/usr/bin/quickshell` process. `pgrep -x quickshell` also matches other instances (the Twitch app, probes); `kill`ing by name or relaunching blindly creates duplicates.
- **Don't edit a popup's QML file while that popup is open.** Hot reload crashed Qt's Repeater (segfault) twice. Test in an isolated instance (`quickshell -n -p <dir>`) or with the popup closed.
- Hot reload only works for files already loaded. A new QML type needs a `qmldir` entry AND a full quickshell restart: the running shell does not re-read `qmldir` on hot reload ("X is not a type", seen 2026-10-22 with NotchMask, which blanked Settings → Bar and Wallpaper until restart). Restart: kill the exact quickshell PID, then `hyprctl eval 'hl.exec_cmd("env QSG_USE_SIMPLE_ANIMATION_DRIVER=1 quickshell")'` (`hyprctl dispatch exec` silently does nothing with the Lua config). Changed `.frag`/`.qsb` shaders are not hot-reloaded either.
- Don't touch the screen-sharing portal routing (`config/xdg-desktop-portal`). ScreenCast/RemoteDesktop/Screenshot must stay on the Hyprland portal.
- **Secrets:** `config/hypr/.secrets` (Twitch `TWITCH_SECRET`, `TWITCH_CLIENT_ID`, Home Assistant `HA_URL`, `HA_TOKEN`) is git-ignored; placeholders live in the tracked `config/hypr/.secrets.example`. Never print, log or commit values. WiVRn pairing data is also not tracked.
- **Licensed fonts stay out of the repo.** Font Awesome 7 Pro Sharp is installed in `~/.local/share/fonts/FontAwesomePro/` only; the repo is public on GitHub.
- Screenshot tips: `grim -g "x,y WxH"`; Hyprland dims unfocused windows (focus first when sampling colours); popups open on the *focused* monitor.

## The machine

- Arch-based (CachyOS), **Hyprland 0.56 with the native Lua config** (`hl.*`, not a plugin), Quickshell 0.3.2 (QML) for bar/launcher/notifications, hyprpaper, hyprlock.
- Two monitors on the "battlestation" host: `DP-3` (main, y=0) and `DP-1` (second, below it), 2560x1440. Per-host differences live in `config/hypr/hyprland/host.lua`.
- Only `~/dotfiles/config` is live (symlinked into `~/.config`). The Nix files (`flake.nix`, `home.nix`, `hosts/`, `modules/`, `system/`, `lib/`) are an **old setup**, kept for reference. `install.sh` is the Arch bootstrap.
- UI font: Paper Mono. Icon font: **Font Awesome 7 Pro Sharp (Regular)** plus Font Awesome Brands (system package) for brand logos.
- Hyprland dispatch: `hyprctl dispatch "hl.dsp.focus({workspace=N})"`; window focus `hl.dsp.focus({window="address:0x..."})`. Lua globals can be called with an IIFE: `hyprctl dispatch '(function() if pip_bar_changed then pip_bar_changed() end return hl.dsp.exec_cmd("true") end)()'`. Lua monitor objects expose name/serial/description but **not** model.

## Repository map

```
config/
  hypr/            hyprland.lua + hyprland/*.lua (env, settings, theme, binds, rules, workspaces,
                   monitors, input, apps, autostart, host, permissions), scripts/ (power menu, TV mode,
                   pip-reserve, wallpaper start, ...), hyprlock.conf, hyprpaper.conf
  quickshell/      the desktop shell (bar, popups, notifications, launcher, OSD, Settings window incl. wallpaper/theme page)
  theme/           wallpaper-driven theme engine (theme.py, theme.toml, templates/, out/, bin/)
  kitty ghostty alacritty nvim zellij herdr nautilus opencode fastfetch wivrn
  xdg-desktop-portal systemd/user  portal routing + units
  brave-flags.conf
install.sh         Arch bootstrap (packages, symlinks)
plans.md tv-behavior.md   older notes
```

Quickshell replaced waybar, swaync and tofi; their config dirs (and the `qmenu` dmenu helper, `Menu.qml`, `hyprlauncher.conf`, the tofi template and the `launcher` layer rule) were removed 2026-10-20 (recoverable from git 836cd5b). Dangling `~/.config/{swaync,waybar}` symlinks from old installs can be deleted by hand.

## Theme system (`config/theme`)

- **One place to edit:** `theme.toml` (+ `templates/`). Never hard-code colours in an individual app config.
- Per-monitor wallpapers: see **Per-monitor wallpapers**. `theme.py apply <img>` extracts a palette from the wallpaper (own extractor — wallust/matugen were tried and average away small saturated accents), renders templates, and live-reloads: kitty, ghostty, alacritty, nvim (RPC sockets), zellij, herdr, hyprland/hyprlock/hyprpaper, quickshell, GTK css, KDE/qt6ct palette, hue-rotated Adwaita icons (`Wall-Adwaita`), Brave seed-colour theme.
- `python3 -I config/theme/selftest.py` must pass after touching the extractor; it has to work for any wallpaper.
- GTK4/libadwaita apps are translucent over the glass: see **GTK4 / libadwaita glass**.
- Known limits: GTK4/libadwaita (Nautilus) cannot restyle open windows; Brave only picks up the theme while closed (shim `~/.local/bin/brave`); Dolphin is low priority.
- Qt apps: `QT_QPA_PLATFORMTHEME=kde` (set in `hyprland/env.lua`), style Kvantum theme `Wall` (`qt_style`), icon theme `Wall-Adwaita-Qt` (see **Other toolkits**). Brave/Chromium dialogs use the generated GTK theme `Wall` via `GTK_THEME`, and portal FileChooser = `xdg-desktop-portal-gnome` (Nautilus-style).
- Wallpapers: `~/Pictures/wallpapers`. The Quickshell wallpaper picker is the Settings → Wallpaper & theme page, `SUPER+T` (`qs ipc call picker toggle`).
- Layout defaults in `theme.toml [settings]`: `rounding` 0, `gap` 0, `border_size` 2, `bar_floating` false, `smart_gaps` true, plus `bar_border` and `bar_opacity`, written to `out/palette.json` as real numbers/booleans (`bar_gap`/`bar_radius` were removed, the bar margin/radius are derived; theme.py's fallback for `rounding` is now 0, was 10). These drive **Quickshell only**: `out/palette.lua` still carries just `wallpaper`, `rounding` and colours, and Hyprland's values stay hand-written in `hypr/hyprland/` (keep the two in sync if you change a default). User overrides do not live here: see Appearance below.
- **Thunderbird (2026-10-22)**: `templates/thunderbird-userChrome.css` + `thunderbird-userContent.css` (shared variable block `config/theme/tb-vars.css`, pasted into both as `{{tb_vars}}` by `render_all`; about: pages such as settings / address book are content documents and ignore userChrome, so they get the variables from userContent) -> `out/thunderbird-*.css` -> `<default profile>/chrome/` by `apply_thunderbird()` (profile from `~/.thunderbird/installs.ini`), plus a marked `// >>> theme` block in the profile's `user.js` enabling `toolkit.legacyUserProfileCustomizations.stylesheets`. Runs on every theme `render`/`apply`; **restart Thunderbird to load**. `[settings] thunderbird` (on/off), `thunderbird_radius` (px, default `rounding`). A foreign userChrome/userContent is moved to `*.pre-theme` once; nothing else in the profile is touched. Approach: palette on TB's own Bolt/layout/toolbar/tree tokens (no structural selectors), Paper Mono, accent red; HTML mail bodies untouched (userContent is limited to `about:` pages).
  - Verified (TB 158, throwaway profile with Local Folders + fake mail, nested Hyprland, before/after screenshots): 3-pane (selected/unread rows, plain + HTML mail), context menu, app menu, compose, settings, address book, calendar. Not verified: hovered menu items, dialogs, IMAP-only UI (account hub), light-theme users. Selectors/variables can break on TB updates (re-diff against `omni.ja` `messenger/colors.css`).
  - Test rig recipe: nested Hyprland (`XDG_RUNTIME_DIR=/tmp/<x>/rt`, started hidden via `hl.dsp.exec_cmd(..., {workspace="special:x silent"})`, headless output `TEST` 1500x950 for grim), `thunderbird --profile <scratch> --no-remote --marionette`, Local Folders prefs need `storeContractID=@mozilla.org/msgstore/berkeleystore;1` and an absolute `directory`. To drive the UI without input injection: Marionette `Addon:Install {path, temporary:true}` of a tiny experiment WebExtension that polls a localhost HTTP server and runs chrome JS (`Cu.evalInSandbox` on the 3pane window: open tabs, `PanelUI.show()`, popups) or `messenger.*` calls.
- Details: `config/theme/README.md`.

## Quickshell (`config/quickshell`)

Palette comes from `~/.config/theme/out/palette.json` through the `Theme` singleton (`Theme.qml`): colours, `font`, `fontSize`, `iconFont` ("Font Awesome 7 Sharp"), `iconSize` (15), `barOpacity`, `barHeight`, `alpha()`, and the shape/spacing API (`radius`, `radiusChip/Item/Page/Deep/Modal`, `inner()`, `barGap`, `barRadius`, `barReserve`, `popupGap`, `edgeGap`, …; text sizes `fontSmall` 12 / `fontCaption` 11 and the brand font tokens: use these instead of literal pixel sizes), see **Appearance** below.

| File | Role |
|---|---|
| `shell.qml` | root: per-screen Variants for Bar, BarReserve, PopupHost, Toasts, Osd; Launcher, Settings; IPC handlers (`picker` = Settings wallpaper page, `launcher`, `screenshot`/`record` aliases, `notif`, `popup` (no chip to measure: the card hangs under the bell chip), `osd`, `brightness`, `settings`) |
| `Bar.qml`, `Chip.qml`, `BarLabel.qml` | the bar and its modules. Three sections (left Row, center Row placed by computed `x`: truly centered, only pushed aside when it would hit left/right; right Row) each = Repeater of Loaders over `BarWidgets.layoutFor(<this screen's key>)`. Every widget is a `Component` keyed by id in `widgetComps` (add a widget = registry entry + Component + map entry). A widget with nothing to show sets `property bool show: false` (media without player, empty tray) and its Loader hides. The bell (control center) chip is outside the layout, always last on the right, cannot be hidden; icon: DND → bell-slash (muted), `Notifs.count > 0` → bell + count (accent), otherwise hamburger `bars` (U+F0C9). A MouseArea over the bar background (under the chips) calls `Popups.closeAll()` on empty-bar clicks, since PopupHost's scrim has the bar strip cut out of its input region (written 2026-10-18, not yet tested live). Chips highlight (`active`) while their popup is open via `bar.isOpen(name)`; `bar.pop(name, item)` anchors from any section |
| `BarWidgets.qml` | per-monitor bar layout, `~/.local/state/quickshell/bar-widgets.json` = `{version: 2, default: {left,center,right,hidden}, monitors: {"model\|serial": {...}}}` (key = `BarConfig.keyOf`). Monitors without an entry use `default`. Old v1 files (`{id: false}` = hidden) are read as the default layout with those hidden and rewritten as v2 on the first change. Every layout is normalized on load: unknown ids dropped, duplicates removed, registry ids missing from it appended to their registry `section`. API: `layoutFor(key)`, `move/moveBy/shift/hide/show(key, id, ...)`, `copyFrom(key, srcKey)`, `reset(key)`; `shown(id)` = on any enabled bar (AiUsage / HomeAssistant pollers use it) |
| `Settings.qml`, `SettingsBar.qml`, `SettingsWallpaper.qml`, `SettingsAppearance.qml`, `SettingsAnimations.qml` | Settings window (pages: Bar, Wallpaper & theme, Appearance, Animations (see **Animations settings**), Glass): fullscreen overlay (`qs-settings`, click outside / Esc closes, registers via `PopupRegistration`), centered card (≥920×600 or ~60% of the screen; 920 so the content area stays ≥668px with the wider sidebar), sidebar = `pages` array (its width = max(200, widest entry's implicit width + margins): nav entry implicitWidth = icon 18 + spacing 10 + label at Theme.fontSize + 12 left / 18 right padding → 216px today, so the selected box holds "Wallpaper & theme" (134px) with room) (add a page: one entry + a QML file whose root sets implicitHeight; also list it in qmldir), page in a Flickable. Optional page hooks: `readonly property bool wantsFocus: true` (page gets keyboard focus on open/load; unhandled keys such as Escape bubble up to the card's `Keys.onEscapePressed`), `property Flickable flick` (set in `onLoaded`, lets the page scroll to an item) and `property Component headerItem` (instantiated by a Loader in the title row between title and close button, fills that width, doesn't scroll; the title row has a fixed height = close button (30px) and title, Loader and button are all vertically centered on it, so a header item never changes the row height — don't give the Loader `Layout.fillHeight`, it stretches the row to the whole page; the Component is declared in the page file so its ids resolve; a plain `Component {}` object, not an inline `component X:` type) and `property var memory` (Settings sets it in `onLoaded` to a per-page object kept on the window, `win.pageMemory[pageId]`: session state that survives the page being unloaded on close, never written to disk; the Wallpaper page keeps its tab per monitor, edited monitor and next-video mode there). Opened by the control-center gear (`Popups.settingsRequest`, opens on that popup's monitor) or `qs ipc call settings toggle\|open\|close`, `qs ipc call settings page bar|wallpaper|appearance` (`monitors` = old page id, kept as an alias that opens the Bar page); `qs ipc call picker toggle` (SUPER+T, unchanged Hyprland bind) = `settings.togglePage("wallpaper")`: opens Settings on the Wallpaper page, switches to it if Settings is open on another page, closes if already there. Wallpaper & theme page (`SettingsWallpaper.qml`; current layout and tabs: see **Per-monitor wallpapers** > Settings page; replaced the old standalone fullscreen picker overlay, which was removed together with its Hyprland layer rules and its opacity theme setting): one line in the Settings title row via `headerItem` (always visible while the grid scrolls): current wallpaper file name (muted 11px, right-aligned, elided in the middle) to the LEFT of the palette swatches (11, 20px high, width adapts 14–28px keeping ≥110px for the name); hovering a swatch replaces the name with "name  #hex" in the same Label (no layout shift). Verified in an isolated instance by logging scene-mapped centers: title, caption, swatches and close button share the same center y, row height 30 with and without hover, at 1536×893 and forced 900/920×600, thumbnail grid (≥2 columns of ~300px, 16:9, sized to the card width, non-virtualized Repeater in the Settings Flickable) of `wallpaper_dirs` (rescanned each time the page loads), click or arrows/Home/End + Enter/Space applies via `Quickshell.execDetached(python3 -I theme.py apply <img>)` (detached so closing Settings doesn't kill it); the cell shows "Applying…" until `Theme.wallpaper` changes (30 s fallback), Settings stays open so the new palette is visible. Caveat: `nch2.png` (16000×9000) exceeds Qt's 256 MB image allocation limit and shows an empty thumbnail (same as in the old picker). Bar page (the separate Monitors page was merged into it and `SettingsMonitors.qml` removed): "Monitors" = a wrapping row of cards (≥260px, side by side at the 920px minimum), one per screen: icon, model, connector · resolution, bar on/off Toggle (disabled for the last enabled bar, BarConfig also refuses), Top/Bottom buttons; clicking a card selects the monitor whose layout is edited below (accent border), monitors without a bar are dimmed but selectable and the editor shows a "bar is off" note. Then "Layout · <model>" head (own/default layout + "Copy from <other>" / "Reset" buttons in its trailing slot), preview strip, Left/Center/Right columns + Hidden tray. Chips: drag handle, icon, title, eye (hide/show); moving and reordering is drag-and-drop only (Drag/DropArea with a proxy item outside the ColumnLayout; the old ‹ › ↑ ↓ fallback buttons were removed, the user confirmed real-pointer drags work). Checked in an isolated instance at the normal card size and forced 920×600 with 2 monitors (one without a bar); the single-monitor case was not looked at. Monitor links (2026-10-22): the cards sit in a `MonitorCardRow` with a link button between neighbours, see **Monitor links**; the "Copy from <other>" buttons moved from the head's trailing slot to a wrapping row under the head (with 3 monitors the head overflowed the 668 px content width, an old bug). Caveats: blur: ONE layer rule in hypr/hyprland/rules.lua blurs every `qs-.*` namespace (ignore_alpha 0.1; layer-rule namespaces are matched against the WHOLE name, so a bare `^qs-` matches nothing and silently turned all blur off once); fullscreen modal overlays (`qs-settings`, ModalMenu kinds `qs-screenshot` / `qs-record` / `qs-menu`) have NO dim backdrop since 2026-10-18 (window `color: "transparent"`, still catches click-outside; the old ignore_alpha 0.6 rule for them was removed), so the 0.1 rule blurs only the card (0.85); the global `default` layout has no editor (only migrated/fallback) |
| `BarConfig.qml` | per-monitor `{enabled, edge}` (Settings → Bar, monitor cards) saved to `~/.local/state/quickshell/bars.json` (key `model\|serial`), refuses to disable the last bar, notifies Hyprland (`pip_bar_changed`) |
| `Appearance.qml` | singleton, the only writer of `~/.local/state/quickshell/appearance.json` (radius, gaps, border, floating bar, smart gaps globally and per monitor; Settings → Appearance), pokes Hyprland after each save. See **Appearance** below |
| `BarReserve.qml` | invisible strip that reserves `Theme.barReserve` (bar height + the floating gap); the bar itself uses `exclusiveZone: -1` so it ignores the monitor `reserved_area` |
| `Popups.qml`, `PopupHost.qml` | one popup at a time per screen; card placed with computed x/y (not anchors), content/container fade together. `Popups.focusedName()` / `Popups.focusedScreen()` = the focused monitor (use these, no per-file Hyprland lookups) |
| `CenterPanel.qml` (gear → Settings window) + `Notifs.qml`, `NotifCard.qml`, `NotifIcon.qml`, `Icons.qml`, `Toasts.qml` | control center and notification server (click = default action + `bin/focus-app`, right-click = slide-out dismiss, mouse swipe right = dismiss via `SwipeDismiss`) |
| `AudioPanel/AudioSection/DevicePicker/Slider`, `NetPanel`, `BtPanel`, `CalPanel`, `MediaPanel` | popups |
| `BtProfilePicker.qml` (in `AudioSection`, so Audio popup + control center) | Added 2026-10-19. Bluetooth profile / codec dropdown under the output `DevicePicker`, same look; visible only while the default sink has `api.bluez5.address`. See **Bluetooth audio profile** below |
| `TailscaleSection.qml` (in `NetPanel`, popup `net`) | Added 2026-10-18. Third section of the network popup under Wired / Wireless: `SectionHead` "Tailscale" with "N / M online" + a `Toggle` (`tailscale up` / `tailscale down`, no flags: plain `up` only sets WantRunning, so existing prefs are kept; busy pulse while the command runs or BackendState is `Starting`; 25 s timeout kills a hanging `up`). State line for `Stopped` (Disconnected), `Starting` (Connecting…), `NeedsLogin` (run `tailscale login`, switch disabled), `NeedsMachineAuth`, daemon down / unparsable output ("tailscaled is not running"), no peers. Tailnet name under the head. Peer list = ListView (cards like the Wi-Fi rows, ~5.5 rows then scrolls, Layout.fillHeight so it shrinks on short screens): self first (accent card, "this device"), then online before offline, by name. Row: OS glyph (FA Brands linux/apple/android/windows, else desktop), name, tags / "exit node available" or "Last seen N d ago" for offline, IPv4 (click = `wl-copy -- <ip>`, "Copied" for 1.2 s), green/grey dot. Click a row = expand: MagicDNS FQDN, Hostname (only if it differs), IPv4, IPv6 (both click-to-copy), OS, Owner (only for another user's device), Tags, exit-node state + "Use as exit node" / "Stop using exit node" (`tailscale set --exit-node=<ip>` / `--exit-node=`). **Name mapping:** title = first label of `DNSName` = the machine name from the admin console (renameable, the closest thing to an alias; e.g. HostName `CrossBattlestation` shows as `home`); `HostName` = what the OS reports. There is no other nickname field in `status --json`. Data: `tailscale status --json` on open and every 4 s while the popup is open (1 s while an action runs or Starting); nothing runs while closed (the section only exists inside the popup Loader). Model replaced only when the parsed list changes (no delegate rebuild every poll). Errors: stderr matching `access denied`/`operator` shows a red box with `sudo tailscale set --operator=$USER` + Copy button; other errors show their first line with URLs replaced by `<link>` (login URLs never displayed). **This machine has no operator set** (`tailscale debug prefs` has no `OperatorUser`; a no-op `tailscale set --accept-routes=false` returned "Access denied: checkprefs access denied"), so up/down/exit-node fail until the user runs `sudo tailscale set --operator=$USER` once. Test hook: `QS_TAILSCALE=<stub>` replaces the binary. Verified 2026-10-18 in an isolated instance with a stub (fixtures from the real JSON: running, extra tags/exit nodes, stopped, NeedsLogin, Starting, no peers, daemon down; up / down / exit-node calls and the access-denied path), screenshots of each; live shell hot-reloaded (qmldir line first, then NetPanel) and showed the real tailnet (9 machines, 5 online). Not verified: real `tailscale up/down` (deliberately not toggled: user may rely on it for remote access; would fail anyway without operator), real clicks/hover (no synthetic input), clipboard contents, a real exit node, the 25 s timeout. No confirm before `down` (unlike NetPanel's last-link guard). |
| `MullvadSection.qml`, `FoldSection.qml`, `VpnSwitch.qml` (singleton), `bin/vpn-switch` (NetPanel, popup `net`) | Added 2026-10-22. **Fold:** Tailscale and Mullvad sections are `FoldSection`s, folded by default (no UI-state persistence pattern exists; resets each popup open): head row = `SectionHead` + chevron + `Toggle`; a click anywhere on the head folds/unfolds, the Toggle's own MouseArea sits above it so on/off is one click without unfolding. Body height animates with `Theme.popupMoveMs` / `popupEaseOpen|Close`; fully open it is a normal fillHeight layout item (lists squeeze). Folded summary: Tailscale "N / M online" or short state; Mullvad "City, Country" / Connecting… / Disconnected / Blocked / Error / Not running / Not installed; "Switching…" while VpnSwitch runs. An action error unfolds the section. **Mullvad data:** `mullvad status -j` once, then `mullvad status -j listen` (one TunnelState JSON per change, nothing on start; an unparsable line triggers a `status -j`); 10 s poll only while the listener is not running (daemon down). `relay get` + `lockdown-mode get` + `auto-connect get` in one `sh -c`, `relay list` parsed ("Country (cc)" / tab "City (ccc)"). Never shows the disconnected-state geo location/IP, never runs `account get`. Shows: tunnel card (state, relay city/hostname, exit IPv4 click-to-copy), location row -> country list -> city buttons (`relay set location <cc> [city]`), lockdown + auto-connect switches in rows (deliberate exception to "switches only on headers": daemon settings, not connections). Daemon down = Notice with `sudo systemctl enable --now mullvad-daemon` + Copy. `QS_MULLVAD` replaces the binary. Parsing (both sections) lives in `launcher/js/Vpn.js`, shared with the launcher sections. **Switching (one place):** Tailscale on, Mullvad on and Mullvad off go through `VpnSwitch.run()` -> `bin/vpn-switch tailscale|mullvad|mullvad-off` (Tailscale off stays plain `tailscale down`). Exclusive mode (default): Tailscale on = refuse under Mullvad lockdown (exit 3, hint), `mullvad disconnect` + wait for disconnected (10 s), `tailscale up`; Mullvad on = `tailscale down` + wait for Stopped, `mullvad connect`; Mullvad off = disconnect + wait, then `tailscale up` (skipped with a hint under lockdown). First step failing/timing out = exit 2, nothing else run. Coexist mode = `systemctl is-active mullvad-tailscale.service` (or `VPN_SWITCH_MODE`): each switch only touches its own VPN. VPNs switched outside the UI are only reflected, never fought. Script tests: `<scratch>/test-switch.sh` pattern = fake `mullvad`/`tailscale` on `QS_MULLVAD`/`QS_TAILSCALE` with file state, 13 cases (both modes, lockdown, stuck disconnect, denied `tailscale down`, daemon down) all pass. **Coexist rules (installed by the user 2026-10-22, in use):** `system/etc/mullvad-tailscale/coexist.nft` (own table `inet mullvad-ts`: 100.64.0.0/10, fd7a:115c:a1e0::/48 out and `tailscale0` in get ct mark 0x00000f41 / meta mark 0x6d6f6c65; MagicDNS 100.100.100.100 is inside the /10; public Tailscale ranges, privacy-ok) + `system/etc/systemd/system/mullvad-tailscale.service` (oneshot, nft -f / delete table). Syntax and re-apply idempotence checked in `unshare -rn`. Assumed, not verified: Mullvad only rebuilds its own `inet mullvad` table on reconnect; marked packets skip Mullvad's routing rule and reach table 52. DNS: systemd-resolved enabled 2026-10-22 (`/etc/resolv.conf` -> stub-resolv.conf, NetworkManager picks it up automatically), so Tailscale and Mullvad set per-link DNS instead of overwriting resolv.conf. Verified live 2026-10-22: Mullvad connected (am.i.mullvad.net says connected) while a tailnet peer pings by MagicDNS name. Verified (live shell, crops in session scratchpad): both folded, Mullvad unfolded with the real daemon (disconnected), Mullvad mocked connected with picker + cities, Tailscale unfolded; no QML warnings. Not verified: any real click, real connect/disconnect/up/down or relay set, the `listen` line format on a real change, "not logged in" error text. |
| `TrayMenu.qml` | themed tray context menu (platform `QsMenuAnchor` needs QApplication mode, so we draw our own; position is read at click time) |
| `Launcher.qml` + `launcher/` | see "Launcher" below. The old `Menu.qml` picker and `bin/qmenu` (dmenu helper) were removed 2026-10-20; `twitch-qs-mpv menu` now falls back to fzf |
| `Osd.qml` | volume/brightness OSD |
| `HomeAssistant.qml` (singleton), `HaPanel.qml`, `HaEntity.qml`, `HaStrip.qml` | Home Assistant popup (house chip, popup name `ha`, bar widget id `homeassistant`): REST API via in-process XHR (token never in argv/logs), polls `/api/states` every 4 s while open, once a minute for the chip. Lights (toggle, brightness, hue/saturation strips, colour temperature by `supported_color_modes`), climate (power, target ±, HVAC modes), vacuum (start/pause/dock, suction, rooms + passes, see below), fans, covers, switches/input_booleans, scenes/scripts. Unavailable/unknown entities are left out (footer count) and come back on their own; entities hidden in HA and switches belonging to a shown light/climate/vacuum device are dropped (template `is_hidden_entity` / `device_id`). Eye button = hide/unhide mode, saved in `~/.local/state/quickshell/homeassistant.json`. |
| `AiUsage.qml` (singleton), `AiPanel.qml`, `AiLimit.qml`, `bin/ai-usage` | AI usage popup (sparkles chip, popup name `ai`, bar widget id `ai`; chip shows the SHORT-window (daily / 5h session) limit of ONE account (`AiUsage.currentWindow` → `currentPercent`; user request 2026-10-18, before it showed the account's fullest window, usually the weekly one): window length from `windowMinutes(w)` = key (`five_hour` 300, `seven_day*` 10080), else detail ("5-hour window", Codex "5h/7d window"), else title (Session 300 / Daily 1440 / Weekly 10080 / Monthly 43200); shortest known length wins, unknown lengths (e.g. a scoped "Limit") rank after all known ones, ties → earlier `resetsAt`; an account with only weekly windows shows its (earliest-resetting) weekly one. `worst` (polling speed) still uses the max over all windows. Verified with QS_AI_HELPER stub fixtures in an isolated instance (5h lower/higher than weekly, weekly-only, stale recent + fresh older, all stale (muted), Codex 5h+7d, unknown scoped) and live (chip 12% = 5h, weekly 28%). The account is `AiUsage.current`: the most recently used account (helper field `lastUsed` = mtime of its newest `projects/**/*.jsonl`, else of `.credentials.json`; Codex = newest session log) that has limit windows and is not `stale` (stale = status ≠ ok, e.g. lapsed/expired token, 429, offline, error, or values >15 min old). If none is fresh it shows the most recently used one with values, number in `Theme.muted`. Before this it showed the max over all accounts, so a stale work account's old % hid the active one. **Limit reached (2026-10-18):** `AiUsage.blockedUntil` = latest `resetsAt` (epoch s, still in the future) among the chip account's windows with `percent >= 100` (0 = not blocked; any exhausted window blocks the agent, so a weekly limit shows its countdown even while the 5h window is low); the chip then shows `AiUsage.until()` ("2h 14m", "47m", "3d 4h", same helper as the popup's "resets in") instead of the percentage, with `Layout.preferredWidth` at least the width of "23h 59m" and centered text so the minute countdown does not jitter. Depends on `AiUsage.now` (30 s tick), so it returns to the percentage by itself after the reset passes; an exhausted window with a missing/past `resetsAt` is ignored (percentage shown). Popup (`AiLimit`) already printed `resets in` from the same field, so it agrees. Verified with screenshots using a temporary `script` override pointing at a stub that printed a fixture JSON (live data edited: 5h 100 % in 2h 14m, 47m, both exhausted -> 3d 4h, weekly-only exhausted, reset in the past, normal); mock reverted. To re-test: temporarily point `script` in AiUsage.qml at a `python3 -I`-runnable stub (the live shell cannot take env vars) and change the file content (a pure `touch` does not reload) to re-run the helper. Chip number is otherwise plain `Theme.fg`; `levelColor` is defined but currently unused). `bin/ai-usage` (Python, run with `-I`) discovers every Claude Code profile as `~/.claude*` dirs (one per `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`; profile file = `<dir>/.claude.json`, for `~/.claude` it is `~/.claude.json`), label = email/display name, plan from `subscriptionType` + the profile's `userRateLimitTier` multiplier. Limits come from **`GET https://api.anthropic.com/api/oauth/usage`** (`Authorization: Bearer <claudeAiOauth.accessToken>`, `anthropic-beta: oauth-2025-04-20`): **unofficial and undocumented** (what Claude Code's `/usage` calls), may change or disappear; parsed defensively (`five_hour`, `seven_day`, `seven_day_opus/sonnet/…`, model-scoped entries of the `limits` array, `extra_usage`). Claude Code's own cached copy (`cachedUsageUtilization` in `.claude.json`) is adopted when newer, so often no request is made. Token only in the request header inside the helper (never argv/QML/cache/logs); it is **never refreshed** (would rotate the refresh token and can log the CLI out): lapsed access token = "start claude in this profile", 401/lapsed refresh token = "session expired", no retry until `.credentials.json` changes. Answers cached 60 s (`--force` 20 s) in `~/.cache/quickshell/ai-usage/`, 429 → Retry-After or 5 min backoff, offline → 2 min, last good values shown dimmed. Without windows (API key, no login) it shows local estimates from `projects/**/*.jsonl` (files touched in the last 8 days, read incrementally by byte offset). Also lists Codex CLI (rate limits from its newest session log), opencode, Gemini CLI when present. Runs on popup open, every 3 min while open (or while a limit ≥80 %), every 10 min for the chip, not at all while the chip is hidden. Tests: `QS_AI_HOME` (where to look), `QS_AI_CACHE`, `QS_AI_MOCK` (dir of `<profile dir>.json` = `{"status":200,"body":{…}}`), `QS_AI_HELPER`; `ai-usage --shape` prints response keys/numbers only. |
| Recording chip (in `Bar.qml`, widget id `recording`) | shown only while `screenrec.sh` records: pulsing `Theme.red` dot + elapsed time (+ speaker icon with audio), click = `screenrec.sh stop`. Each bar polls `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR/screenrec/state` once a second with a FileView `reload()` (a watch alone misses the file being created) and checks `/proc/<pid>/comm` against the stored `comm`, so a dead recorder hides the chip even if the state file is left behind. `SCREENREC_RUNTIME_DIR` overrides the dir for isolated tests. In the registry it is first in `right`, but existing saved layouts get it appended at the end of their right section (just before the bell); move it in Settings → Bar. The script is the API for recording itself (`screenrec.sh region|window|output|stop|status`); the mode menu is `RecordMenu.qml` (below). |
| ~~`RecordMenu.qml`~~ | removed 2026-10-20: recording is the launcher `record` section (see "Launcher: screenshot / record sections" below); `qs ipc call record menu\|close` stay as aliases, `screenrec.sh` unchanged |
| `bin/` | `focus-app`, `calendar-events` (read-only Thunderbird `cache.sqlite` helper), `ai-usage` |

### Shared building blocks (2026-10-18 refactor; use these, do not hand-roll)

| Component | What / when |
|---|---|
| `Icon` | Font Awesome glyph Label (iconFont, Regular). Any icon outside the bar; bar chips keep `BarIcon` |
| `PanelHeader` | title row of a popup (bold title, optional status, right-side children such as Btn/Toggle) |
| `ListRow` + `RowText` | list card (BT device, wired, Wi-Fi, Tailscale): leading icon, `RowText` title/sub (also tiles, HA cards), optional expandable `body`; trailing `RowState` |
| `Notice` | boxed confirmation / error (`danger`) / empty state / setup steps inside a popup |
| `LevelRow` | icon + name + `Slider` + value: volume, mic, brightness rows (control center, Audio popup) |
| `Meter` | thin value bar (Slider track, OSD, media seek, AI limits) |
| `Segmented` | choice button group (`values`, `labels`, `current`, `picked(v)`) |
| `SettingRow` | one Settings-page line: dim fixed-width title column + controls (also used in the record menu) |
| `ModalMenu` + `KeyBadge` | keybind-opened fullscreen modal card (used by `Launcher.qml`): header (`icon`, `iconFamily`, `title`), Esc/click-out, tiles with key badges; `focusItem` (e.g. a TextInput) gets keyboard focus on open instead of `onKeyPressed` |
| `PopupRegistration` | registers a non-PopupHost click-away thing (launcher, Settings, modals, tray menu) with `Popups`; `onDismissed` closes it |
| `Icons.volumeGlyph` | volume icon by level/mute (bar, OSD, tiles) |
| `HoverGlow` | (2026-10-21, revised on user feedback: larger, item-coloured, subtler) pointer-following hover light: a `Shape` rounded rect (`PathRectangle`, per-corner radii supported) filled with a `RadialGradient` centred on the pointer, no smoothing. Pointer tracking is a passive `HoverHandler` re-parented to the ITEM (`parent: glow.parent`), not the item's MouseArea: hover reaches an item's ancestors while a child control (Tailscale IP copy label, Wi-Fi reveal, a Btn in a row) has it, where `containsMouse` dropped (user report 2026-10-21); it takes no events and sets no cursor. `area` only gates it (`area.enabled` false = no glow). `glow.hovered` is also what every flat hover tint now uses (ListRow `hovered`, Tile, Btn, pickers, HaEntity, HaVacuum buttons / cells / room tiles, CalPanel, NotifCard), so tint and glow agree; a child Btn inside a glowing row shows its own subtle glow on top (kept: both are faint). Radius = max(3 x item height, 0.7 x item width) (`reach`, `widthReach`), stops 0 / 0.5 (40 %) / 1 (0). Colour = the parent's CURRENT fill made opaque (`fill`; transparent or no fill -> `Theme.fg`), so a gray row/tile/button glows gray, a selected row / "on" tile / active button / today cell glows accent, and it follows state changes; override with `color`. Peak alpha `strength` = 0.06 / sqrt(luminance of that colour), clamped 0.04..0.12 (white-ish fg ~0.06, red accent ~0.11), so gray and red look about equally strong. Fades with `Theme.popupFadeInMs`; invisible (not rendered) when not hovered; `z: -0.5` = over the item fill, under its content. Built into `ListRow` (network / wired / Wi-Fi / Tailscale / BT rows; a selected row now keeps its accent fill while hovered), `Tile` (control-center quick toggles), `Btn`; added to `DevicePicker` / `BtProfilePicker` (head + options), `HaEntity` (scene/script tiles), `HaVacuum` (action buttons, segmented cells), `CalPanel` day cells, `NotifCard` action buttons. Flat hover tints lowered (ListRow 0.12 -> 0.08, Tile 0.13 -> 0.10 / on 0.34 -> 0.30). Settings window (2026-10-21, user: most items lacked it): every interactive item has it: sidebar nav entries (`Settings.qml`), monitor cards, bar-layout chips (`WChip`, a passive handler so the Drag / DropArea proxy still works) in `SettingsBar.qml`, the Animations preview box, the Wallpaper page (monitor cards, tiles; its tabs / Play once-Loop / Follow primary are `Btn` / `Segmented`), plus the shared `Toggle` (glow on the track, radius `sw.r`, so also the section-head and per-category toggles); `Btn` / `Segmented` already had it. Not on: Sliders (user: no glow on sliders, handle or track), text links. Visual check: isolated nested instance with the glow forced on every item (a temporary `testPos` default in the copy, not committed); real hover and chip drag not tried in the live session. Test hook `testPos` (Qt.point). Unit test (qmltestrunner, offscreen, real HoverGlow.qml + a stub Theme singleton with fg / accent / popupFadeInMs / alpha()): `mouseMove` over a card and then over a child MouseArea: the card MouseArea's containsMouse drops, `glow.hovered` stays true and `glow.at` follows; disabled area = no glow; colour follows the fill. Run: `QT_QPA_PLATFORM=offscreen /usr/lib/qt6/bin/qmltestrunner -input tst_hoverglow.qml -import <dir>` (test files kept out of the repo). Verified in an isolated instance (real ListRow / Tile / Btn, glow forced at two positions on neutral and selected rows, idle and on tiles, normal and active buttons; comparison crop in the session scratchpad); real mouse tracking not tried. |
| `SwipeDismiss` | (2026-10-21) mouse swipe-to-dismiss for a card: `SwipeDismiss { onSwiped: ... }` + `transform: Translate { x: swipe.offset }` + `opacity: swipe.fade` (see **Notification swipe-to-dismiss**) |

Recipes. New popup panel: file with `PanelHeader` + sections (`SectionHead`), add the name to `PopupHost`'s content switch and width map, bar chip calls `bar.pop(name, item)`, then qmldir entry. New list row: `ListRow { icon; selected }` + `RowText { title; sub }` + `RowState` or a `Btn`. New Settings row: `SettingRow { title }` + `Slider` / `Toggle` / `Segmented` inside a page column. Anything click-away that is not a popup: `PopupRegistration` (modal cards: `ModalMenu`).

Visual check 2026-10-18 (isolated `quickshell -n -p` instances of main vs this branch, grim on DP-1, same state): bar, control center, net (with Tailscale), bt, audio, cal, media, ha, ai popups, Settings (Bar / Wallpaper / Appearance), screenshot and record menus, launcher: pixel-identical apart from live data (clock, reset timers). Not verified visually: toasts (the live shell owns the notification D-Bus name; code diff is only token / Icon swaps), tray menu, expanded rows and DevicePicker dropdown, popups opened from a bar chip (only the DELL on DP-3 has a bar; popups were opened via IPC on DP-1).

Home Assistant setup: create a long-lived access token in HA (profile → Security → Long-lived access tokens) and put `HA_URL=http://<host>:8123` and `HA_TOKEN=<token>` (optional `HA_URL_FALLBACK=`) into `config/hypr/.secrets`; the panel re-reads it each time it opens. No host is hard-coded: without both keys the popup shows the setup steps, an unresolvable/unreachable host shows "unreachable". Vacuum rooms: from the vacuum's attributes (`rooms`/`segments`, Dreame) or `roborock.get_maps` (Roborock); selected rooms are cleaned with `dreame_vacuum.vacuum_clean_segment {segments, repeats}` or `vacuum.send_command app_segment_clean [{segments, repeat}]`; a full clean's passes go through a same-device `number`/`select` "repeat / cleaning times" entity when one exists. Testing: `QS_HA_SECRETS` / `QS_HA_STATE_DIR` point an isolated instance at a mock server's files — never test service calls against the real HA.

Home Assistant "popup shows nothing" (2026-10-21): not a shell bug. API reachable (HTTP 200, ~1000 entities, token fine), but the climate and vacuum entities were `unavailable` in Home Assistant itself (device / integration offline; ~25 % of all entities were unavailable), one light was hidden by the user and the Switches section was turned off, so only one light was left and the rest showed only as "N unavailable". The footer now names them (`HaPanel.unavailableNames`, first 4: "Unavailable in Home Assistant: <names>. They come back here once Home Assistant reaches them again."). Diagnose without the UI: read-only `GET <HA_URL>/api/states` and `GET /api/config/config_entries/entry` (entry `state` != loaded) with the token passed to curl via `-K -` on stdin (never argv/logs); `~/.local/state/quickshell/homeassistant.json` holds the hidden entities / sections. Verified in an isolated instance (footer text logged, no QML warnings); the footer was not looked at on screen.

Gotchas: **never use endless shell loops (`while :; do ...; sleep`, `awk 'BEGIN{while(1)...}'`) in a `Process`: they orphan to systemd (ppid 1) when qs exits or restarts; poll with a `Timer` + `FileView.reload()` (works on /proc, see the CPU/memory chips in Bar.qml) or one-shot Processes.** Also: **no inline `component X: ...` in a file the running shell already loaded without one** (hot reload of Bar.qml failed with "Section is not a type"; a fresh `quickshell -n -p` start worked, so it only shows up on reload. A failed reload keeps the old generation running). New files with inline components (SettingsBar, HaVacuum) are fine. To test a change the way the live shell sees it, start an isolated instance on `git archive HEAD config/quickshell` and copy the new files over it. Isolated test env: `HOME=<scratch>/home` (with `.config/theme` and `.local/share/fonts` symlinked, a copy of `.local/state/quickshell/*.json`) plus `XDG_{CONFIG,STATE,CACHE,DATA}_HOME` pointing in there, so state files stay untouched; the test bar overlays the live one while it runs. `qmldir` must list every type; **never bind an item's own `visible` to its `visibleChildren`** (a hidden parent makes all children report `visible: false`, so it latches off forever if evaluated while they were hidden: `SectionHead`'s trailing slot did this and the live shell lost the Wi-Fi and Tailscale switches; fixed 2026-10-18: the slot stays visible and collapses to width 0 / no margin via `used`, gaps are left margins with `spacing: 0`, layout pixel-identical, checked on NetPanel and Settings → Bar); a QML property named like an id (`bar: bar`) shadows it — name properties differently; `Chip`'s hover MouseArea sits at `z: -1` so child MouseAreas (tray icons) get clicks; singletons aren't warmed in isolated test instances.

### Bar chip / icon alignment (2026-10-18)
- **Problem:** icon chips had uneven padding: icon-only chips were fixed 34 wide (9-10 px sideways vs 6 px up/down), glyph ink was off-centre by up to 1.5 px (hamburger/bell, ethernet, workspace kana), widths and the row `x` were fractional (blurry pill edges), icon box widths differed per chip (`iconSize + 4` on audio only), tray items were 18 px with a -4 margin highlight, the recording dot was 9 px.
- **Spec (tokens in `Theme.qml`):** `chipHeight` = barHeight - 2*barPad = 28, `chipPadX` 10, `chipGap` 6 (icon <-> label), `chipIcon` 16 (fixed icon box). `Chip.qml`: content <= one icon box wide gives a 28x28 square (6 px padding on all four sides); otherwise `ceil(content) + 2*padX`; content row at a whole-pixel x. Tray chip uses `padX: 6`, 16 px tray images, open-menu highlight margin -3.
- **`BarIcon.qml`** (registered in `qmldir`): fixed 16x16 box; the glyph's *rasterised* ink is centred in it. **`InkMeter.qml`** (Canvas, `opacity: 0`, `renderTarget: Canvas.Image`, `Immediate`) draws the string with the same font/size, scans the alpha and returns `ink {ok,l,r,t,b,adv}` (x from the text origin, y from the baseline; edges fractional: an edge column at 30 % coverage counts 0.3 px). Horizontal placement is exact and NOT snapped (Qt renders glyphs at fractional x cleanly, LCD AA); vertically the baseline (`y + baselineOffset`, which Qt does NOT round) is put on a whole pixel so horizontal strokes stay crisp, leaving <= 0.5 px vertical residual (near-ties within 0.1 px go up). `measure`/`vMeasure` = string whose ink is centred (audio uses `\uf028` so the speaker does not jump between levels; kana are each centred on their own ink now, the shared `"\u3042"` baseline was dropped). `inkLeft`/`inkRight` = blank px from the box edges to the ink. `BarLabel.qml` exposes the same two (side bearings + alignment slack; elided text assumes the monospace bar font). `Chip.qml` centres the row on the visible ink of its first/last visible item (`inkL`/`inkR`; fractional x allowed); icon-only chips and rows without ink-reporting items (tray bitmaps) stay on whole pixels. Do not read child `x`/`width` or the row size inside those bindings (re-polishes the RowLayout = binding loop); `chip.contentW` caches `row.implicitWidth`.
- **Minimum 1:1 rule (user requirement):** `implicitWidth = max(chipHeight, content + 2*padX)`, so no chip is ever narrower than its 28 px height; icon-only chips are squares, text chips (AI %, volume, clock, media, cpu/mem, tray with several icons) grow wider. Measured widths x height after the change (px): workspaces 28x28 (all), media 502x28, AI 66x28 (varies with %/countdown, always >= 28), home 28x28, network 28x28, bluetooth 28x28, volume 74x28, tray 72x28, clock 146x28, bell 28x28 (wider with a count). Before: 32-35 wide icon chips, tray/home chip 36.
- **Add a chip:** `Chip { BarIcon { text: ...; color: ... } BarLabel { ... } }`; never set `implicitWidth` on a chip (icon-only becomes square by itself), never use `BarLabel` with the icon font. Popup chips still call `bar.reg(name, chip)` / `bar.pop(name, chip)` unchanged (chip item geometry == visible pill).
- **How to measure (works):** temporary `console.log` timers in Chip/BarIcon dumping `mapToItem(null,0,0)` + size (log via `qs log`), temporary `(true || hover.containsMouse)` for forced hover, `grim -g "0,1400 2560x40"`, then per chip: background = per-row linear interpolation between columns 4 and w-5 inside the pill, ink = RGB distance, 4 px border band ignored (active border/radius), report (a) bbox margins at 8 % of max, (b) fractional-edge margins (column/row max coverage, 5 % floor), (c) intensity-weighted centroid. Judge on (b); (a) flips on faint LCD fringes. Scripts were in the session scratchpad (meas.py, glyphs.py, sheet.py), not kept; all debug edits reverted.
  - **Why the previous fix still looked off:** it centred `TextMetrics.tightBoundingRect`, which Qt rounds outward to whole px (hamburger reported 15 wide, draws 13.1), assumed the baseline at `round(baselineOffset)` (it is 13.125, drawn fractional), snapped x to whole px (a 13.1 px glyph in a 16 box is then 0.44 px off = ~0.9 px margin difference), and measured with a 60/765 threshold that ignored anti-aliased edges. Before (fractional L-R / T-B diff, px): center +1.09/+0.99, net +1.20/+1.01, bt +1.21/-0.98, home +0.93/+0.78, ws "ち" +0.75/+0.59, ws "い" -0.25/+1.33, AI -0.89/+1.02, volume +0.23/+1.00.
  - **After (normal and forced hover identical):** horizontal |L-R| <= 0.45 for every chip: center +0.03, home -0.03, bt 0.00, net +0.35, kana -0.42..+0.37, AI -0.37, volume +0.29, media +0.45, clock 0.00; vertical |T-B| 0.23..1.07 (center +0.76, net +0.76, home +0.82, bt +0.87, AI/volume/"い" ~-1.05 up): the whole-pixel-baseline residual, see caveats.
- **Caveats:** vertical centring is limited to +-0.5 px (margin difference up to ~1 px) because the baseline stays on a whole pixel; a fractional baseline was tested and centres to <= 0.46 but smears the hamburger bars over two rows. Most FA glyphs at `iconSize` 15 have fractional/odd ink heights in the even 16/28 boxes; `iconSize` 16 would likely give even heights (untested, design change). InkMeter measures an unhinted path (Canvas fillText): screen ink is ~0.13 px lower and ~1/3 px wider on each side (LCD filter), symmetric so harmless. Tray icons are bitmaps with their own padding (tray chip -0.61 px, not corrected). Display scales other than 1: see the next section.
- **Verified:** live on DP-3 (scale 1): `qs log` clean after the final reload (no binding loops), measurements above from grim crops (normal + forced hover, 10x crops viewed), `popup toggle net` card centre ~2171.5 vs chip centre 2172. Only code-read: record chip (dot + label + icon), bell with a count, DND glyph, media play/pause glyphs, elided media title math with a different font.

### Display scales (1 / 1.25 / 1.5 / 1.75 / 2 / 2.5 / 3, 2026-10-18)
- **Bug:** at any scale != 1 bar icons jumped by several px (above the text at 1.25-2, the bluetooth glyph out of the chip at 2.5, the hamburger over the clock at 3). **Root cause:** `InkMeter`'s Canvas is backed at the window's devicePixelRatio and `getImageData(0, 0, w, h)` returns raw DEVICE pixels from the top-left (verified: a rect at logical 4..10 read back at 8..19 at scale 2, at 5.. at 1.25), so the scan covered part of the glyph and reported ink in device px. **Fix:** `InkMeter.dpr` = `QsWindow.window.devicePixelRatio` (fractional, 1.25 etc.; `Screen.devicePixelRatio` is the integer ceil and wrong here), scan `ceil(width*dpr) x ceil(height*dpr)`, divide by dpr; repaints on dpr change. `BarIcon.snap()` puts the baseline on a whole DEVICE pixel (`ceil(v*dpr - 0.6)/dpr`; assumes the 16px box sits on the device grid, true for bar chips: 12 logical px from the edge). Scale 1 is mathematically unchanged (measured identical).
- **Measured after (icon chips, logical px, fractional ink edges):** |L-R| <= 0.16 at every scale (volume icon+label chip -0.5..-0.8, same as at scale 1: its `measure` is the widest speaker glyph); |T-B| 1.25: <= 0.07, 1.5: ~0.6 (half device px), 1.75: <= 0.37, 2: <= 0.43, 2.5/3: <= 0.19; scale 1 unchanged (~0.76). Before the fix the same chips were off by 3-7 px. Bottom and top edge identical.
- Popups, control center, Settings (3 pages), screenshot/record menus and the drun picker looked right at all scales (viewed, 1-device-px borders crisp at 1.25/1.75); no other scale bug found. No `QT_SCALE_FACTOR*` env is set; Qt 6.12 uses fractional-scale-v1 directly.
- **Scale-test recipe (works, nothing touches the live outputs):** nested `Hyprland -c /tmp/qss/hcfg/hyprland.lua` in a window (`XDG_RUNTIME_DIR=/tmp/qss/rt`, short path, `HOME=/tmp/qss/home`), then INSIDE it `hyprctl output create headless TEST` and a monitor rule `hl.monitor({ output = 'TEST', mode = '<1536*s>x<864*s>@60', scale = s })` + `hyprctl reload` (same logical 1536x864 at every scale, any size, no window resizing; the window output itself needs an explicit mode, `preferred` ignores the scale). `bars.json` in the test HOME must enable `"TEST"` (headless outputs have no model, key = name) or no bar shows. Isolated `quickshell -n -p` under `dbus-run-session`, popups/menus via `quickshell ipc -p <dir> call ...`, `grim -o TEST`. Always export the nested `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR`/`HYPRLAND_INSTANCE_SIGNATURE`/`WAYLAND_DISPLAY`; the user may run other nested Hyprlands. Measuring: temporary `console.log` of each chip's `mapToItem(null,0,0)` + size, then per chip median background, colour distance -> coverage, fractional edge margins. Scripts were in the session scratchpad (`hscale.sh`, `sweep2.sh`, `mloop.sh`, `meas.py`), not kept.
- **Live check (2026-10-18):** the Dell (DP-3, 2560x1440) was set to 1.6 and 2.0 via `monitors.lua` scale + position (`hyprctl keyword` is refused with the Lua config, `hyprctl eval hl.monitor(...)` did not apply) and reload; bar screenshots looked aligned, the user confirmed; reverted to 1.0. 1.5 is not a valid scale on 2560x1440 (non-integer logical size), Hyprland snaps it to 1.6; move the monitor so it still touches its neighbour (e.g. y = 1440 - 1440/scale).
- **Not verified:** a real monitor at 1.25/1.75/2.5/3, tray icons (no SNI items on the private bus), bell with a count, media chip with a player, toasts, floating bar (`barGap` not a multiple of 4 puts chips off the device grid at 1.25, so the device-pixel snap is then approximate), HiDPI hover/click.

### Popups centred on their chip
- **Change:** chip popups are horizontally centred on the chip that opened them; `x = clamp(Popups.center - card.width/2, side, host.width - card.width - side)` in `PopupHost.qml` (`side` = `Theme.edgeGap` floating, else 8; a binding, so it follows card width changes). No shrinking or flipping; vertical placement unchanged. Launcher, Settings, record/screenshot menus and tray menus are untouched.
- **How:** `Popups.right` was replaced by `Popups.center` (chip centre x in screen coordinates). `Bar.pop(name, item)` passes `Theme.barGap + item.mapToItem(null,0,0).x + item.width/2` (the bar window is inset by `barGap`; host windows are full-screen per monitor). Each bar chip registers itself with `bar.reg(name, chip)` on creation and each `Bar` registers in `Popups.barObjs[screen]`; `Popups.toggle(name, screen[, centerX])` looks the chip up via `Bar.chipCenter(name)` when no x is given (IPC `popup toggle`, `CenterPanel.open()` for its net/bt shortcuts). Fallback 1e6 = clamp to the right margin. Switching popups just sets a new `center`, so the card jumps to the new chip (no x animation).
- **Verified (2026-10-18, live shell, hot reload clean):** `qs ipc call popup toggle <name>` on DP-3 (the only monitor with a bar; focus moved with `hyprctl dispatch 'hl.dsp.focus({ monitor = "DP-3" })'`, plain `focusmonitor` is the old syntax and fails under the Lua config), grim screenshots: net card centre = chip centre (742 vs 742 px in the crop), media 800 vs 800, cal -> net switch lands on the net chip (1339 vs 1339), cal (chip at x~2443) clamped with its right edge ~8 px from the screen edge. Not verified: real chip clicks (no input injection; the click path only differs by passing the chip's own centre), left-edge clamp (no popup chip sits at the left end; same expression as the verified right clamp), a monitor with a bar at the top (DP-1 has no bar; it falls back to the right end).
- **Manual test:** click the network chip, then the clock chip: each card is centred under its chip; the clock card sits flush right.

### Toggles only on section headers; rows use RowState (2026-10-18)
- Switches (`Toggle`) exist only on main-section headers (BT "Power", Net "Wireless", Tailscale). Item rows (BT devices, Net wired rows, Wi-Fi network rows) use `RowState.qml`: fixed 22px right column, check (`\uf00c`, accent) when connected, pulsing ellipsis (`\uf141`) when busy, faint plug (`\uf1e6`) on hover when idle. This replaced the earlier 4px/8px shared-inset experiments (reverted) that never aligned row vs header switches.
- Click behaviour: BT/Wi-Fi rows unchanged (left = connect/disconnect, right = forget). Wired rows are now clickable (`net.setWired(dev, !on)`), only when `!!network || connected`; otherwise dimmed, no hover/hint.
- Verified: glyphs exist in the Sharp Regular font (fontTools cmap), qmllint reports no errors. NOT verified visually/live (opening popups would disturb the user's session); check each popup once after reload.

### Popup switching in one click (PopupHost input mask)
- **Bug:** with a bar popup open, clicking another chip only closed the popup; a second click was needed. **Cause:** `PopupHost.qml` is a full-screen Overlay-layer window (the click-away scrim), stacked above the bar, so the click hit the scrim (`Popups.close()`) and never reached the chip.
- **Fix:** `PopupHost.qml` now has an invisible `barStrip` item (`Theme.barReserve` tall, on the bar's edge) and `mask: Region { item: scrim; regions: [Region { item: barStrip; intersection: Intersection.Subtract }] }`. The strip is cut out of the window's input region, so clicks there reach the bar; the chip calls `Popups.toggle()`, which swaps popups (same chip still toggles closed). Clicks elsewhere still hit the scrim and dismiss.
- **Verified:** hot reload clean (`Configuration Loaded`, no errors); IPC `popup toggle net` then `toggle bt` leaves only Bluetooth open (grim screenshot shows the card, bar still visible). Real clicks could not be tested (no input injection): the click path is from code reading only.
- **Caveats:** tray-icon menus (`TrayMenu.qml`, `grabFocus: true` xdg popup) were not changed; a click on a chip while a tray menu is open may still be eaten by the compositor grab (untested). Keybind-launched overlays (launcher, Settings, record/screenshot menus) keep their full-screen (transparent, undimmed) click-away windows by design. The bar strip also no longer dismisses on clicks on empty bar space while a popup is open.
- **Manual test:** open e.g. the network popup, click the Bluetooth chip once: the Bluetooth popup should show immediately.
- **Second click on the same chip did nothing until the mouse moved (fixed 2026-10-20, pre-existing, not caused by the launcher work):** root cause: `PopupHost` mapped with `keyboardFocus: OnDemand`; Hyprland then sent `wl_pointer.leave` to the bar and the popup never received the pointer (no hover/press in either window, found with HoverHandler logging), and Hyprland re-picks pointer focus only on motion/axis events, so a click at rest was lost. Fix: `keyboardFocus` is `None` at map and `OnDemand` only after the `armed` timer (150 ms) in `PopupHost.qml`. Verified live (virtual-pointer client, no keystrokes): volume and AI chips, open + second click without motion closes, 4/4; before the fix it failed every time (scratch repro 4/4 on the volume chip). Not verified: click-away on the scrim after the fix, typing in the Wi-Fi password field (needs OnDemand after 150 ms), an interrupted/very fast second click (<150 ms). Dead ends: margin-inset window instead of the input hole, empty input region until armed (neither fixed it).

## Appearance (radius, gaps, floating bar)

Settings → Appearance changes the corner radius, window gaps, window border, docked/floating bar and smart gaps (globally and per monitor), in Hyprland AND Quickshell, live. Built 2026-10-18; per-monitor smart gaps added the same day.

**Data model.** User overrides live in `${XDG_STATE_HOME:-~/.local/state}/quickshell/appearance.json`, e.g. `{"version":1,"radius":12,"gap":10,"edgeGap":10,"border":2,"barFloating":true,"smartGaps":true,"smartMonitors":{"Dell Inc. DELL U2515H 9X2VY5AK1D5L":false}}`. A missing key means "theme default"; `{}` or no file means all defaults. Keys and clamps (applied by both the QML writer and the Lua reader): `radius` 0–32, `gap` 0–32, `edgeGap` 0–40 (optional; unset = `gap`), `border` 0–6, `barFloating` bool, `smartGaps` bool (the default for every monitor without its own entry), `smartMonitors` = per-monitor smart gaps `{"<monitor key>": bool}` (see **Per-monitor smart gaps** below; max 16 entries, keys 1–96 printable ASCII characters without `"` and `\`, values true/false only; any invalid entry voids the whole map, the other keys still apply; an empty map is "not set"). The biggest valid file is about 1.8 KB, so the 4 KiB cap stays. The page sliders cover less: radius 0–24, gap 0–32 in steps of 2, edge gap 0–40, border 0–4. Numbers are rounded. Non-numbers, NaN, a non-object, files over 4 KiB, directories and FIFOs all fall back to defaults. Precedence: Quickshell **appearance.json > `theme.toml [settings]` (via `out/palette.json`) > built-in fallbacks**; Hyprland **appearance.json > the hand-written config**, so the page's "(default)" labels show Quickshell's defaults (today both are 0/0/2/0). The fallbacks equal the look before this feature: radius 0, gap 0, border 2, docked, smart gaps on. Derived values: `gaps_in = round(gap/2)` (the visible gap between windows is about G) and `gaps_out = edgeGap ?? gap`. Env overrides for tests:
- `QS_APPEARANCE_FILE`: the state file path, QML side.
- `APPEARANCE_FILE`: the state file path, Lua side (`managed.lua`).
- `QS_HYPRCTL`: the hyprctl binary the shell calls.

**Who reads and writes.**
- **Only writer:** the `Appearance.qml` singleton. It applies a change in memory at once, then saves after a 400 ms debounce (FileView `setText`, `mkdir -p` first). 250 ms after each save it pokes Hyprland with `Quickshell.execDetached([hyprctl, "eval", "if layout_apply then layout_apply() end"])` (array form, no shell). If `smartGaps` or `smartMonitors` changed since the last poke, it runs `hyprctl reload config-only` instead (kept from the global toggle: `set_enabled` alone is not verified to re-evaluate window rules of open windows). When the set of screens changes while `smartMonitors` is set, it also runs the `eval` once (1.5 s later), as a safety net for hot-plug.
- **Hand edits:** the FileView watcher picks them up and pokes Hyprland the same way.
- **At start or hot reload:** the shell makes no hyprctl call, because Hyprland reads the same file itself.
- **theme.py** never touches the file, so overrides survive a wallpaper apply and its `hyprctl reload`.

**Hyprland side (high level).** Hyprland reads the same `appearance.json` at every config load and applies, on top of the hand-written values, `gaps_in`/`gaps_out`, `border_size` and `decoration.rounding`. It also switches the smart-gaps rules (a lone tiled window gets no gaps, border or rounding; per monitor) and keeps the pinned PiP box gaps_out away from the screen edges and the bar (see **Pinned video box and gaps** below). At runtime the shell pokes it after each save, see above. Everything there has to be `pcall`ed and clamped, because a Lua error at config load disables the rest of the config, keybinds included.

**Hyprland managed file (`hypr/hyprland/managed.lua`).** Everything Settings → Appearance does in Hyprland lives in this one file, so the feature never edits hand-written config and vice versa.
- **Loading:** one line in `hyprland.lua`, `pcall(require, "hyprland.managed")`, after `permissions` and before the scripts. `scripts/pip-reserve.lua` requires it again (cached; the module sets `package.loaded` first, so a half-way failure is not re-run and no rule is registered twice).
- **Overrides:** reads `appearance.json` (`M.read_small_file`: `r+` open so a FIFO can't block the compositor, 4 KiB cap; pattern parser, no JSON lib) and writes ONLY the keys that are set (`hl.config` per option, each `pcall`ed): `radius` → `decoration.rounding`, `gap` → `gaps_in = round(gap/2)` and `gaps_out` (unless `edgeGap`), `edgeGap` → `gaps_out`, `border` → `general.border_size`. A missing key leaves the hand-written value alone.
- **Baseline and reset:** at load, before applying, it reads the four options back with `hl.get_config` (gaps come back as `{top,right,bottom,left}` and are normalized to an integer when equal) into `M.baseline`. `layout_apply()` writes the baseline back for keys it overrode earlier that are no longer set (Reset writes `{}`). If `get_config` fails for a key, a reset leaves the current value; a reload restores it.
- **Smart gaps:** two layers of lone-window rules, all always registered and flipped with `set_enabled` (never re-registered at runtime, so no duplicates). The gaps rules only matter while gaps are non-zero. If managed.lua is missing or broken there are no smart rules (lone windows get border 2).
  - **Catch-all** (the rules from before, `M.smart_rules`): workspace rules `w[tv1]` and `f[1]` (gaps 0) and window rules `no-border-single-wtv1` / `no-border-single-f1` (border 0, rounding 0). Enabled while `smartGaps ~= false` AND no `smartMonitors` entry is `false` (`M.catch_all_on`). It covers monitors that have no rules of their own yet.
  - **Per monitor** (`M.smart_sets`, one set per connected monitor): workspace rules `m[desc:<description>]w[tv1]` and `m[desc:<description>]f[1]` (gaps 0) and window rules `no-border-single-wtv1-m<n>-<slug>` / `no-border-single-f1-m<n>-<slug>` matching the same selectors (border 0, rounding 0). Enabled per `M.smart_for(s, key)` = the monitor's entry, else `smartGaps`, else true. `f[1]` is scoped per monitor too. A set whose monitor is gone is switched off.
  - **Selector:** `m[desc:<description>]`, except when that would not select exactly this monitor: Hyprland's `desc:` is a prefix match that returns the first monitor found, so identical monitors (same description, e.g. no serial), a description that is a prefix of another connected one, or a description with `[`/`]` or edge spaces get the connector-name selector `m[DP-5]...` (computed at registration from the monitors connected then; the key stays the description). `window_rule match.workspace` accepting `m[...]w[tv1]` is inferred from the 0.56.2 headers (WorkspaceMatchEngine uses the same static workspace selector as workspace rules), not verified live.
  - **Hot-plug:** `hl.on("monitor.added" | "monitor.removed" | "hyprland.start")` → `M.on_monitors_changed()` = `M.apply_smart(M.read())`, which registers sets for new monitors and sets every rule's state; `layout_apply()` does the same. The `hyprland.start` hook matters if the config loads before the outputs exist at Hyprland start. Subscriptions of an earlier load are removed first (global `__managed_smart_subs`), in case a reload keeps the Lua state. At most 32 sets per config generation (`M.SMART_SETS_MAX`); a reload starts over.
- **Runtime API:** global `layout_apply()` (re-read + apply + `pip_bar_changed()`, never raises, returns ok, err); module `M.parse/read/targets/effective/get/edge_margins/apply/apply_options/apply_smart/smart_for/smart_enabled/catch_all_on/monitor_key/monitor_selector/valid_key/on_monitors_changed/set_smart/capture_baseline/state_path/read_small_file`, `M.smart_rules`, `M.smart_sets`, `M.BAR_HEIGHT` 40, `M.BAR_GAP_DEFAULT` 0, `M.MAX_BYTES` 4096, `M.SMART_KEY_MAX` 96, `M.SMART_MAP_MAX` 16, `M.SMART_SETS_MAX` 32, `M.EDGE_MARGIN_MAX` 200. `M.parse` cuts nested objects out before reading the top-level keys, so a monitor named like a key (`"radius"`) inside the map never counts as one. pip-reserve uses `managed.get().barReserve` (bar height + floating gap; fallback 40) for its bottom offset and `managed.edge_margins()` for the gaps and `managed.smart_enabled(mon)` (is smart gaps in force for that monitor; re-reads the file, never raises, false on failure) for the single-window case, and refits when the resulting box changes.
- **Untouched:** `settings.lua` and `theme.lua` are the user's hand-written values (identical to before); `workspaces.lua` / `rules.lua` only lost the moved rules; `theme.py`'s `palette.lua` output is back to wallpaper + rounding + colours.
- **Reload pitfalls:** Hyprland auto-reloads the Lua config when a file is saved, so every file must load on its own. managed.lua must stay after the hand-written modules (its baseline is whatever they set). Runtime `hl.config` changes are lost on reload, but managed.lua re-applies the file at every load. Assumed, not verified live: a reload resets options to their defaults before re-running the config (either way the hand-written files set all four options explicitly).

**Pinned video box and gaps** (`scripts/pip-reserve.lua`, battlestation; added 2026-10-18).
- **Rule:** the pinned PiP/mpv/Twitch box keeps the same distance as tiled windows (margin `m` below; but see **Smart gaps** further down: `m` is 0 while "No gaps for a single window" applies on the monitor): gaps_out from the right screen edge and from the bar (bottom bar: from the bar reserve, i.e. `barReserve` = 40 docked / 40 + gapOut floating; top bar or no bar: from the bottom screen edge). The reserved right strip stays `BOX.w` = 1588 wide; the box's left edge is the strip boundary, where the tiled layout adds its own gaps_out, so tiled windows end gaps_out before it too. The video shrinks by the margin and stays 16:9: `w = 1588 - m - 2*b`, `h = floor(893*w/1588 + 0.5)`, `x = mon.x + mon.width - m - b - w`, `y = mon.y + mon.height - bottomOffset - m - b - h`. Gap 0 gives exactly the old 1588x893 at the old place.
- **Margin `m`:** `managed.edge_margins()` = effective gaps_out per side: the override (edgeGap, else gap), else the hand-written gaps_out read at load (`M.baseline`, integer or per-side table), else 0; clamped 0..200. Without managed.lua, or if it raises or returns garbage, all margins are 0 (old behaviour).
- **Border `b`:** Hyprland draws borders OUTSIDE a window's `at`/`size` for tiled and floating windows alike (a tiled window at gaps_out 10 / border 4 reports `at` 14, checked read-only with `hyprctl clients -j`), so the visible edge is `at - border`. Pinned windows have border 0 from the `pinned` rule in `rules.lua` (`hyprctl getprop <pinned window> border_size` = 0), so `PIN_BORDER = 0` in pip-reserve; change both together.
- **Who places it:** the static `rules.lua` rules (cb-pip, cb-mpv, twitch-player: `move (monitor_w-1588) (monitor_h-933)`, size 1588 893) are unchanged and still assume a docked bottom bar without gaps; pip-reserve corrects the box on `window.open` (PiP, mpv), on the first `window.title` of a pinned video window (the Twitch player has no title at open; only when margins are non-zero, so gap 0 behaves exactly as before), on heal, and in `refit()` whenever the box (bar offset, margins, size) changes. `layout_apply()` → `pip_bar_changed()` → refit, so a gap change in Settings moves open pinned windows at once. A window placed once is not snapped back by later title changes.
- **Smart gaps ("No gaps for a single window"):** the effective margin `m` is 0 on both sides (right edge and bar) while Hyprland gives the lone tiled window beside the box no gaps. That is the case when (a) `managed.smart_enabled(mon)` is true for the video's monitor (the monitor's rule set: its `smartMonitors` entry, else `smartGaps`, else on; a monitor without a rule set follows the catch-all rules, `catch_all_on`; any failure = false) and (b) the monitor's active workspace has exactly one visible tiled window, the same thing the `w[tv1]` rule counts: floating and pinned windows (the video itself included), hidden and unmapped windows do not count, other monitors and workspaces neither; or it holds a maximized window (`fullscreen == 1`, the `f[1]` rule; real fullscreen is `f[0]`, does not match, keeps the gaps). Otherwise `m` = `gapsOut` as before. With `m` 0 the box is the old flush 1588x893 box. **Rounding:** on the battlestation the pinned windows had NO rounding rule (only the workstation's cw-pip/twitch rules have `rounding = 0`), so they got the normal `decoration.rounding` (12 with the radius override; `hyprctl getprop address:<a> rounding` = 12 measured). While `smart_gaps_apply` holds, `pip-reserve.lua` enables three window rules `pip-smart-rounding-1..3` (`rounding = 0`, matching `pin = true` + the PiP title / mpv initial_class / org.quickshell class), registered once disabled and flipped with `set_enabled` in `sync()` (`sync_rounding`); otherwise rounding stays the normal value. Verified live after `hyprctl reload`: smart on (one tiled window on workspace 7) -> getprop rounding 0 for both pinned windows, configerrors empty. Not verified: the off state (second tiled window) and that a live `set_enabled` flip re-evaluates already-open windows (assumed; if not, rounding only updates on the next window rule evaluation). Workstation unchanged (its rules already force 0). The reserved strip stays 1588 wide. `pip-reserve.lua` does the counting (`smart_gaps_apply`) and refits on `window.open/close/kill/destroy/move_to_workspace/pin/fullscreen/update_rules`, `workspace.active/move_to_monitor/removed` and `layout_apply()` (setting changes); the refit only moves the box when its computed place changes, so moving a floating window or repeated events never loop. A closing window that is still listed during its own close event is left out of the count. Unknown event names are skipped (`pcall`). **Hyprland Lua API objects are userdata** (monitors, workspaces, windows: `type(x)` is `"userdata"`; probed live with a read-only `hyprctl eval` writing to a scratch file): test them with nil checks (`ws == nil`, `w.workspace ~= nil`), never `type(x) == "table"`; their fields are plain values (`floating/pinned/mapped/hidden` booleans, `fullscreen` a number, 0 for normal windows, `workspace.id` a number, `address` a string, `at/size` tables; `hl.get_windows()/get_monitors()` return real tables), and stub tests must model that (the harness hands proxies and overrides `type`). Still only confirmable live: `fullscreen == 1` for a maximized window (assumed from `hyprctl clients -j`) and that `window.fullscreen` / `window.update_rules` fire on fullscreen and float toggles.
- **No stacking:** every pinned video window goes into the same single box (they overlap); there is no multi-box layout.
- **Hooks:** pip-reserve's `hl.on` subscriptions are kept in the global `__pip_reserve_subs` and removed at the next load, so a reload that keeps the Lua state does not double them.
- **Not changed:** `pip-orientation.lua` (workstation only, its own fixed geometry), the Steam OSK rule (pinned, flush bottom center on purpose), the Twitch popout chat rule (not pinned), `switch-workspace.lua` (restores a window's own previous geometry).

**Per-monitor smart gaps.**
- **Monitor key:** the monitor description exactly as Hyprland reports it (`hyprctl monitors -j` `.description` = Lua `hl.get_monitors()[i].description` = Quickshell `Hyprland.monitorFor(screen).description`; e.g. `Microstep MPG271QX OLED 0x01010101`, `Dell Inc. DELL U2515H 9X2VY5AK1D5L`, checked read-only 2026-10-18). It is make + model + serial, so it survives reboots and port changes (connector names DP-1/DP-3 do not). Fallback when the description is not a valid key (non-ASCII, `"`, `\`, empty, > 96): the connector name (not stable; both sides use the same rule, `Appearance.monitorKey()` / `M.monitor_key()`). Not `model|serial` as in `bars.json`: Lua monitors have no `model` field, and the description is what the `desc:` selector needs.
- **Edge cases:** identical monitors without a serial share one key (one setting for both; the rules use connector selectors so both are matched). A monitor that is not connected keeps its entry (and while it is `false`, the catch-all stays off; the connected monitors have their own sets). Entries are never pruned automatically; Reset or the all-monitors switch clears them.
- **Limitations:** a monitor connected later is covered by the catch-all until its set exists; if neither the `monitor.added` event nor a later `layout_apply()`/reload registers it, and an explicit `false` exists, it has no smart rules (gaps/border like a normal window). The QML parser decodes JSON escapes (`\u0041` → `A`) while managed.lua rejects any backslash in the map, so a hand-written escaped key is ignored by Hyprland only; Quickshell never writes escapes (valid keys need none).

**Theme.qml API** (effective value = override, else palette default):
- **Mirrors:** `radius` (R, for outer elements), `rounding` (old alias of `radius`), `gap`, `gapIn`, `gapOut`, `borderSize`, `barFloating`, `smartGaps`.
- **Bar:** `barGap` (= floating ? gapOut : 0), `barRadius` (= floating ? R : 0), `barPad` 6, `barReserve` (= barHeight + barGap).
- **Popups:** `popupGap` (floating: max(4, gapOut); docked: 8) and `edgeGap` (floating: gapOut; docked: 12).
- **Concentric rule:** `inner(outer, inset) = outer <= 0 ? 0 : max(min(outer, 4), outer - inset)`. It returns exactly 0 at R = 0, and never goes below 4 px (or R, if R is smaller) otherwise.
- **Named radii:** `radiusChip = inner(R, 6)` (bar chips), `radiusItem = inner(R, 12)` (items in a popup card), `radiusPage = inner(R, 18)` (Settings page boxes), `radiusDeep = inner(radiusItem, 8)`, `radiusModal = inner(R, 20)` (formerly RecordMenu rows, now unused).
- **Pills stay pills:** toggles, slider tracks, dots and progress bars keep `min(h/2, …)` shapes; only where R comes from changed.
- **Slider handle:** `Slider.qml` thumb is always square (16x16, width == height), radius `min(8, Theme.radius)`: square at R=0, full circle once R >= 8; it travels `x = value * (width - 16)`, row height unchanged (24).
- **Slider glass handle (2026-10-21, user request; reworked twice the same day: v1 compositor glass "does not refract", v2 in-app pill "too wobbly, too large, should stay a circle / squircle, follow our animations and glass settings, work without the custom Hyprland"):** while pressed / dragging (not hover, not wheel) the 16 px handle grows into a 28 px (1.75x) in-app glass lens that refracts what the shell drew under it (track, fill, card, labels). Always 1:1: corner radius goes from the handle's `min(8, Theme.radius)` to fully round, corners circular when `Theme.radius >= 8` (round handle) else a superellipse power 4 (squircle). Size picked from nested sheets: 28 px still shows the fill bulging through the lens and the rim, 62 px (v2) dwarfed the row. No stretch, no custom spring. Hit area unchanged; value mapping follows the handle travel (see Pointer centring).
- **Slider wheel (2026-10-22, user: wallpaper and colours changed while scrolling the Wallpaper page):** `Slider.onWheel` always changed the value, so scrolling the page over a timing slider set wind 3 -> 0 and ran a full `theme.py apply` (play-once clip frozen on frame 0, palette from the t100 still). `Slider.wheel` (default true) now lets the wheel pass through (`accepted = false`) when false; every Slider on the scrolling Settings pages (Glass, Appearance, Animations, Wallpaper) sets `wheel: false`. Popups (LevelRow, HaEntity) keep wheel control. Not verified with a real scroll.
  - **Pointer centring (user, after a real drag: "the pointer must always be in the centre of the handle"):** pointer x -> value uses the handle's own travel, `valueAt(px) = (px - 8) / (width - 16)` clamped (handle x = v * (width - 16), centre at v * (width - 16) + 8), so press (no grab offset, also on the handle's edge) and drag keep the handle / lens centre exactly on the pointer; clamped only in the 8 px end zones. The lens centre is the handle centre, never clamped. The Meter fill ends at the handle centre too (fill fraction (v * (width - 16) + 8) / width, 0 at v = 0). Release: `held` (not `drag.pressed`) switches `shown` from the pointer to `value`, after `settling` is set, so a stepped / snapping caller's rounded value (gap steps, speed 1x snap) is reached by a glide (handle `Behavior on x` + Meter fill, `Theme.popupCloseMs` / `popupEaseClose`) instead of a jump; unstepped sliders do not move. Wheel unchanged.
