Verified with the rig hook pressing at local x 0, 4, 8, 9.5, 50, 201 / 207 / 213 (the handle's edges at v 0.5), 300, 402, 406, 410: handle and lens centre = press x to 0.00 px inside [8, width - 8], clamped to the ends outside; screenshots with a marker at the press x; release glide from v 0.74 to 0.5 at popup speed 0.25.
  - **Motion:** `grow` 0..1 is a NumberAnimation on the popup tokens, exactly like PopupHost open / close: press = `Theme.popupOpenMs` x remaining share, `Theme.popupEaseOpen`; release = `Theme.popupCloseMs` x share, `Theme.popupEaseClose`; style none = instant. Default cubic: no overshoot; Back overshoots on press only (as popups). Settings > Animations has no spring preset for popups (spring is Hyprland-only), so none here.
  - **How:** on press `findHost()` = nearest `isGlassItem` ancestor (popup / Settings card), else the item under the window root. A `Loader` (active only while `down || grow > 0.004`) re-parents a `ShaderEffect` to the host's PARENT (z 1000), sized to the card + 24 px, fed by a child `ShaderEffectSource { sourceItem: host; hideSource; sourceRect: card + 24 px }`: the card is hidden and redrawn in full by the effect (pass-through outside the lens), so un-refracted content never shows through and the lens is not in its own capture. Shader `shaders/sliderlens.frag` + compiled `sliderlens.frag.qsb` (both committed). Build: `/usr/lib/qt6/bin/qsb --qt6 -o sliderlens.frag.qsb sliderlens.frag` in `config/quickshell/shaders/`. Qt caches a ShaderEffect shader per URL for the life of the process: after changing the .qsb, restart qs (a reload keeps the old shader, and with a changed uniform block the lens renders wrong until then).
  - **Glass settings mapping** (the optics are a port of the fork's `solidglassfinish.frag`, ~/code/hyprland-blur-test): values = `GlassTuneCtl.values` (live `getoption` of decoration:blur:solid_glass:*, so a Settings > Glass drag changes the lens at once) when `Theme.glass && GlassTuneCtl.ready`, else `GlassTuneCtl.defaults()` (stock Hyprland, other variants, not read yet). `refraction`, `bulb`, `depth`, `edge_curve` -> rim bend (same convex profile; bead bend = refraction x depth x min(bulb, half size) / bulb, capped at 0.75 x that width: the compositor's sqrt share and 1.5x cap pull the track to the rim of a 28 px lens) and centre magnification 1 + 0.35 x depth; `aberration` -> chromatic split on the bend; `frost` (+ 3 px x (1 - `clarity`)) -> 13-tap hex blur of what is seen through; `light_angle`, `specular_intensity`, `specular_power`, `sheen` -> rim / counter rim / sheen / inner shadow with the compositor's formulas (nothing with intensity 0). Not applied: `tint`, `darkening`, `saturation`: backdrop operations the card's own compositor glass already applies under the lens; the shell content sits on top of that glass untinted, and a dark tint (the battlestation has 0xFA000000, absorption ~3.9) would black out the slider. Not a glass setting: a soft drop shadow (0.18 light / 0.32 dark palette). Shell pixels are premultiplied sRGB, not linear light.
  - **With the battlestation's current glass.json (specular 0, sheen 0, frost 0) the lens has no rim**: only the bulge / bend of the track, the aberration and the shadow (faithful to the popups, which also have no rim). With the Glass page defaults it is a lit bead.
  - **Fallbacks:** no lens with the software scene graph (`GraphicsInfo.api === Software`, e.g. `QT_QUICK_BACKEND=software`) or a shader that fails to load (`ShaderEffect.status === Error` -> `lensBroken`, one console warning; `hideSource` is off on Error, so the card stays drawn): the handle itself scales up to the lens size with the same animation. Never gated on `Theme.glass`.
  - **Crash found and fixed (v2):** the `ShaderEffectSource` must be a visual child of the effect (zero size), not a parentless `property var source: ShaderEffectSource {}`: hiding the window while the lens is up segfaulted in `QQuickWindowPrivate::dirtyItem` (reproduced for the test window, Settings, popup; none with the child source over repeated runs).
  - **Compositor glass for the lens: none** (the v1 `isGlassOverlay` / `Glass.qml findOverlays()` are gone). Nested A/B: no visible gain at this size over a glass card; the wallpaper behind a translucent card is seen through the lens unrefracted.
  - **Cost:** nothing at rest (no item, no capture). While pressed / animating: one offscreen render of the card (Settings ~920x600, popup ~420x480, + margins) and one full-card shader pass per frame.
  - **Caveats:** two sliders animating at once on one card stack two full-card effects (the releasing lens vanishes early); the card's own popup transforms are not copied onto the effect.
  - **Verified (nested, isolated HOME/XDG, rig-only IPC hook forcing press / value / grow, grim on the nested socket):** fork `/usr/local/bin/Hyprland` with the battlestation's glass values and with the page defaults (`glasstune reset all`); stock `/usr/bin/Hyprland` 0.56.2 (Theme.glass false, GlassTuneCtl "no solid_glass", lens with defaults); dark and light test palettes; Settings > Appearance sliders and the control-center LevelRow (rest, mid, settled, at 0 and 1), a test card with text under the track, a slider ending at the card edge and one on an opaque panel; real grow animation at popup speed 0.25 with Cubic (monotonic) and Back (overshoot on press, plain close); software backend and a missing .qsb (handle grows, card intact). Live: "Configuration Loaded", no warnings. **Not verified:** a real pointer drag, the real wallpaper, fractional scales.
- **Masks:** `clip: true` clips to the bounding box, not the rounded shape. Media art and wallpaper thumbnails therefore use a MultiEffect mask when the radius is > 0. The HaStrip/HaVacuum segment ends use per-corner radii (Qt 6.12).
- **Palette read:** the first palette read is synchronous (`blockLoading`), so the first frame already has the right shape.

**`Btn`.** Two properties set the corner: `outerRadius` (default `Theme.radius`) and `inset` (default 12). The radius is `min(Theme.inner(outerRadius, inset), floor(height/3))`, so a small button never turns into a pill at a large R. A caller that is not directly inside a popup card sets both:
- Btn `outerRadius: Theme.radiusModal; inset: 8` was RecordMenu's segment style (menu removed 2026-10-20).
- Settings close button and page-level buttons on the Bar/Appearance pages: `inset: 18`.
- Monitor-card Top/Bottom: `outerRadius: Theme.radiusPage; inset: 9`.
- Chip eye buttons (`Mb`): `outerRadius: inner(radiusPage, 8); inset: 6`.
- Nested buttons in NetPanel, HaPanel, HaEntity and HaVacuum.

NotifCard action buttons follow the same rule. The NotifCard and CalPanel accent stripes run from `y = radius` to `height - radius`, so they stay on the straight edge.

**Docked vs floating geometry.**
- **Docked:** margins 0, radius 0, reserve = barHeight. This is today's look and pixel-identical to the committed shell.
- **Floating:** the bar sits `gapOut` from the screen edge and from both sides, with radius R. The reserve (`BarReserve` exclusiveZone) is barHeight + gapOut, so the gap between bar and windows is gapOut.
- **Popups:**
  - The card sits `barReserve + popupGap` from the bar edge, and `edgeGap` from the side when floating (docked: 8).
  - Placement is chip-centred (2026-10-18, see "Popups centred on their chip" below); `Popups.right` no longer exists.
  - `qs ipc call popup toggle <name>` looks the chip up by name (`Popups.chipCenter`); a hidden/unknown chip or a monitor without a bar falls back to the right end.
- **Toasts, OSD and tray menu:**
  - Toasts: top = `edgeGap + barReserve` with a top bar.
  - OSD: bottom = `70 + barReserve` with a bottom bar.
  - TrayMenu: `popupGap` away from a floating bar, flush when docked.

**Appearance page** (`SettingsAppearance.qml`; `qs ipc call settings page appearance`):
- **Controls:** sliders with numeric readouts for corner radius, window border, window gap and edge gap. A "Separate edge gap" toggle reveals the edge-gap slider: on = `edgeGap` set to the current gapOut, off = key removed. Docked | Floating buttons. "No gaps for a single window" under Gaps:
  - **Main switch** ("a lone tiled window fills the screen · all monitors"): `Appearance.setSmartAll(v)` = sets `smartGaps` (also the default for monitors connected later) and removes `smartMonitors`, so every monitor follows it. "(default)" while `smartGaps` is not set.
  - **One row per monitor, only with 2+ screens** (`Quickshell.screens`): toggle aligned under the main one, monitor icon, model (like the Bar page cards), connector name muted, "(default)" while that monitor has no entry (it follows the main switch). Writes `Appearance.setSmartFor(key, v)` (an explicit entry, even when equal to the default). A screen without a usable key shows a dimmed, disabled toggle.
  - **One screen:** only the main switch, as before; it shows that monitor's effective value (a leftover entry of its own wins in Hyprland) and clears the entry when used.
  - **QML API** (`Appearance`): `smartMonitors` (raw), `monitorKey(screen)`, `smartOverridden(key)`, `smartFor(key, fallback)` (fallback = `Theme.smartGaps`), `setSmartFor(key, v)`, `unsetSmartFor(key)`, `setSmartAll(v)`, `validKey(k)`, `cleanMap(v)`.
- **Previews:** a real-size nested sample (card R, then item, then chip, with the values printed) and a scaled mini desktop.
- **Labels and reset:** values that come from the theme get a muted "(default)" label. "Reset to theme defaults" writes `{}` (also clears `smartMonitors`); it is a no-op when nothing is overridden.
- **Drag guard:** the page holds `dragging` for 300 ms after a slider move, so the Settings Flickable doesn't steal the drag.
- **Sidebar:** the Settings sidebar's left corners follow the card radius.

**Pitfalls and caveats.**
- **Deploy order** is theme files (`palette.{json,lua}` with the new keys) → Hyprland files → Quickshell files. Both Hyprland and Quickshell reload on save, so every intermediate state must load.
- **Name clash:** `Theme.edgeGap` is the popup/toast distance from the screen edge. It is **not** the user's `edgeGap` key, which shows up as `Theme.gapOut`.
- **Mini preview:** it always draws the bar on top, is scaled, and is not pixel-exact.
- **Not verified:**
  - real pointer drags on the sliders;
  - the NetPanel confirm/password form and the HaVacuum Segmented control at R > 0;
  - single-monitor setups (the Appearance page's one-screen layout was only checked with a test hook forcing it on two monitors);
  - per-monitor smart gaps in the live compositor: `m[desc:...]` selectors in `window_rule` `match.workspace`, `hl.on("monitor.added")` firing and rule registration from inside it, rules registered after config load, and whether `set_enabled` alone re-evaluates open windows (hence the `reload config-only`);
- **No config check from the CLI:** `Hyprland --verify-config -c <file>` dumps core even on the committed Lua config (0.56.2), so it cannot check a config.

**Test recipes.**
- **Isolated Quickshell:**
  1. Copy `config/quickshell` to a short dir.
  2. Create a test HOME with `.config/theme` holding symlinks to the live entries, except `out/palette.{json,lua}`, which are copies carrying the new keys. Add `.local/share/fonts` as a symlink, plus a `bars.json` fixture.
  3. Make a short `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR` with `hypr` and `wayland-1` symlinks into `/run/user/1000`.
  4. Run `dbus-run-session --config-file=<conf>` with a session config that has **no `<servicedir>`**. Otherwise portals, gvfs and a11y get activated.
  5. Set `QS_APPEARANCE_FILE=<fixture>` and `QS_HYPRCTL=<stub that appends "$*" to a log>`, then run `quickshell -n -p <dir>`.
  6. Drive it with `quickshell ipc --pid <pid> call …`: `popup toggle`, `settings page appearance`, `record menu`. Test-only IpcHandlers go in the copy only; the 2026-10-18 harness added `pg.slide()`, `Appearance.set()` and chip pops.
  7. Take screenshots with `grim`. Kill the instance by exact PID, then kill orphaned `sh -c 'while :; …'` pollers whose environ has your test HOME and whose ppid is 1.
- **Expectations:**
  - no hyprctl call at start;
  - one stub call per burst of changes;
  - `reload config-only` exactly when smartGaps or smartMonitors changes (hand edits that only touch other keys, or a map that is invalid and therefore ignored, give `eval`);
  - `{}` looks identical to the committed shell.
- **Hot-reload rehearsal:** start on `git archive HEAD config/quickshell`, then `rsync` the new files over it. This gave one "Reloading configuration… / Configuration Loaded", the new singleton active, and no restart needed.
- **Lua:** run `luac5.5 -p` on every file. Hyprland-side stub harness `managedtest.lua` (not in the repo: it lives in the 2026-10-18 appearance staging cache under `~/.cache/`; copy it into the repo, e.g. under `tests/`, if you want to keep it): `lua5.5 managedtest.lua <committed hypr dir> <new hypr dir> <scratch dir> <committed palette.lua> <new palette.lua>`, run from inside the scratch dir. It loads both whole trees through `hyprland.lua` against a recording `hl` stub (os.execute/io.popen stubbed, HOME/XDG_STATE_HOME in the scratch dir), writes `log-committed.txt` / `log-staged.txt`, and checks equivalence, overrides at load and via `layout_apply()`, reset to the baseline, hostile state files, a missing/raising module, reload and pip-reserve offsets. Result 2026-10-18: 446 passed, 0 failed on lua5.5 and lua5.4. Per-monitor version (the same harness extended for the per-monitor rules, same arguments, reference tree = the live c121a14 config): the stub adds `hl.get_monitors` (monitors modelled on the real DP-1 MSI / DP-3 Dell) and `hl.on` with removable subscriptions and a `fire(event)` helper; equivalence = an LCS diff of the call logs, which must show 0 removed calls and exactly 12 added (1 `get_monitors`, 4 rules × 2 monitors, 3 `hl.on`) right after the catch-all rules. Further checks: map parsing (limits, escapes, nesting, duplicates, keys named like top-level keys), override/default combinations, stale entries, runtime toggling, hot-plug add/remove/re-add, config loaded before monitors exist, identical/prefix monitors, connector reuse, `get_monitors`/`hl.on` raising, the 32-set cap, kept-VM reload (old subscriptions removed). Result 2026-10-18: 760 passed, 0 failed on lua5.5 and lua5.4. After the pinned-box gaps change the harness copy expects one more added call (`hl.on("window.title")`), 11 removed subscriptions on a kept-VM reload and gap-aware PiP y values: 761 passed, 0 failed (reference = the c121a14 tree via `git archive`; against a newer reference the three "12 added calls" checks fail by design). Companion harness `piptest.lua` (kept next to it, same arguments, reference = the live tree): fires window events (`window.open/pin/title/close`, `workspace.active`, heal, `layout_apply()`, bar top/bottom) through every script's handlers with a stub that applies move/resize/pin; checks gap-0 equivalence of all side-effect calls and geometries against the reference for 21 state/bars combinations, the geometry for gap/edgeGap/floating/top/no-bar/baseline cases, live re-fit, hostile or missing managed module, and kept-VM reload: 440 passed, 0 failed on lua5.5 and lua5.4.
- **Theme:** `python3 -I -m py_compile theme.py` and `python3 -I selftest.py`.

## Bluetooth audio profile (`BtProfilePicker.qml`, 2026-10-19)

- **Why:** the MOMENTUM 4 (`bluez_card.XX_XX_XX_XX_XX_XX`) kept connecting in `headset-head-unit-cvsd` (mono, terrible). Cause: WirePlumber's `~/.local/state/wireplumber/default-profile` had saved that profile for the card and restores it on connect; `bluetooth.autoswitch-to-headset-profile` is on (default), and the default source is the BT mic (`bluez_input.…` loopback), so any app recording from the default source (WebRTC calls) flips the card to HFP again.
- **How it works:** Quickshell 0.3.2's Pipewire service has no card/profile API (checked its qmltypes), so the picker runs `pactl -f json list cards`, takes the card `bluez_card.<address with _>` from the sink's `api.bluez5.address`, lists its profiles (`off` and unavailable hidden; A2DP first by WirePlumber priority, labelled by the codec parsed from the description; `a2dp-sink` = the best codec, deduped against a named one; `headset-*` last as "Headset · <codec>" + "mic on, low quality", the header shows that warning in yellow). A choice runs `pactl set-card-profile <card> <profile>`, then re-reads at once and after 1.5 s; it also re-reads whenever `Pipewire.defaultAudioSink` changes (the sink node is replaced on every profile switch, incl. autoswitch).
- **Verified (PipeWire / WirePlumber 1.6.9 / 0.5.18):** `pactl set-card-profile` switches live (mpv kept playing, moved to stereo), the sink's `api.bluez5.codec` follows, and WirePlumber writes the choice to `default-profile`, so it is restored on the next connect. This headset offers SBC, SBC-XQ, AAC, aptX, aptX HD (`a2dp-sink`), mSBC/CVSD headset. Isolated `quickshell -n -p` copy with a test-only IPC hook: dropdown screenshot, pick AAC → card + label switched; live shell hot-reloaded clean, popup screenshot shows "aptX HD". Left on `a2dp-sink` (aptX HD). Not tested with real clicks (no input injection) or with the headset disconnected / in `off`.
- **Also changed:** `AudioPanel.qml` lost its fixed `implicitHeight: 200` (content-sized now), otherwise the open dropdowns overflowed the card.
- **Caveats / follow-ups:** the picker follows the *default* output only (a BT headset that is not the default sink shows no picker). Autoswitch to HFP is still on; if calls keep degrading playback, a WirePlumber drop-in (`~/.config/wireplumber/wireplumber.conf.d/*.conf`, `wireplumber.settings = { bluetooth.autoswitch-to-headset-profile = false }`) would stop it, at the cost of the headset mic in calls; not added, no `config/wireplumber` exists yet (would need an `install.sh` link). `bluetooth-autoswitch` state still saves `headset-head-unit-cvsd` as this card's headset profile (mSBC would be better for calls).

## Popup motion (`PopupHost.qml`, tokens in `Theme.qml`)

- **Spec** (Theme tokens, defaults = speed 1, curve cubic, style scale): open `popupOpenMs` = base 200 ms OutCubic (fade + scale 0.96->1 from the bar side/chip x + 10 px slide), close `popupCloseMs` = 0.75 x open (150 ms) with the SAME family decelerating and never overshooting (cubic/back -> OutCubic, quint -> OutQuint, expo -> OutExpo, linear), chip switch `popupMoveMs` 200 with the open curve for card x/width/height, content fade-out 35 % / fade-in 65 % of the base. Speed / curve / style are user settings (**Animations settings** below).
- **How it works:** `t` = open progress, driven by two explicit NumberAnimations (`openAnim` / `closeAnim`, started from the current t with the remaining share of the duration; NOT a Behavior, see the close fix below). `content`/`center` are the *displayed* popup and chip centre; `Popups.name`/`center` are the request. `sync()` handles cold open (load content, 40 ms for the layer surface to map, then t=1), switch (fade out, swap content+center, fade in; `morphing` true for ~300 ms so Behaviors on x/width/height glide), same-popup re-request while closing (resume). x is computed from the TARGET width so x and width animate together; y hangs off the bar edge so the card grows away from it. Content is a Loader at the target size inside an Item sized to the animating card (clipped only while morphing), so panels don't re-layout per frame. Behaviors are off outside `morphing`, so panels that animate their own height (CenterPanel collapse) are not smoothed twice. `layer.enabled` only during open/close (no text-over-background double blend).
- **Jitter root causes (before):** x/y jumped on switch (no animation at all); card height flashed to a wrong size for 2 frames after the content swap (loader had no item / panel settling); 28 px slide + 150 ms OutCubic = only ~6 visible frames; opacity applied per child; content width followed nothing but was swapped mid-fade.
- **Verified** (backdrop rig: a temporary solid-green layer window + wf-recorder on DP-3, card bbox/alpha per frame; rig removed afterwards): max per-frame x step 956 px -> 222 px (smooth OutCubic curve, ~14 frames per switch); open/close monotonic alpha; rapid A->B->C and mid-flight reversal retarget with no snap; same-chip toggle closes. Clamped (right-edge) and centred chips both tested via `qs ipc call popup toggle <name>`.
- **Not verified:** real mouse click path (only IPC driven; clicks use the same `Popups.toggle`), Esc, click-outside.
- **Close fix (2026-10-21, user: "closing scales and fades half way, then disappears"):** two causes, both measured with frame bursts over a solid backdrop. (1) `Behavior on t` had `easing.type: host.active ? open : close`; when the close started, that binding still gave the OPEN easing, so with the user's Back curve the close ran OutBack: t crossed 0 after 72 of 200 ms (logged t values), `visible: t > 0.01` hid the window, and t then swung to -0.1 while hidden. Now explicit animations, `visible: active || t > 0 || closeAnim.running`, and `finishClose()` drops the content (a function named `closed` clashes with the window's `closed` signal: "Duplicate method name"). (2) solid_glass is drawn at full strength whatever the item's opacity (and Hyprland's own layer fade also drops it in the first frame), so the dark glass stayed until the end and vanished in one frame. Glass during motion (2026-10-21, current): the popup / launcher card animates with the normal Scale / Slide / Fade styles and its glass region IS the card (Glass.qml maps it through the card's transforms every frame). The glass can only FADE with the card on the fork build with `decoration:blur:solid_glass:layer_alpha_ref` (originally branch `more-blur-rebased-20261021`; since 2026-10-22 installed as branch `glass-upstream-20261022` in ~/code/hyprland-blur-test; see that repo's NOTES.md "Rebase 2026-10-21 + glass fade on layers" for install / rollback): the glass of a layer surface is scaled per pixel by the surface's own alpha / ref. `managed.lua` `apply_glass_layer_alpha()` sets ref = 0.3 (= the card fill with glass) only when the option exists; on the installed build nothing is set and the glass cannot fade (see **Close cut** below for what the shell does there). Nested test with the patched build: glass luminance at a card spot open 39.1 -> 6.5 and close 6.5 -> 39.1, monotonic, no step. History (all replaced): a flat stand-in fill while moving (brighter: 17.3 vs 12.2), a tint-derived stand-in (no visible glass motion), glass scaled from the chip (content over raw wallpaper next to a dark box), a drawer reveal (user did not like it). Root cause for all of them: solid_glass alpha = (ignore_opacity ? blurA : a*blurA) * overallA in OpenGL.cpp renderTextureWithBlurInternal, ignore_opacity is global, blurA = Hyprland's own layer fade, and Quickshell has no wp_alpha_modifier. Test hook: `qs ipc call popup toggleOn <name> <monitor>`.
- **Close cut (2026-10-21, user: "the out animation has a hard cut", popups AND launcher):** measured with wf-recorder (every compositor frame, lossless) over a test checkerboard backdrop LAYER (test-only quickshell instance, namespace `testbackdrop`, sized to the card area, input mask empty), card-area mean diff vs the backdrop per frame. Root cause, on BOTH surfaces, only one: the glass. Installed build: the content and fill fade, the glass stays at full strength (diff ~77-79 of the ~91 open value) for the last ~4 frames at 60 Hz (popup) / ~55 ms at 360 Hz (launcher) as a dark empty box, then 77 -> 0 in one frame when Glass.qml drops the region / the window hides. No other cause: timing chain is fine (window hides only after closeAnim, region dropped at opacity <= 0.004 at the same time, qs-popup / qs-launcher no_anim, so no Hyprland layer fade). Patched build (nested, layer_alpha_ref 0.3): open and close already smooth with the unchanged motion code, close 45 -> 43 -> 32 -> 23 -> 15 -> 8 -> 4 -> 1.5 -> 0.8 -> 0 (launcher), 45 ... 5.4 -> 2.2 -> 0 (popup). **Fix:** `Theme.glassFades` (glass probe also reads `getoption decoration:blur:solid_glass:layer_alpha_ref`, true when > 0; re-checked on config reload) and `Theme.glassScaleAt(t)`: 1 when glassFades; otherwise, below t = `glassTailFrom` (0.15, content <= 15 % visible) the glass region shrinks about the card centre to nothing at t = 0.005 (cube-root mapping = linear in time for the cubic curves; ending at 0 is needed because Glass.qml drops the region below opacity 0.004, which otherwise left a 30 % box vanishing at once). Glass.qml applies an optional `glassScale` on a glass item (centre scale, radius clamped); PopupHost card and ModalMenu card set it from their `t`. Installed build after: popup close ... 78 -> 41 -> 14 -> 0.5 -> 0, launcher ... 76 -> 68 -> 60 -> ... -> 2 -> 1.2 -> 0.3 -> 0 (monotonic, no step). Open unchanged (its first frame is already past t = 0.15, so the glass still appears in one frame on the installed build, as before). **Caveat:** on the installed build the end of the close is the glass box shrinking to its centre (only 3 frames on the 60 Hz bar monitor), not a fade; no shell-side trick can fade it (alpha = blurA x overallA, no per-surface alpha from Quickshell). The real fix is installing the patched build; the shell then switches to the plain fade by itself. Not verified: the user's look at the shrink on the real wallpaper, the patched build on the 360 Hz monitor (nested window = 60 Hz), the launcher with launcherMotion off (untouched path). Seen but not fixed: one repeated frame right at the start of a close (layer.enabled switching on). Rig scripts were in the session scratchpad (closecut/: burst.sh, nburst.sh, an.py, strip.py).
- **Animation clock / fps (measured 2026-10-21):** one Quickshell process with windows on the 360 Hz MSI and the 60 Hz Dell: Qt's default animation driver runs animations too fast (800 ms NumberAnimation finished in 578 ms on both monitors), `QSG_USE_SIMPLE_ANIMATION_DRIVER=1` gives the right duration (801-803 ms) AND native frame rate (frameSwapped + distinct value counts: 352-356 fps on DP-1, 62 on DP-3). A single-window process is correct with either driver. Live shell with the env var: popup open on DP-1 71 frames / 200 ms (355 fps), on DP-3 12 frames / 200 ms (60 fps, the Dell is 60 Hz). So the env var stays; it does not cap the frame rate. Bar popups live on the Dell, i.e. 60 fps is that monitor's limit. Hyprland's layer fade for `qs-popup` is off (`no_anim` layer rule in rules.lua) so only QML animates; the launcher (`ModalMenu.animated`) got the same explicit animations / glass suspension, and managed.lua switches a `qs-launcher` `no_anim` rule on with `launcherMotion`.
- **Verified (close fix):** isolated instance (`quickshell -n -p` copy + a test-only popup with solid blocks and opaque test backdrop LAYERS under the popup / launcher area, so no window content can enter a capture; every frame's corners checked against the backdrop colour, the run aborts if focus is not on the test monitor). Card background / content luminance per frame: before, close kept the glass dark (19) until 83 ms and jumped to the backdrop (77) with content still at 41/255; after, open and close are monotonic at 1x and 0.5x down to 77/78 before the window hides; style None opens/closes in one frame with no intermediate frame; a chip switch keeps the glass and cross-fades; launcher "Like popups" monotonic both ways. Not verified: on the real bar monitor / real chip clicks (test popup opened via IPC on the other monitor), how the unblurred backdrop during motion looks over busy wallpapers, the one repeated frame at the start of an open (surface map).
- **Test:** open one popup, then `qs ipc call popup toggle media` / `net` / `cal` in turn: the card should glide and cross-fade; same name again closes.

## Animations settings (Settings > Animations, 2026-10-20)

- **Files:** `Motion.qml` (singleton, the only writer of `${XDG_STATE_HOME:-~/.local/state}/quickshell/animations.json`; `QS_ANIM_FILE` overrides; IPC `qs ipc call anim get | set <key> <value> | unset <key> | reset popup|hypr|all`), `SettingsAnimations.qml` (page id `animations`: `qs ipc call settings page animations`), `Theme.qml` popup tokens, `PopupHost.qml`, `ModalMenu.qml` (`animated`, used by `Launcher.qml`), `hypr/hyprland/animations.lua` (NEW: the hand-written curves + leaves, moved verbatim out of `settings.lua`, which now `require`s it; edit hand-written animation values THERE), `hypr/hyprland/managed.lua` (animations part).
- **Keys** (flat, missing = default, same pattern as glass.json/appearance.json): `popupSpeed` 0.25-10 (higher = faster; durations = base / speed, floored at 16 ms = one frame, so 10x gives 20 ms open and 16 ms fades, never 1-7 ms), `popupCurve` cubic|quint|expo|back|linear (open/move Out*, close In*; back = OutBack open, InCubic close), `popupStyle` scale|slide|fade|none (scale 0.96 + 10 px slide / 24 px slide / fade only / 0 ms); `launcherMotion` bool (default false = launcher has no motion of its own, only Hyprland's layer fade, as before; true = `ModalMenu.animated`: the launcher card fades/scales/slides from above and its height glides with the SAME popup speed/curve/style tokens; the launcher shares the popup settings, only this on/off is its own; reset with "Reset popups"); `hyprEnabled` bool (animations.enabled), `hyprSpeed` 0.25-10 (divides every leaf speed of animations.lua; effective factor hyprSpeed x group 0.0625-100, leaf speed floored at 0.01 ds, which the fork accepts), `hyprCurve` config|smooth|snappy|overshoot|linear|spring (config = each leaf keeps its own curve; presets registered as `qs_<id>` curves in managed.lua `M.ANIM_CURVES`); per group `<g>Enabled` / `<g>Speed` (multiplies hyprSpeed) / `<g>Curve` for g in windows, layers, fade, workspaces, specialWorkspace (label "Scratchpad"), border (leaf lists in `M.ANIM_GROUPS`; `global`/`zoomFactor` only follow the global values). Styles (popin, slidefade) are always kept.
- **Speed sliders (2026-10-20, user request):** every speed slider (popups, Hyprland, per category) uses the same range (`Motion.speedRange` = managed.lua `M.ANIM_SPEED` = 0.25-10) and the same log mapping (`SettingsAnimations.toNorm/fromNorm`): 1x at ~38 % with a tick (new optional `Slider.mark`, default -1 = none, so other sliders are unchanged), snap to 1x within 1.5 % of the track, steps 0.05 / 0.1 (>=2x) / 0.25 (>=5x). Spring curves per factor are named `qs_spring_<round(f*100)>` (2.5x -> 250, 7.5x -> 750, up to 10000); measured stable (no ringing) at f = 10, 30, 100. Verified via `qs ipc call anim set`: 10x -> 0.05 + qs_spring_1000, 10x x windows 10x -> 0.01 + qs_spring_10000, 0.25x -> 2.0 + qs_spring_25, 2.5x linear -> 0.2; out-of-range input (12, 0.1) is clamped; page header showed "open 20 ms" at 10x and "open 800 ms" at 0.25x; screenshot with popups, Hyprland and Windows at 2.5x (same knob position on the equal-width popup/Hyprland tracks). The category tracks are narrower (toggle in the row), so the same value sits at the same fraction, not the same pixel.
- **Shared base: 1x = 200 ms on both sides (2026-10-20, user: "1x Quickshell is way faster than 1x Hyprland").** `A.BASE_MS` in `hypr/hyprland/animations.lua` (every hand-written leaf is `speed = BASE_MS / 100`; Hyprland speed is in ds, measured: linear speed 3 = 300 ms) and `Motion.baseMs` in Motion.qml MUST be equal (the Lua config cannot read QML). Popups: open/close/switch = baseMs / factor, content fades 35 % / 65 % of it. Spring preset: time-scaled by factor x `settle_ms` (575, measured settle-to-2 % of the unscaled "easy"-shaped spring) / BASE_MS, so it settles in BASE_MS / factor (1x = `qs_spring_288`). Page headers show "1x = 200 ms · now N ms". Chosen 200 ms = the popups' existing duration (popups unchanged); Hyprland's hand-written leaves went from speed 0.5 (50 ms) to 2.0 (200 ms), i.e. "As configured" at 1x is 4x slower than before; 4x now equals the old Hyprland look. With no file, managed.lua still applies nothing; the hand-written values ARE the base.
- **Qt animation clock:** Quickshell is started with `QSG_USE_SIMPLE_ANIMATION_DRIVER=1` (`hypr/hyprland/autostart.lua`). Qt's default vsync-driven driver ran QML animations ~1.4x too fast on the high-refresh (360 Hz) monitor (isolated test: 800 ms linear fade took ~580 ms there, ~785 ms on the 60 Hz one; with the simple driver ~780 ms on both). A shell restarted by hand needs the same env (`hyprctl dispatch "hl.dsp.exec_cmd('env QSG_USE_SIMPLE_ANIMATION_DRIVER=1 quickshell')"`), otherwise QML motion on that monitor is fast again.
- **Timing measured after the change** (window move settle-to-2 %, popup preview linear fade; table in the session scratchpad): 1x linear 185 / spring 210 / popup 196 ms; 2x 95 / 106 / 95; 0.5x 383 / 417 / 375 ("quick" as configured 150 / 84 / 292: that curve reaches 98 % early). Method: throwaway floating kitty (solid red) over a solid kitty backdrop, `hl.dsp.window.move`, grim 20 px strips; popups: Settings preview card with style fade + curve linear. Not measured: workspace switches (would move the user's workspace), real bar popups (they open on the bar monitor), a perceived comparison by the user.
- **Live apply:** popups: Theme tokens bind to Motion, PopupHost picks them up at the next open. Hyprland: after a debounced save that changed a Hyprland key, Motion runs `hyprctl eval "if anim_apply then anim_apply() end"`; `anim_apply()` re-reads the file and rewrites EVERY leaf from animations.lua (so an unset key restores the hand-written value) plus `animations.enabled` (unset -> baseline read with `hl.get_config` at load). A config reload re-applies the file. Hand edits of the file are picked up (FileView watch).
- **Springs (bug fixed 2026-10-20):** a spring curve must be referenced as `hl.animation{ spring = "<name>" }`; `bezier = "<spring>"` logs `no such bezier` and leaves the leaf unchanged WITHOUT raising (pcall says ok), so choosing Spring silently froze every leaf at the previous state (user saw old snappy 0.5 values; the save -> `anim_apply()` path itself was working). `M.anim_leaf` now sets `spring =` for spring presets. Springs also IGNORE `speed` (measured: window move identical at speed 0.5 and 5), so the factor f = hyprSpeed x group speed is baked into a per-factor curve `qs_spring_<f*100>` with stiffness*f^2, dampening*f (time-scaled, same shape); `hyprctl animations` shows `spring:qs_spring_30` plus a speed value that has no effect. A hand-written leaf with `spring =` in animations.lua is passed through under "As configured" but is NOT speed-scaled. Measured with a throwaway floating kitty moved by `hl.dsp.window.move` and grim strips: qs_spring_30 still at 1487 px of a 1600 px move after 309 ms, qs_spring_300 settled in ~250 ms.
- **Verified 2026-10-20:** Lua unit test with a stub `hl` (parsing, clamping, bad values ignored, group x global speed math); live: set keys via `qs ipc call anim set` (same `Motion.set` path as the controls) -> `hyprctl -j animations` showed `qs_snappy` 0.33 (0.5/1.5), windows `qs_overshoot`, fade disabled, workspaces 0.67, `getoption animations:enabled` false with hyprEnabled false; after `reset all` + `reload config-only` every leaf and animations.enabled equal the pre-change snapshot; `configerrors` empty. Screenshots of the page (defaults, changed, Hyprland off) over a throwaway solid kitty backdrop. Hyprland curves persist across `reload config-only` (a curve defined once stays listed).
- **Verified (fix round):** via `qs ipc call anim set/reset` (same `Motion.set`/`reset` the controls call): the json -> `hyprctl -j animations` exactly (spring 0.3x -> `spring:qs_spring_30` 1.67 on every leaf; linear/overshoot/windowsSpeed 2 -> 0.83), "Reset Hyprland" -> identical to the pre-change snapshot, user file restored byte-identical; a `reload config-only` re-runs managed.lua and re-applies the file. Launcher: grim captures over a solid backdrop with launcherMotion off (unchanged), on (mid-open faded, open identical, closing faded), page row screenshot.
- **Not verified:** real clicks/drags on the page (no synthetic input), the preview replay animation and the actual bar-popup motion with non-default curves/styles (tokens only; the probed popup opened on the monitor with the bar, not captured), the launcher height glide while typing, per-group disable side effects (disabling `fade` also disables the inherited fadeSwitch/fadeShadow/fadeDim/... leaves).

## Notification click -> Brave web app window (2026-10-21)

- **Root cause:** Chromium/Brave sends every notification (all sites and `--app=` windows) with `app_name=Brave` and `desktop-entry=brave-browser`, so `bin/focus-app` scored the main `brave-browser` window (class equality) and never the web app window (class `brave-<host>__<path>-<Profile>`, e.g. `brave-web.whatsapp.com__-Default`).
- **Change:** `NotifCard.activate()` reads the `x-kde-origin-name` hint (Brave's binary contains the string; Quickshell exposes it in `n.hints`) and passes it as 4th arg to `bin/focus-app`, which gives a window whose class matches `^(brave|chrome|chromium)-<host>__` a score of 20 (above everything else). No app window of that origin open (or no hint) -> old scoring, i.e. the main browser. The default action is still invoked, the focus dispatch/retry is unchanged.
- **Verified:** `focus-app` scoring against a stubbed `hyprctl` (several fake clients: right window picked for host and for full-URL origin, lookalike class not matched, empty/unknown origin falls back to `brave-browser`); isolated Quickshell + private dbus session shows `n.hints` carries `x-kde-origin-name`. **Not verified:** a real Brave notification (the exact value Brave puts in the hint, host vs URL vs app name, is assumed; the script accepts host or URL). The click log line now prints `origin=[...]` to check it.
- **Caveats:** only works when the web app is open as its own window; origin with a different host than the app's start URL (e.g. a notification sent from a sub-domain) will not match. Test: `bash config/quickshell/bin/focus-app brave-browser Brave "" web.whatsapp.com` (focuses for real).

## Link router: native-app links -> Brave web app window (`config/url-router`, 2026-10-21)

- **What:** `bin/url-router` (python3 -I, no deps; `~/.local/bin/url-router` symlink) + `url-router.desktop.in` (installed as `~/.local/share/applications/url-router.desktop`, `@ROUTER@` -> absolute path, NoDisplay) set as default for `x-scheme-handler/http` + `https` (not `text/html`: local files still go straight to Brave). install.sh does the link, the sed and `xdg-mime default url-router.desktop x-scheme-handler/http x-scheme-handler/https`. Every argument is a URL (no flags). http(s) link whose host matches a `webapp_*.desktop` with a Brave/Chromium argv[0] and `--app=<start url>` -> that entry's own argv with `--app=<link>` (keeps e.g. `--profile-directory`, drops field codes); anything else -> `brave -- <url>` (shim via PATH; `--` so nothing becomes a flag). Never calls xdg-open; `URL_ROUTER_ACTIVE` env guards re-entry; fallbacks named xdg-open / gio / url-router etc. are refused.
- **Matching:** urlsplit; URLs with `user@` / `user:pass@` never match; host lowercased, IDNA -> punycode, trailing dot and ONE leading `www.` stripped, must be equal (no sub-domains unless aliased); ports equal with default ports counted equal (http link matches an https app); a start URL with a path (`/web`) only takes links at/below it; longest start path wins. Optional `~/.config/url-router/config` (template `config/url-router/config.example`): `fallback = <cmd>`, `disable = <slug>...`, `alias = <slug> <host|*.domain>...`, `dry_run = -|<file>` (print the argv JSON instead of launching); env `URL_ROUTER_FALLBACK`, `URL_ROUTER_DRY_RUN`.
- **Existing window (tested 2026-10-21 on a throwaway `--user-data-dir` profile, windows on a silent special workspace via `hl.dsp.exec_cmd(cmd, {workspace="special:x silent"})`, counted with CDP + `hyprctl clients`):** every `brave --app=<url>` forwarded to a running instance opens a NEW app window, also for the exact URL of an already open app window; nothing is reused and an existing window cannot be navigated without CDP / an extension. Decision: always open a new app window with the link (like launching the web app with a deep link); no focus-only mode (it would drop the link). The new window's class follows the LINK (`brave-<host>__<link path with / -> _>-Default`), not the entry's StartupWMClass; `bin/focus-app`'s `^brave-<host>__` match still works, exact-class rules / launcher grouping do not. Headless Brave does not take part in the process singleton (second launch aborts), so that check needs a real window. `brave -- <url>` verified to open the URL (headless throwaway profile).
- **Grouping (opt-in, 2026-10-21):** config `group = <slug>... | *` (default off; enabled for one app in the user's own config, outside the repo). Under Hyprland (needs `HYPRLAND_INSTANCE_SIGNATURE` + a working `hyprctl`, else the plain launch) the router forks (parent exits at once, the caller is never held; the child logs `group <slug>: <status>` to stderr = journal, no titles / URLs), reads `hyprctl -j clients` (only address / class / initialClass / grouped / focusHistoryID / mapped ...), picks the app's windows by class host (`^(brave|chrome|chromium)-<host>__`, same host rules + aliases as the link matcher), takes the lowest focusHistoryID, focuses it (`hl.dsp.focus({window="address:..."})`, retried until `activewindow` agrees, max 4 tries), `hl.dsp.group.toggle()` if it is not grouped, launches, then polls clients every 0.1 s for at most 5 s for the new app window: in the group already = `joined` (Hyprland's default `auto_group` does it for the focused group), else one Lua IIFE `t.group:add(w)` (skipped if the group is locked / denied or the new window is in another group) = `added`; no window -> nothing more (`timeout`). Statuses: no-window (plain launch), not-focused (launched, no group change), joined, added, target-gone, timeout. The link is always launched, also when anything in the grouping path fails.
  - **Verified in a nested `/usr/bin/Hyprland` 0.56.2** (own `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR` under /tmp, socket paths are too long for the scratchpad; WAYLAND_DISPLAY must be the absolute parent socket path then; started through the live compositor with `hl.dsp.exec_cmd(cmd, {workspace="special:<name> silent", no_initial_focus=true})` so it stayed hidden; minimal config with default group options like the live one; throwaway kitty windows with Chromium-style classes, a stub `brave` that opens one per `--app` URL; killed by exact PID): no window -> plain window; one ungrouped -> both in one group; already grouped -> joined; two candidates on different workspaces -> joined the most recently focused one (focus switched to its workspace); group shared with another app -> joined it; floating -> joined (group floating); fullscreen -> joined, the group leaves fullscreen (shown maximized); hidden special workspace -> special opened, joined; window on the other monitor (headless output in the nested instance) -> focus moved there, joined; a window rule sending the new window to another workspace -> `added` (pulled into the group); bogus / missing signature -> plain launch, rc 0. **Not verified:** real Brave windows (a real deep link to an app with an open window), a real group lock.
- **Portal / Discord (Flatpak `com.discordapp.Discord`):** in-sandbox `xdg-open` = portal OpenURI; xdg-desktop-portal skips the app chooser for http/https/mailto/webcal and launches GIO's default for `x-scheme-handler/<scheme>` (reads `~/.config/mimeapps.list`; no `hyprland-mimeapps.list` exists) on the host. Verified with a temporary `dry_run = <file>` config: direct gdbus OpenURI call and `flatpak run --command=sh com.discordapp.Discord -c 'xdg-open <placeholder link>'` both reached the router with the right decision. Discord needs nothing else. **Not verified:** a real click in the Discord app (Electron shell.openExternal assumed to use that xdg-open), a real routed launch into the user's Brave.
- **Caveats:** Brave's "make default browser" button (or `xdg-settings set default-web-browser`) puts brave-browser.desktop back as the http(s) default: re-run the xdg-mime line. xdg-open's own desktop-file parser does not unquote Exec, so the Exec path is unquoted (no spaces in $HOME). Webapps outside `XDG_DATA_HOME/applications` or not named `webapp_*` are ignored.
- **Roll back:** `xdg-mime default brave-browser.desktop x-scheme-handler/http x-scheme-handler/https` (or whatever `xdg-mime query default x-scheme-handler/https` said before); optionally delete `~/.local/share/applications/url-router.desktop` and `~/.local/bin/url-router`.
- **Tests:** `python3 -I -m unittest discover -s config/url-router/tests` (32 cases; grouping: class parsing, window selection (lookalikes, unmapped, initialClass, sub-domain only via alias, newest focus first), decision steps against a fake Hypr (no window / toggle + joined / already grouped / Lua add / focus failure / exception still launches), a detach test with stub `hyprctl` + `brave` on PATH (parent exits < 1 s, child logs `joined`), no signature = plain exec; the original 22: parsing / www / case / ports / http vs https / sub-domain + lookalike / userinfo + backslash tricks / path-scoped apps / punycode / weird inputs / aliases + disable / loop-refusing fallback; exact argv with a stub `brave` first on PATH in a throwaway HOME, shell metacharacters inert, `-` args after `--`, two URLs; xdg-mime default + `xdg-open` in a scratch XDG env (needs a WAYLAND_DISPLAY value for xdg-open to consult xdg-mime; BROWSER is a stub so nothing real can start)). Check a decision without launching: `URL_ROUTER_DRY_RUN=- url-router <url>` (also prints whether grouping is on).

## Notification swipe-to-dismiss (`SwipeDismiss.qml`, 2026-10-21)

- **What:** drag a notification to the right with the mouse to dismiss it, in the toasts and in the control center's notification list. One building block, `SwipeDismiss.qml` (qmldir entry), used only by `NotifCard.qml`; `NotifCard.dismiss()` is now the single dismiss path (close button, right click, click-to-app, swipe): toast -> `Notifs.dismissPopup` + `releaseLater`, center -> `leave()` (row collapse, then `n.dismiss()`).
- **Behaviour:** a `DragHandler` re-parented onto the card (`target: null`, x axis only, `dragThreshold` 6 px, left button). Offset follows the pointer 1:1 to the right; left is a rubber band approaching 24 px. Fade = 1 - offset / width. Release dismisses when offset >= 38 % of the width (152 px on a 400 px toast), or on a flick >= 900 px/s to the right that moved >= 24 px (velocity = smoothed per-move estimate, zeroed if the pointer paused > 100 ms before release). Fly-off to x = width: `Theme.popupMs(baseMs * 0.75 * max(0.4, rest / width))` with `popupEaseClose`, shortened to match a fast flick (OutCubic starts at 3x mean speed). Spring-back: `popupOpenMs` / `popupEaseOpen` (Back curve = slight overshoot). Gap close: `popupMoveMs` / `popupEaseClose` (no overshoot: a height must not go negative). All follow Settings > Animations.
- **Why a Translate, not x:** Toasts drives the card's x (slide-in, `slot.slideX`) and `leave()` animates x; the swipe offset is a `Translate` on the card. Glass.qml collects transforms at search time, so the toast's glass moves with it. Toasts multiply `card.swipeFade` into their own opacity; the toast timeout pauses while `card.swiping` (drag, spring-back, fly-off) as well as on hover. A swiped card skips the old slide-out step (`swipedOut`), so only the gap closes.
- **Clicks / lists:** under 6 px of movement it stays a click (the card's TapHandlers); child MouseAreas (close, action buttons) keep their presses. Axis lock: if the gesture is more vertical than horizontal when the threshold is crossed it is ignored and its grab may be taken over; the ListView (startDragDistance ~10 px) takes vertical drags before the handler activates. While horizontal, the handler does not approve a takeover, so the list cannot steal it. CenterPanel list: `spacing: 0`, each row carries its own 8 px gap (`index < count - 1`), so the gap collapses with the row instead of jumping 8 px when the JS-array model rebuilds.
- **Verified:** qmltestrunner unit tests (offscreen, stub Theme/Motion, outside the repo: session scratchpad `notif-swipe/unit/`, `sync.sh` copies the real file in and runs them): 14/14, covering the pure threshold / flick / fly-duration functions, click vs drag, child button click, 1:1 follow + fade, spring-back below the threshold, dismiss past it, flick, flick-then-pause, left rubber band, vertical gesture ignored, ListView vertical scroll vs horizontal swipe. Nested Hyprland (floating window on the second monitor, test Quickshell copy in its own `dbus-run-session`, test notifications sent only to that private bus) driven by a test-only `zwlr_virtual_pointer_v1` client that refuses any socket but the nested one; frame strips captured with grim on the nested socket: toast below-threshold spring-back, past-threshold dismiss + gap close, flick, click with 3 px movement (default action ran), panel list swipe + reflow, panel vertical scroll (list scrolled, no card moved). Live shell hot-reloaded clean.
- **Toast window height (fix 2026-10-21, found in the swipe frame captures, also hit the old click / timeout exit):** Toasts.qml sized its layer window to the animating column, so every frame of a gap opening / closing resized the surface, and the compositor showed the previous buffer stretched to the new size: the remaining cards looked squashed and overlapping during the reflow (a single shrink after the collapse still squashed one frame). Now the window height comes from the cards' FULL heights (`slot.fullHeight`, `updateStack()` on item added / removed / height change) and, with `Theme.glass` (solid_glass: only the cards are glass, the rest is transparent and outside the input mask `Region { item: col }`), it only grows while toasts are up and drops when the stack is empty (`heldHeight`). Without solid_glass the whole window is the blur region, so there it follows the stack (one resize after a card has left; that path was not captured). Verified nested at scale 1: dense per-frame strips of the reflow show the cards sliding up with matching glass; urgency stripe heights constant per frame.
- **Nested-only artifact:** at a nested output scale of 0.5 the glass of every glass surface (toasts AND the control-center popup) covered only about the left / top half of the item: the blur region is mis-scaled at fractional scale < 1 in the compositor. At scale 1 (both real monitors) the toast glass covers the whole card (nested capture). Other scales (1.25-3) not checked.
- **Residual (not fixed, not caused by the swipe):** in one capture the first frame of the reflow showed the glass region already at the NEXT frame's position (region one frame ahead of the buffer, ~18 px at the motion start); repeat captures did not show it. Looks like a general Glass.qml region-vs-buffer timing issue at the start of motion.
- **Not verified:** real mouse / touchpad feel (only synthetic pointer events), 360 Hz monitor (nested runs at 60 Hz). The installed Hyprland is the fork with `layer_alpha_ref` (0.3), so the toast glass fades with the card during the drag and fly-off (the nested tests ran that same binary). Touchpad two-finger horizontal scroll does nothing (not a drag).
- **Caveat:** any config reload drops the notification history (`keepOnReload: false` in Notifs.qml, unchanged).

## Stock Hyprland (workstation) compatibility (2026-10-21)

The workstation runs the Arch `hyprland` package (0.56.2, no `decoration:blur:variant` option at all); the battlestation runs the fork. Audited with `/usr/bin/Hyprland` 0.56.2 nested, the repo config with autostart and the tv / steam scripts stubbed, `HOSTNAME=CrossWorkstation` (host.lua reads `$HOSTNAME` first, so that is the host override), a test copy of the shell with its own D-Bus session and state dir:

| Feature | Stock Hyprland |
| --- | --- |
| Config load, `hyprctl configerrors` | works: empty (solid_glass block is battlestation-only; `layer_alpha_ref`, glass options probed with `pcall(hl.get_config)`) |
| animations.lua curves incl. `spring`, `no_anim` layer rules, `set_enabled`, `hyprctl eval` | works (all exist in 0.56.2) |
| Settings > Animations (`anim_apply`) | works: every preset incl. spring, speeds 0.25-10x, group off, `hyprEnabled` off, launcherMotion; no errors |
| Theme.glass / glassFades | false (probe regex finds no variant); cards use `bar_opacity + 0.3` fill, the whole window is the blur region |
| Popups, launcher, toasts | work (plain blur, 0.85 fill). Close: the plain blur stays full until the card alpha drops under `ignore_alpha` 0.1, then vanishes (a blurred box for the last frames). Same as before 2026-10-21 (the card already faded in QML), not a regression; the glass shrink fallback does not apply to the whole-window region |
| HoverGlow, SwipeDismiss, toggleOn IPC, QSG env var | work (pure QML; env var not host-gated, harmless) |
| Slider pressed lens (in-app shader) | works: same lens, built-in Glass page defaults for the optics (GlassTuneCtl has no solid_glass to read), plain blur behind the card; nested stock 0.56.2 checked. Software scene graph or a missing .qsb: the handle grows instead (any host) |
| GTK4 / GTK3 / Kvantum / Brave Wall-Glass alphas | work after a local `theme.py render`: the fill follows the running compositor (0.3 solid_glass, `bar_opacity` 0.55 on stock since 2026-10-21; was 0.85). Was broken: the rendered files were committed, so a battlestation render (0.3) reached the workstation with every pull and Nautilus was nearly see-through over plain blur (nested screenshot). Fixed: they live in `out/host/` (git-ignored); a pull touching `config/theme/` re-renders them via `.githooks/post-merge` / `post-rewrite` -> `theme-render` (skip with `THEME_RENDER_SKIP=1`) |
| `qt_style = "kvantum"` without Kvantum installed | falls back to Fusion with a message (`theme.py qt_style()`); install `kvantum` for the glass Qt look |
| Video wallpapers on the work machine: RX 5700 (RDNA1, 8 GB VRAM, VCN 2.0: H.264 / HEVC / VP9 decode, H.264 / HEVC VA-API encode, NO AV1) | simulated (`THEME_FORCE_NO_HWDEC=av1`, `THEME_BAKE_VRAM_GB=8`, h264_vaapi available), not run on the machine: an AV1 original is never played, the still stays until the copy exists; output HEVC Main (`hevc_vaapi` qp 16; VCN 2.0 decodes and encodes HEVC; H.264 only if the probes fail), RIFE (Vulkan / RADV, long-lived directory-mode processes, `-j 2:1:2`) with 3 workers at 1440p by the VRAM rule (8 GB, ~6.5 free), an out-of-memory failure halves the workers for the rest of the bake; levels HEVC 5.1 / 5.2 (H.264 fallback: 5.2, 4K120 -> 60); RIFE speed there unmeasured |

Not verified on the real workstation. After pulling there: `~/.config/theme/theme.py render` once (relinks GTK CSS to `out/host/`; until then the old links dangle and GTK apps use stock Adwaita).

## Bar glass (2026-10-21)

The bar container is a glass surface like the popup cards: `Bar.qml`'s background Rectangle has `isGlassItem: true` and radius `Theme.barRadius`, so Glass.qml declares it as the blur region (docked: full width, square; floating: the window is already inset by `Theme.barGap`, the region follows it with the Appearance radius corners, and the transparent gap around it is never blurred). It follows the bar rectangle live (it is a binding to the item), so toggling floating / gap / radius / top-bottom in Settings needs nothing else. Exclusive zone / `BarReserve.qml` untouched.
- **Fill:** new `Theme.barFill` = `cardFill` (0.3) on solid_glass, else `bar_opacity` (so on stock the bar looks exactly as before: whole-window blur region via Glass.qml's fallback, `qs-.*` layer rule, `bar_opacity` fill). `SettingsBar.qml`'s preview uses it too. On solid_glass `bar_opacity` is no longer used by the bar (the cards never used it either); border unchanged.
- **Chips:** `Chip.qml` no longer sets `isGlassItem`: chips sit flat on the bar's glass (a glass item's subtree is not searched, but chips are siblings of the background, so they would have been a second lens on top of it).
- **Verified (2026-10-21):** nested Hyprland (the installed fork binary, own `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR`, headless output `TEST`, isolated `HOME`/`XDG_*` with a copy of the shell, a checkerboard Background-layer window as backdrop, `grim -g` on the bar strip only): docked-bottom, floating-bottom, floating-top, docked-top; floating changed to docked and top/bottom by rewriting `appearance.json` / `bars.json` in the test home (live reload, no restart). Lens / rim follow the rounded floating bar, docked bar is square, gap area shows the unblurred backdrop. Live shell reloaded ("Configuration Loaded"), `hyprctl configerrors` empty.
- **Chip binding loop (2026-10-21):** `Chip.qml` row `x` logged `Binding loop detected for property "x"` in bursts of ~7 at runtime (not on a plain reload; seen after a chip's content changed, e.g. around the Home Assistant connect). Not caused by the glass commit (that Chip.qml change only dropped `isGlassItem`). The x reads laid-out values (`row.width`, ink insets from the labels' widths), which a layout pass changes while x is being evaluated. Fix: x moved into a `Binding { target: row; property: "x"; delayed: true }` outside the RowLayout; positions unchanged (chip x/width/row.x logged for all 14 chips before and after, identical). Not reproduced on demand, so the fix is confirmed only by a clean log after reloads; if the warning returns, look at which chip it is.
- **Not verified:** the stock Hyprland path (unchanged code path: `glass` false -> `barFill` = `bar_opacity`, whole-window region), scales other than 1, multi-monitor. A "Binding loop ... Chip.qml:50 RowLayout x" warning shows in the live log on reload; not attributed to this change (the chip layout code is untouched).

## zsh / zoxide (`.zshrc`, 2026-10-22)
- zoxide used to be the full `zoxide init zsh` output pasted mid-file (stale, with nvm/starship/PATH lines after it). Now `eval "$(zoxide init zsh)"` is the last line of `.zshrc`; keep it last (its doctor warns if anything after it changes the chpwd hooks).
- `alias cd=z`: Claude Code's Bash tool replays aliases/functions from a shell snapshot but not `chpwd_functions`, so every `cd` there printed the doctor warning. `.zshrc` sets `_ZO_DOCTOR=0` only when `$CLAUDECODE` is set. Verified: `zsh -ic 'cd /tmp; z /home'` prints no warning and `chpwd_functions` contains `__zoxide_hook`. Already-running Claude sessions keep the old snapshot until restarted.

## bash (`.bashrc`, `.bash_profile`, `.inputrc`, 2026-10-22)
- Interactive shell is bash now. `~/.bashrc`, `~/.bash_profile`, `~/.inputrc` are symlinks to the same files in the repo root (created by hand, like `~/.zshrc`; install.sh still only generates the zsh setup). `.zshrc` is untouched, so switching back = `chsh -s /bin/zsh`.
- `.bash_profile` carries the TTY1 autologin `exec session-supervisor.sh` block that lives in the (unmanaged) `~/.zprofile` for zsh, then sources `.bashrc`. Originals of the old plain files: scratchpad `bash-backup/`.
- Ported: aliases, history (50k, append, erasedups, ignorespace), bash-completion, case-insensitive completion + Up/Down prefix search (`.inputrc`), Esc-Esc sudo prefix, common OMZ git aliases (subset), zellij tab rename (PROMPT_COMMAND, not chpwd), webp-all/update/create_brave_webapp, nvm, starship, zoxide last with `_ZO_DOCTOR=0` under `$CLAUDECODE`.
- Not ported: autosuggestions, syntax highlighting, the rest of Oh My Zsh (all git aliases, sudo toggle, themes), bun's zsh completion file. starship rewrites `PROMPT_COMMAND` into an array, so own hooks are appended after its init.
- Still zsh: `hosts/shared/user.nix` (`shell = pkgs.zsh`), `programs.zsh.enable`, `modules/home/shell.nix`, install.sh (installs zsh/OMZ, `chsh -s /bin/zsh`). `config/zellij/layouts/nvim.kdl` now runs bash.

## macOS (secondary machine, 2026-10-23)

Occasional-use Mac (arm64, Homebrew in /opt/homebrew, zsh). Not Arch: never run `install.sh` there.
- **Linked** (`~/.config/<app>` -> `config/<app>`, same convention as `install.sh`): kitty, zellij, nvim, herdr, theme (kitty/zellij/nvim/herdr read `~/.config/theme/out/*`, committed outputs, so no theme engine run is needed). `~/.zshrc` -> repo `.zshrc`. Pre-existing targets were moved to `<path>.bak-mac-<timestamp>`. Not linked (apps not installed): ghostty, alacritty, fastfetch, opencode.
- **Installed (brew)**: kitty, neovim, zellij, herdr (formula `herdr`), ripgrep, fd, node, lazygit, starship, zoxide, imagemagick, tree-sitter-cli, zsh-autosuggestions, zsh-syntax-highlighting, cask `font-paper-mono`. Oh My Zsh is a git clone in `~/.oh-my-zsh`.
- **OS guards in `.zshrc`**: brew `shellenv` on darwin; Oh My Zsh sourced only if present (`autoload add-zsh-hook` added); autosuggestions/highlighting from brew share dir on mac, `/usr/share/zsh/plugins` on Linux; `starship` guarded; `/home/<user>` paths now `$HOME`; Android SDK only if `/opt/android-sdk` exists; `update()` runs `brew update && brew upgrade` on mac.
- **Zellij clipboard**: `copy_command "wl-copy"` stays (Linux). On the Mac a host-local symlink `~/.local/bin/wl-copy -> /usr/bin/pbcopy` makes it work. Not in the repo.
- **Fonts**: Paper Mono installed; Font Awesome Pro and the WC3 Icons font are not (licensed / plugin package), so those glyphs fall back. The zellij `claude-tabs` plugin (`config/zellij/plugins/`, git-ignored) is not set up on the Mac. **Zellij fallback:** the `zellij` function in `.zshrc` checks for `$ZELLIJ_CONFIG_DIR|~/.config/zellij/plugins/claude-tabs/claude-tabs.wasm`; if missing it adds `--layout compact` (bare/flag-only calls), maps `-l nvim` to `layouts/nvim-compact.kdl`, and appends `options --default-layout compact` to `attach -c/-b`; plugin present = plain passthrough (Linux unchanged). Applies to interactive zsh only (kitty does not launch zellij itself). Verified: arg rewriting with a stubbed `command`, real detached sessions dump `zellij:compact-bar`, no new plugin errors in the log. Caveat: `zellij --layout x attach -cb` does not create a session (use `attach -cb name options --default-layout x`); bash has no wrapper.
- **Kitty (2026-10-23)**: the config parses and loads cleanly on mac (isolated kitty 0.49.2, stderr log: only the known `tab_active_*` warnings; Paper Mono resolves to the variable `PaperMono[wght].ttf`). A kitty started before `~/.config/kitty` was linked keeps its old (empty) config until fully quit (cmd+Q) and relaunched; that is the most likely reason for "does not work" (assumed, not provable headlessly). One real mac breakage found: the `symbol_map ... WC3 Icons` lines map the coloured-circle emoji to a missing font, kitty then falls back to Menlo (log: "font WC3 Icons was not found"). Fix: those lines moved to `config/kitty/linux.conf`, pulled in by `include ${KITTY_OS}.conf` in `kitty.conf` (Linux resulting config identical); new `config/kitty/macos.conf` sets `macos_option_as_alt yes` (both option keys = alt, so right option drives zellij Alt+hjkl). Caveats: kitty's font kitten appended a `BEGIN_KITTY_FONTS` block (`family='Paper Mono' variable_name=PaperMono ...`) to `kitty.conf` and commented the old `font_family` lines; that block is mac-resolved, so check it on Linux (fontconfig) before keeping. `kitty.conf.bak` is kitty's backup. Not verified: any visual rendering (no screenshots allowed), `macos_option_as_alt` effect, `host.py` geninclude on Linux. `macos.conf` also sets `hide_window_decorations titlebar-only` (no title bar, keeps shadow and rounded corners).
- **AeroSpace + Karabiner (2026-10-23, shortcuts only, key behaviour untested)**: `config/aerospace/aerospace.toml` (0.21.3, reads `~/.config/aerospace/`; `start-at-login` is per user) and `config/karabiner/` (dir symlinked; `karabiner.json` is GENERATED by `python3 -I config/karabiner/generate.py`, edit the table there; `--aerospace` prints the matching binding block; `.gitignore` for `automatic_backups/`, `assets/`). Layers as on Linux: left Option = Hyprland SUPER, right Cmd = Hyprland ALT; left Cmd stays normal. Nothing is rewritten: each Karabiner manipulator matches the PHYSICAL modifiers (`left_option`, `right_command`, `shift`) and emits a unique combo that aerospace.toml binds: SUPER cmd-ctrl-alt, SUPER+SHIFT cmd-ctrl-alt-shift, ALT ctrl-alt-shift, SUPER+ALT cmd-ctrl-shift, SUPER+ALT+SHIFT cmd-alt-shift. Binds: SUPER+Return kitty, B Brave, E Finder, D Raycast (no AeroSpace bind; Raycast hotkey set to ⌃⌥⌘D in Raycast settings, so it toggles; left Cmd+Space sends the same combo), F fullscreen, V float, X tiles toggle, hjkl focus, SUPER+SHIFT+Q close; SUPER+ALT+hjkl move, SUPER+ALT+SHIFT+hjkl resize; ALT+a s d f q w e r z x c v = workspace 1-12, SUPER+ALT+letter = move window there (no follow, as Hyprland). Caps Lock = left Ctrl; right Shift tap = Esc (held = Shift; pass-through while right Cmd/left Option held). kitty only: right Cmd + h j k l i n o, arrows, - = [ ] -> plain Alt+key for zellij (not f: workspace key). Unbound combos stay normal (right Cmd+h = Hide app outside kitty). Skipped: scratchpad, monocle ws 9, pin, groups, launcher/Quickshell/screenshot/lock binds, Raycast (own hotkey). Needs Accessibility (AeroSpace) and Input Monitoring + driver extension (Karabiner). Reload: `aerospace reload-config`; Karabiner reloads on save. Caveats: lone right Shift tap sends Esc; right Shift pass-through manipulators are an unproven detail.
- **Caveats**: kitty logs "unknown config key tab_active_*" for `theme/out/kitty.conf` (generator emits non-kitty key names, also on Linux; harmless). nvim `:checkhealth` errors are optional tools (ghostscript, tectonic/pdflatex, mmdc, luarocks). Use `nvim --headless "+Lazy! restore" +qa` to install plugins at the locked versions (`Lazy! sync` rewrites `lazy-lock.json`; revert it with git). Mason installs (stylua, shfmt, shellcheck, prettier) need nvim kept open until done.
- **Trackpad workspace swipe (2026-10-23)**: SwipeAeroSpace (brew cask, mediosz tap; needs Accessibility) switches AeroSpace workspaces with a three-finger swipe. macOS three-finger horizontal swipe disabled per user (`TrackpadThreeFingerHorizSwipeGesture 0` in `com.apple.AppleMultitouchTrackpad` and `com.apple.driver.AppleBluetoothMultitouch.trackpad`, plus `defaults -currentHost write -g com.apple.trackpad.threeFingerHorizSwipeGesture -int 0`, which is the one that actually mattered, then `killall Dock`), four-finger swipe left on macOS (`...FourFinger... 2`). SwipeAeroSpace settings live in the app, not the repo.
- **Theme engine off on macOS (2026-10-23)**: `theme.py` exits 0 immediately on darwin and `.githooks/theme-render` skips there. The Mac uses the committed `config/theme/out/` files; a render could not work anyway (theme.toml's wallpaper lives in the Linux `~/.cache/theme/`, and the deploy steps target Linux apps).
- **Re-run**: `ln -s` the apps listed above (back up first), `brew install` the list, clone Oh My Zsh, recreate the `wl-copy` symlink.

## Per-OS / per-machine config split (Linux primary, macOS secondary)

Shared files stay identical on both machines; machine-specific edits go in their own file so merges stay clean.
- **kitty**: `config/kitty/kitty.conf` is shared (generic `font_family family="Paper Mono"`); `include ${KITTY_OS}.conf` pulls `linux.conf` (WC3 icon `symbol_map`) or `macos.conf` (option-as-alt, title bar, brew variable-font spec). `host.py` (geninclude) is for per-hostname overrides. If kitty's font kitten appends a `BEGIN_KITTY_FONTS` block to `kitty.conf`, move it into the OS file. `kitty.conf.bak` was a stale kitty backup, removed.
- **zsh**: `.zshrc` branches on `$OSTYPE`/file existence (brew, plugin dirs, Android SDK, `update()`); `$HOME` instead of absolute paths. Put machine-local lines (installer-appended PATH/completion snippets, per-host aliases) in git-ignored `~/.zshrc.local`, sourced just before zoxide. The `zellij` function falls back to the compact bar only when the claude-tabs plugin file is missing (Linux has it: plain passthrough).
- **Linux-only** (not linked on mac, `install.sh` never runs there): hypr, quickshell, theme engine runs, hyprpaper outputs, launcher. **mac-only**: `config/aerospace`, `config/karabiner` (`karabiner.json` generated by `generate.py`). `config/theme/out/*` is generated on Linux only; the Mac just reads the committed outputs, so no split needed (do not run the theme engine on the Mac).
- Verified on Linux (2026-10-24): `zsh -n`, interactive zsh alias/PATH diff vs before pull (only change: nonexistent `/opt/android-sdk` entries no longer added), kitty config loads via `kitty +runpy` with `linux.conf` applied, `zellij setup --check`. mac files only syntax-checked (toml/json/py_compile). Not verified: kitty font resolution after the font-block move on either OS (Linux lines are the pre-existing ones), anything mac runtime.

## Hyprland config (`config/hypr`)

- `theme.lua`: border 2px, rounding 0; `settings.lua`: gaps 0. (Settings → Appearance overrides: see **Appearance** above.)
- **Blur xray is off on every host (2026-10-19, user decision).** The non-battlestation `decoration.blur` block in `settings.lua` had `xray = true`; it was removed (default false), no other xray setting exists (only a commented example). Why: with xray a translucent floating window blurs the **wallpaper**, not the windows below it. The screenshot / record modal (`ModalWindow.qml`, card `Theme.bg` at 0.85 on non-glass hosts, `Theme.glass` correctly false on stock Hyprland 0.56.2) opens centred over the wallpaper's bright red band (wallhaven-8g8v11, vibrancy 0.1696 boosts it), so on CrossWorkstation the whole card read as red. It was not a glass / solid_glass style: nothing glass-only is used on a stock host. Verified (isolated quickshell + throwaway backdrop window, captures of the panel geometry only): xray on, the card ignored a grey backdrop and showed the red wallpaper glow at the edges (up to (43,21,25)); xray off, the card is uniform (26,27,28) over grey and (41,15,18) over a #e7313c backdrop, i.e. 15 % of what is really behind it. `hyprctl reload` → `getoption decoration:blur:xray` false, no config errors. Caveat: floating translucent windows and qs-* layers now blur the windows under them (more GPU work with many blurred surfaces; not measured), and the card still shows a faint tint of whatever is behind it; raise the non-glass alpha in `ModalWindow.qml` if that is too much.
- `scripts/side-pin.lua` (Super+Shift+P, 2026-10-19; was an inline `myPin` in binds.lua with fixed numbers): floats + pins the focused window into the right 866px strip (reserved by pip-orientation.lua) and tags it `side-pin` (tag survives reloads). Gaps: old box, 866x1416 at monitor x+width-880, y+12. While smart gaps apply on its monitor (`managed.smart_enabled` and at most one visible tiled window, same count as pip-reserve `smart_gaps_apply`, maximized counts; zero tiled = pinned window alone also counts) it is flush (x = right edge - 866, full monitor height) with `set_prop rounding 0`; otherwise rounding `unset`. Why: the smart-gaps window rules only match `float = false`, so a pinned window never got them. Refits on window open/close/kill/destroy/pin/move_to_workspace/fullscreen/update_rules and workspace.active/move_to_monitor/removed (subs in global `__side_pin_subs`); only dispatches when geometry differs. Unpinning (Super+P) drops the tag and restores rounding. Verified live (DP-2, throwaway kitty windows, `hyprctl clients -j` + `getprop rounding`): alone/one tiled -> at 2574,0 866x1440 rounding 0; second tiled opens -> 2560,12 866x1416 rounding 12; closing it -> flush again; unpin -> rounding 12, tag gone. Not verified: screenshot, Settings smart-gaps toggle (refit only on the next event), monitors other than 3440 wide (strip width 866 hard-coded, like pip-orientation). Test: launch `kitty --class test-pin-x`, `hyprctl dispatch '(function() require("scripts.side-pin").pin() return hl.dsp.exec_cmd("true") end)()'`, close by exact address.
- `rules.lua`: Twitch player (`org.quickshell` titled "Twitch - …"), PiP and mpv rules — pinned, 1588x893, placed bottom-right of the second monitor (with window gaps pip-reserve shrinks and moves the box, see **Pinned video box and gaps**).
- Workstation Twitch player: `scripts/pip-orientation.lua` treats it like PiP/mpv (`is_twitch`: class org.quickshell, title "Twitch - ..."). The title is empty at `window.open`, so it is placed once on the first `window.title` event (secondary monitor HDMI-A-1, 1200x675 bottom box, pinned, bottom strip reserved) and kept in place by the reserved-area clamp fix. Verified live: monitor 1, pinned, at -1200,945, reserved bottom 675. Pinned-window drift: activating a workspace on the other monitor drags a pinned window's workspace (then monitor) ownership along (reproduced with raw `focus({workspace=N})`, unfocused window; sequence Dell 5->6 while DP-2 goes 3, then DP-2 -> 4 moved it to DP-2). `heal_pinned()` in pip-orientation.lua runs on `workspace.active` for pinned PiP/mpv/Twitch windows (unpin, move to target monitor's active workspace, reposition, pin) and fixed the sequence. Not tested: mpv/PiP themselves (none open), but they share the same code path. The static `twitch-player` rule on this host (float only) is unchanged. **Unpinned after a stream switch (fixed 2026-10-19):** `hl.dsp.window.pin({action = true})` TOGGLES in 0.56.2 (second call on a pinned window unpins; measured with a throwaway kitty). The Twitch window can get its "Twitch - <login>" title already at open, so `window.open` placed+pinned it and the first `window.title` placed it again, and that second pin unpinned it (`pin>>addr,1` then `pin>>addr,0` in `.socket2`). The stream switch itself is not the cause: `twitch-qs-mpv watch` sends the running player an IPC call, same window, only the title changes (a plain title change keeps the pin, verified). Fix: `place()` in `scripts/pip-orientation.lua` pins only if the window is not pinned yet (looked up fresh via `hl.get_windows()`). Verified with a throwaway Quickshell FloatingWindow (scratchpad `t/t.qml`, titled at open, then retitled twice): stays pinned. Not verified with the real player / real Super+Shift+T switch; if it still unpins there, capture `.socket2` events (python socket, no socat) around the switch. Battlestation (pip-reserve.lua `heal`, static pin rule) untouched and untested for this.
- tuifin's mpv (2026-10-19): launched with `--wayland-app-id=tuifin-mpv --x11-name=tuifin-mpv --title=<media title> --input-ipc-server=...`, so `initial_class` is `tuifin-mpv`, not `mpv`; it matched neither the `cw-mpv` rule nor `is_pip_or_mpv` and only the static `tuifin-mpv` rule (fullscreen, tiled, workspace 11 = the TV media workspace) applied, so it never got placed/pinned on the workstation. Fix in `scripts/pip-orientation.lua`: `is_tuifin()`; `is_pip_or_mpv` includes it while the TV output is not connected (`tv_connected()`), and `place()` first unfullscreens + floats it (state-aware toggles). With the TV connected the fullscreen-on-ws-11 rule is left alone. Verified with a throwaway `mpv av://lavfi:testsrc` using the same flags: pinned, floating, fullscreen 0, HDMI-A-1 box 1201x676 at -1200,945. Not verified with the real tuifin or with the TV connected. Caveat: the TV check is at open time only; battlestation untouched.
- Flush vs margins for the pip-orientation boxes (2026-10-19, same rule as side-pin): `compute_target()` uses `side_pin.smart_applies(mon)` (exported from `scripts/side-pin.lua`, `exclude` arg = closing window; tiled windows counted like pip-reserve, groups count once). Smart gaps apply (<= 1 visible tiled window on the monitor's active workspace): box flush to its reserved area, rounding 0. Otherwise margins 12 (top/bottom of the right strip, left/bottom of the bottom box; 14 from the right edge) and `rounding` unset (= 12). `reposition()` sets the rounding prop; `refit_boxes()` (via `sync_and_fit`) re-fits pinned PiP/mpv/tuifin/Twitch boxes on open/close/kill/destroy/pin/move_to_workspace/fullscreen/workspace.active, only when geometry differs. ASSUMPTION: the user wrote 'have gaps' for the multi-window case; implemented as margins when more than one window, flush when alone (user's earlier side-pin wording was flush/no gaps alone). Verified (bottom box, throwaway tuifin-flag mpv + alacritty on ws 7): alone 1200x675 at -1200,945 rounding 0; with a 2nd tiled window 1200x663 at -1188,945 rounding 12; back to flush after closing it. Right strip and Twitch share the code but were not exercised. Note ws 7's messaging windows are one group, so it counts as a single tiled window.
- **Main-monitor lone-window strip and groups (fix 2026-10-21, user: "one group with two windows on my main display loses the reserved area"):** on the workstation the 16:9-ish lone window on the ultrawide is NOT a dwindle `single_window_aspect_ratio` (none is set anywhere): it is `pip-orientation.lua` `sync_monitor1_reserved_area()` reserving the right 866 px (`reserved_area.right`) while a pinned window is there or `count_tiled_on_visible_workspace() <= 1`. Root cause: that count added every non-floating window, so a lone group with 2 tabs counted 2 and the strip was dropped (group full width). Fix: count a group once (only `w.group.current`, same rule as `side_pin.smart_applies`), and resync on `window.update_rules`: grouping has no Lua event of its own (group.toggle, `group:add/remove`, move into/out of a group), but Hyprland re-evaluates rules on each such change (seen in a nested event log); it also fires on focus etc., `set_reserved` / `refit_boxes` are no-ops then. Ungrouped behaviour unchanged. **Verified (nested `/usr/bin/Hyprland` 0.56.2, headless `TEST` 3440x1440 as primary, stubbed host/monitors/side-pin, throwaway kitty):** 1 window 2572 wide, reserved right 866; group of 1 same; 2nd window auto-grouped: before fix reserved 0 and both 3438 wide, after fix 866 and 2572; Lua `remove` -> 0 (split), Lua `add` back (url-router path) -> 866; `group.next` keeps 866; dissolving the group -> 0; group of 1 + 1 ungrouped -> 0; closing back to 1 -> 866. **Not verified:** the real group on DP-2 (not touched); needs a `hyprctl reload` (or the next config reload) to load the edited script.
- `scripts/pip-reserve.lua` (battlestation only): reserves a right-hand strip on the second monitor while a pinned PiP/mpv/Twitch window lives there, heals windows that Hyprland drags to the other monitor (unpin → move → reposition → pin), follows the bar edge (`bottom_offset` = bar reserve from `managed.lua`, 40 docked / 40 + gap floating, with a bottom bar; 0 with a top bar), keeps the window gaps (gaps_out from the edge and the bar, `managed.edge_margins()`; 0 while smart gaps apply to a lone tiled window on the monitor), exposes `pip_bar_changed()`.
- `hyprland/managed.lua`: Settings → Appearance overrides and the smart-gaps rules, see **Appearance** above. Do not put hand-written settings there.
- `autostart.lua`: quickshell, `scripts/start-hyprpaper.sh` (waits for monitors, 3 s settle, single start), clears a stale empty `XDG_DESKTOP_PORTAL_DIR`.
- `env.lua` prepends `~/.local/bin` to PATH (shims: `brave`, `discord`).
- `binds.lua`: `SUPER+D` launcher, `SUPER+T` wallpaper picker (= Settings → Wallpaper & theme), `SUPER+SHIFT+T` Twitch menu, `SUPER+SHIFT+J` Jellyfin menu (launcher section jellyfin; mpv needs the `mpv-mpris` package for the bar now-playing widget), `SHIFT+Print` screen recording (stop / mode menu, see below), `Print` region screenshot, `CTRL+Print` screenshot menu (see below), brightness keys → `qs ipc call osd brightness`.
