- **Screenshot** (added 2026-10-18; since 2026-10-20 the launcher `screenshot` section, the old `ScreenshotMenu.qml` is gone): `Print` = `scripts/screenshot.sh region` directly (hyprshot replaced 2026-10-21, user request); `CTRL+Print` -> `scripts/screenshot-key.sh` -> `qs ipc call screenshot menu` (alias for `launcher.openSection("screenshot")`, toggle). Choosing runs `scripts/screenshot.sh region|window|output` (grim + slurp + wl-copy, all already installed). Region = `slurp -d`; Window = same slurp -r boxes as screenrec.sh; Screen = the only monitor, or with >1 monitors a `slurp -o -r -f %o` click on a monitor (differs from recording, which uses the focused monitor). Saves `~/Pictures/Screenshots/shot-YYYY-MM-DD_HH-MM-SS.png`, copies the PNG to the clipboard, notifies (click = nautilus --select). If the shell is down the key falls back to `screenshot.sh region`. `apps.screenshot` (hyprshot) is gone. Test overrides: `SHOT_GEOM`, `SHOT_OUTPUT`, `SHOT_DIR`, `SHOT_NOTIFY=0`, `SHOT_CLIP=0`, `QS_SHOT_SCRIPT`, `SHOT_KEY_SCRIPT`, `SHOT_KEY_QS`. Not verified: a real Print press, interactive slurp, wl-copy contents, notification click, multi-monitor `slurp -o`, the key fallback with the shell down. **A new QML type needs a `qmldir` line, otherwise the hot reload fails with "X is not a type" and the old shell keeps running.**
- **Screen recording** (`scripts/screenrec.sh`, wf-recorder + slurp; deps `wf-recorder` (installed, 0.6.0), `slurp`, `ffmpeg`, `libva-utils` (vainfo, for the probe), Mesa VA-API; a missing tool gives a "<tool> is missing" notification). Keys: `Print` = region screenshot, `CTRL+Print` = screenshot menu (see Screenshot); `SHIFT+Print` = the only recording key (the old SUPER/CTRL/ALT+SHIFT+Print binds were removed, user request) -> `scripts/screenrec-key.sh`: if `screenrec.sh status` says a recording runs it calls `screenrec.sh stop` (works with the shell down), else `qs ipc call record menu` opens the Quickshell mode menu (`RecordMenu.qml`, toggles); if that call fails (no shell: exit 255) or prints anything (`Target not found.`/`Function not found.` exit 0, e.g. after a failed reload) it falls back to `screenrec.sh region` (3 s timeout on the IPC call; overrides `SCREENREC_KEY_RECORDER`, `SCREENREC_KEY_QS`). Menu tiles: Region (`region`, slurp drag), Window (`window`, slurp -r over the windows on the visible workspaces + pinned ones), Screen (`output`, whole focused monitor); "Record desktop audio" switch adds `--audio` (`--audio=<default sink>.monitor`). Script: `screenrec.sh region|window|output [--fps 30|60] [--format mp4|webm] [--audio|--no-audio] | stop | status` (each start mode is still a toggle; `--fps=N` / `--format=X` also accepted; any other value or unknown option = usage on stderr, exit 2; 24 fps was offered briefly and dropped on user request). Options not given come from the menu's saved choices `${SCREENREC_PREFS:-$XDG_STATE_HOME/quickshell/screenrec.conf}` (so the keybind fallback `screenrec.sh region` with the shell down records with the menu's last fps/format/audio), else **60 fps, mp4, no audio**. That file is parsed line by line with whitelisted keys and values (`fps` 30|60, `format` mp4|webm, `audio` 0|1; CR stripped, max 4 KiB read, only regular files; never sourced): tested with missing file, no trailing newline, `$(...)`/`;`/backtick injection lines, 100 KB of binary garbage, a directory, a FIFO, a symlink to /dev/zero, stale `fps=24` -> all ignored / defaults. Frame rate = wf-recorder `-r N`, which adds an `fps=N` filter after `scale_vaapi` (constant frame rate; ffprobe r/avg_frame_rate = N, decoded frame count ≈ N × duration). **Before this change there was no `-r`: damage-driven variable frame rate up to the monitor refresh** (~69 fps average measured on the 360 Hz DP-1, r_frame_rate 90000/1), so 60 fps CFR is a behaviour change. Saves `~/Videos/Recordings/rec-YYYY-MM-DD_HH-MM-SS.<mp4|webm>` (XDG VIDEOS dir; screenshots go to ~/Pictures/Screenshots), W/H rounded down to even; boxes < 16px = cancelled click. **Encoder: GPU by default** (2026-10-18). The render node of the GPU driving the recorded monitor (output mode: that monitor; region/window: the monitor containing the box's top-left corner) is found via sysfs (`/sys/class/drm/card*-<connector>` -> `cardN/device/drm/renderD*`); here every monitor is on the RX 9070 XT = `/dev/dri/renderD128` (the Ryzen iGPU, renderD129, drives nothing). Encoder matrix: **mp4** = H.264 + AAC (`-C aac`): amdgpu/intel -> `-c h264_vaapi -d <node> -p rc_mode=CQP -p qp=24` (wf-recorder itself adds `scale_vaapi=format=nv12:out_range=full` and uses zero-copy DMA-BUF capture; files are tagged full range, yuvj420p); nvidia driver -> `-c h264_nvenc -p preset=p4 -p rc=vbr -p cq=24 -x yuv420p` (untested, no NVIDIA GPU here; `nvidia-utils` is installed but `nvidia-smi -L` lists nothing, so auto never picks it); CPU = `libx264 -p preset=veryfast -p crf=23 -x yuv420p`. **webm** = AV1 or VP9 + Opus (`-C libopus`, 48 kHz stereo 96 kb/s default): vaapi -> `-c av1_vaapi -d <node> -p rc_mode=CQP -p global_quality=100` (AV1 q index 0-255; the RX 9070 XT / Mesa lists `VAProfileAV1Profile0: VAEntrypointEncSlice`, VP9 is decode-only, so there is no vp9_vaapi path); nvidia -> `-c av1_nvenc -p preset=p4 -p rc=vbr -p cq=32 -x yuv420p` (untested; needs an AV1-capable NVENC, the runtime fallback covers older cards); CPU = `-c libvpx-vp9 -p deadline=realtime -p cpu-used=8 -p row-mt=1 -p crf=32 -p b=0 -x yuv420p`. Probe (ffmpeg encoder list + `vainfo --display drm --device <node>` H264 resp. AV1Profile0 EncSlice, or `nvidia-smi -L`) cached per method, codec and node as `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR/screenrec/probe-<method>-<h264|av1>-renderDxxx` (yes/no; delete it to re-probe; old `probe-<method>-renderDxxx` files are ignored). Small regions always use the CPU: h264 below 128 px on either side; AV1 below 640x360 (`AV1_MIN_W`/`AV1_MIN_H` = the smallest size tested on the GPU, not a known hardware limit; smaller webm regions get VP9 on the CPU, cheap at that size). AV1 alignment (fixed 2026-10-18, "green line on the right edge" of webm recordings): `av1_vaapi` on this RDNA4/Mesa codes the frame width padded up to a multiple of 8 and only puts the real width in the AV1 render size; the container (and `ffprobe -show_streams`) says e.g. 1252x1404, but dav1d/mpv/browsers decode 1256x1404 frames and the 4 padding columns are pure green (RGB ~0,135,0 = zero YUV). Height is coded exactly (1404, 1406, 722, 370 all fine). The earlier note "650x370 decoded fine" was wrong: it only checked the stream size; it decodes as 656x370. Check with `ffprobe -select_streams v -show_frames -read_intervals %+#1 -show_entries frame=width,height` (decoded size), not stream width. Fix in `screenrec.sh`: when the encoder is vaapi + av1, the width is rounded down to a multiple of 8 (region/window: in the geometry; output mode: only if the monitor's logical width is not a multiple of 8 it is recorded as an `-g` geometry of the monitor with the rounded width, otherwise `-o` as before). h264_vaapi is not affected (SPS cropping is honoured: 1252/1274 wide mp4s decode at that size), CPU VP9 neither. Verified 2026-10-18 with direct wf-recorder av1_vaapi runs (widths 1252/1250/1258/1270/650 -> decoded 1256/1256/1264/1272/656 with green columns; 1256/1264/1272/1280 exact) and via the script: region 1253x1405 webm -> 1248x1404 decoded 1248x1404, DP-1 output webm 2560x1440 unchanged, region mp4 still 1252x1404, output on a stubbed 1366x768 DP-3 (fake `hyprctl` in PATH) -> 1360x768. Not verified: fractional scaling (the rounding is on logical px; with scale != 1 the physical width may again be unaligned), av1_nvenc alignment, a real interactive slurp selection. h264 floor reason: on this RDNA4 card VA-API refuses 64x64 / 64x160 (avcodec_open2) and a 200x32 encode page-faulted VCN and forced a `vcn_unified_0` ring reset (recovered, compositor unaffected). If the GPU recorder exits during startup (up to 2 s, until the file is non-empty (mp4 header) or the log shows `Output #0` (+0.2 s still running; webm keeps its header in the write buffer, so the file stays empty for a while)), the script restarts it on the CPU encoder of the format (libx264 / libvpx-vp9), keeps the GPU log as `last-gpu.log` and, after the recording, sends "Recorded with the CPU encoder" with the error line (not at start, it would be in the video). Overrides: `SCREENREC_ENCODER=auto|cpu|vaapi|nvenc` (forced GPU skips the probe, still falls back), `SCREENREC_DEVICE=/dev/dri/renderDxxx`. Measured at 2560x1440 output, 5 s: vaapi 3% of one core vs libx264 490%; 1280x720: 1% vs 35% (h264, before `-r`); 2026-10-18 with `-r 60` on a mostly static screen: av1_vaapi 1440p ~0%, libvpx-vp9 1440p 38%, 720p 11%, libx264 720p30 16% (static content understates CPU encoders); the recorder's DRM fdinfo showed `drm-engine-enc` busy only for vaapi. State `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR/screenrec/state` (key=value pid, comm, wrapper, started, file, mode, audio, enc, codec, fps, format; `enc` = cpu|vaapi|nvenc, `codec` = h264|av1|vp9; the bar chip only uses pid/comm/started/audio, extra keys are ignored; `audio=0` when no default sink was found) written once the recorder runs; the starting script stays as the recorder's parent, waits for it, removes the state and sends "Recording saved" (`notify-send -w -A default=… -A play=Play -A folder="Open folder"`: body click (`default`) and the "Open folder" button both run `nautilus --new-window --select <file>`, "Play" = mpv; it waits up to 1 h for the answer). `NotifCard.qml` renders every non-default action as a button in a Flow, so no QML change was needed: toast and notification center both show "Play" and "Open folder" side by side (screenshots 2026-10-18 in an isolated instance on a private `dbus-run-session` bus, the only way to get notifications to a second server while the live shell owns `org.freedesktop.Notifications`); invoking each action from a test IPC made `notify-send -w` print `folder` / `play` / `default`. Stop = SIGINT to the exact pid from the state (checked against `/proc/<pid>/comm`, so stale/reused pids are ignored), TERM after 10 s; if the wrapper is gone, `stop` cleans up and notifies itself. A flock makes a second key press during slurp a no-op. No "started" notification on purpose (it would be in the video); the bar chip is the indicator. Test overrides: `REC_GEOM`, `REC_OUTPUT`, `SCREENREC_DIR`, `SCREENREC_RUNTIME_DIR`, `SCREENREC_PREFS`, `SCREENREC_RECORDER`, `SCREENREC_NOTIFY=0`. Probe pitfall: the script runs with `pipefail`, so `cmd | grep -q` fails when grep exits early (SIGPIPE); capture output first. Verified 2026-10-18 with wf-recorder 0.6.0 extracted to a scratch dir (official package, checksum matched the sync db): region (odd size → even), window/output modes, output + audio (h264 + aac), toggle stop, SIGKILLed wrapper, stale pidfile, missing tool, off-screen geometry; all files valid in ffprobe. GPU switch verified 2026-10-18 via the real script path: vaapi region 1280x720, odd 641x361 + audio (-> 640x360 h264 + aac), full 1440p output, toggle stop and `stop`, forced vaapi on a bogus node and forced nvenc (both fell back to CPU with the notice), auto with a bogus node (probe no -> CPU), tiny region forced vaapi (-> CPU); all h264 in ffprobe. fps/format verified 2026-10-18 via the script (REC_GEOM 1280x720 on DP-1, 3-4 s each, files deleted): {30,60} x {mp4,webm} x {audio, no audio} all GPU (h264_vaapi + aac / av1_vaapi + opus) with r_frame_rate and avg_frame_rate = the requested rate, forced CPU webm 720p + audio (vp9 + opus) and 1440p output, GPU 1440p output webm (av1), forced vaapi on a bogus node (-> vp9 with the notice, `vaapi av1 on /dev/dri/renderD999 failed: Failed to create hw encoding device`), auto with a bogus node (probe no -> vp9), 400x300 webm (-> vp9 CPU), odd 651x371 webm + audio (-> 650x370 av1 + opus), invalid values (`--fps 25/120/24/30x`, `--format mkv/MP4`, missing values, unknown option -> exit 2, nothing recorded), state-file defaults (no flags -> saved choices; explicit flags win), `screenrec-key.sh` with the shell "down" (qs = /bin/false) -> region with the saved webm/30, second key press -> stop. No amdgpu/VCN errors in `journalctl -k` during these tests. Not verified: interactive slurp selection, a real key press (also not for the SHIFT+Print menu flow; `screenrec-key.sh` was tested with stub recorder/qs: recording -> stop, menu ok -> nothing else, target missing / shell down / hanging qs -> region), a real click on the notification buttons, WebM playback in browsers, long recordings, recording while a game uses VCN/the GPU heavily, nvenc, using the iGPU node (cross-GPU DMA-BUF), regions spanning two monitors on different GPUs.

## Summary of what has been built (chronological highlights)

1. **Wallpaper-driven theming** of every app with live reload, hue-matched Adwaita icons, Brave seed theme, hyprlock wallpaper follows the wallpaper; `herdr` and `nautilus` configs moved into the repo.
2. **Quickshell replaced waybar, tofi and swaync**: bar, launcher, notification server + toasts, notification center, calendar, network, bluetooth, audio, media popups, volume OSD, wallpaper picker.
3. **Look:** by default a docked square bar, Hyprland rounding 0, gaps 0 and 2px borders (adjustable, item 15), blur-aware transparency, Adwaita icons globally, no Oxygen leftovers, portal-based Nautilus-style file dialogs in Brave.
4. **Twitch workflow:** separate project `~/code/crossatko/twitch-qs-mpv` (mpv wrapped in a Quickshell app with chat overlay; its own `AGENTS.md`), streamer menu on `SUPER+SHIFT+T`, matching Hyprland window rule, now-playing in the bar. One-time `bin/twitch-qs-mpv login` still needed.
5. **PiP/mpv layout:** reserved strip on the second monitor, healing of pinned windows, automatic alignment to the bar edge (top/bottom), keeps the Appearance window gaps (none while a lone tiled window has none).
6. **Notifications:** click focuses the app including Hyprland groups, right-click dismisses with a slide-out (toasts and control center), pop-in animation, toasts/OSD ignore the reserved area.
7. **Control center:** per-monitor bar settings (enable + top/bottom), toggles (Wi-Fi, Bluetooth, DND, sound), input/output device pickers, order flips with the bar edge, min height, snappy open/close animations.
8. **Network/Bluetooth panels:** disconnect individual wired/wireless connections, shared toggle component, wired/wireless section heads, inline Wi-Fi password form in the selected card, even spacing, icon/title gap of 14px.
9. **Calendar** with Thunderbird events above the grid (reversed for a top bar). Data: `CalPanel` runs `bin/calendar-events <first grid day> <+42 days>` (whole 6-week grid incl. past days; on open, month change and every 5 min). The helper copies `<profile>/calendar-data/cache.sqlite*` to a temp dir and reads it read-only (expands RRULEs with dateutil, skips CANCELLED and overridden occurrences); no network, no credentials. **Only calendars with "Offline support" enabled in Thunderbird have rows there.** All calendars are Google CalDAV (OAuth inside Thunderbird); `Candelabrador`, `Gym`, `Finiganbrothers` have `cache.enabled = false`, so their events (e.g. "S1 - Sklub" 2026-10-19, Gym 2026-10-02) never reach the popup — that was the "missing events" report (2026-10-18), not a date/timezone/recurrence bug (checked: window covers past days, all RRULEs parse, no duplicates). Fix is user-side: Thunderbird → right-click the calendar → Properties → tick "Offline support" (TB then syncs it into `cache.sqlite`; don't edit `prefs.js` while TB runs, it rewrites it). The popup footer names the uncached calendars ("Not shown: …"). Fetching Google CalDAV directly from the helper was ruled out (needs Thunderbird's OAuth token).
10. **Bar polish:** workspaces per monitor (all shown when only one bar), CPU/MEM sparklines with fixed widths, vertical alignment, state-dependent volume icon, brand logo for the playing media (Spotify/YouTube/Twitch/… via `mediaBrand`), highlighted chip while its popup is open.
11. **Font Awesome 7 Pro Sharp (Regular)** for all bar and popup icons; Brands font for media logos. Installed locally only.
12. **Tray:** own themed context menu with submenus, highlighted icon while open.
13. **Boot reliability:** hyprpaper race fixed, Discord autostart on its workspace restored.
14. **Home Assistant popup:** bar chip (lights-on count) + control popup for lights, climate, vacuum (rooms, passes, suction), fans, covers, switches, scenes; credentials in `config/hypr/.secrets`.
15. **Appearance settings:** corner radius, window gap / edge gap, border, docked or floating bar and smart gaps (per monitor), applied live to Hyprland and Quickshell with concentric inner radii (Settings → Appearance).

## Open items / ideas

- Twitch project README and end-to-end verification.
- Optional: Bibata cursors instead of Oxygen, brightness via `brightnessctl` (not installed), PiP rule parity for the workstation host.
- Mic chip in the bar (the mic icon currently exists only in the control center).
- Leak (found 2026-10-18, not fixed): the bar's CPU/MEM pollers (`Bar.qml`, `sh -c 'while :; do awk … /proc/meminfo; sleep 3; done'`) survive hot reloads and test-instance exits as orphans (ppid 1); ~45 of them had piled up. Fix idea: no endless shell loop (Timer + one-shot Process or FileView on /proc), then clean up the orphans by exact PID.

- PopupHost Esc (2026-10-18): Esc closes a popup via a Hyprland bind, not via popup keyboard focus (keyboardFocus stays OnDemand; Exclusive breaks ClickCatcher click-away and one-click chip switching, so never use it on PopupHost). `hypr/hyprland/binds.lua` binds `Escape` (non_consuming, no submap) to a Lua function that runs `qs ipc call popup esc` only when `hl.get_layers({namespace="qs-popup"})` is non-empty (the layer is mapped only while a popup is open/fading). `Popups.esc()` closes unless `Popups.escAt` was stamped <400 ms ago; NetPanel's password field stamps it in its own `Keys.onEscapePressed`, so Esc there only cancels the form. The `keys` Item in PopupHost still handles Esc when the popup itself has focus. Verified: config reloads with no errors, both Escape binds listed in `hyprctl binds`, `popup toggle cal` + `popup esc` over IPC closes it. NOT verified: a real Esc key press (no synthetic input allowed). Caveats: non_consuming means the focused window also receives that Esc; the password-field case relies on the client handling the key before the IPC arrives (~tens of ms race, should be fine).
- Window borders (2026-10-18): active = solid Quickshell accent (`theme.lua` `M.active.border_gradient` is a single stop, `M.selected = "accent"`), inactive = fully transparent (`0x00000000`); the old tinted-gray gradient was removed. Groupbar inactive tabs keep a faint `groupbar_inactive` so they stay visible. Verified via `hyprctl getoption` after reload (accent matches palette.json); not eyeballed.
- NotifCard urgency stripe (2026-10-18): flush 3px stripe when radius is 0; with rounded corners it is an inset pill (x 6, 12px top/bottom, fully rounded) so the curve no longer cuts it off. qmllint clean; not eyeballed.
- pip-reserve `smart_gaps_apply` (2026-10-18): a tab group counts as ONE tiled window (only its `group.current` member is counted), like Hyprland w[tv1]. Before, every member of a group (all report hidden=false) was counted, so a workspace with e.g. 4 grouped chat apps lost both the zero margin and the zero rounding for the pinned PiP/mpv/Twitch box. Verified: ws with 4 grouped windows + Twitch box: box flush to the right edge and bar, rounding 0 (hyprctl getprop + screenshot).
- Picker restyle (2026-10-18): the old `Menu.qml` picker moved to a `ModalMenu` card; the picker itself was removed 2026-10-20 (the Raycast-style launcher replaced it).

## Draggable modal windows (Settings, screenshot / record menus)

- **What changed:** `Settings.qml` (and, until 2026-10-20, `ModalWindow.qml` / the screenshot and record menus, since removed) is a Quickshell `FloatingWindow` toplevel titled `qs-settings`, no longer layer-shell overlays. `ModalMenu.qml` (layer shell) stays for `Launcher.qml` (its height follows the result list, so a fixed-size window did not fit). Hyprland: rule `qs-modal` (float, no border/rounding/shadow/dim) and `title = "negative:^qs-.*"` on the twitch-player rules in `hypr/hyprland/rules.lua` (same class `org.quickshell`, Quickshell has no app-id setting); `hypr/scripts/modal-windows.lua` (required from `hyprland.lua`) remembers positions and handles header double-click (see below).
- **Findings:** layer surfaces cannot be dragged: `bindm` / `hl.dsp.window.drag()` hit-tests windows only (`windowAt`), so SUPER+LMB over an overlay drags the window underneath and Qt never sees the press; emulating it in QML was not viable. Title matches work for Quickshell windows (title is set before rules run, `window.open` sees it). Position memory: on `window.close` the offset from the monitor origin is written to `${XDG_STATE_HOME:-~/.local/state}/quickshell/modal-windows` (`title<TAB>dx<TAB>dy`; a window closed centred deletes its entry), re-applied on `window.open` (clamped to the monitor it opens on = the focused one). Double-click: second SUPER+LMB press within 350 ms, via an extra non-consuming `hl.bind` (the real drag bind is untouched); no Hyprland double-click exists.
- **Verified:** isolated quickshell (scratchpad harness, symlinked config) on the live session: windows float, centred on the focused monitor, glass look kept, not pinned/moved by the twitch rule; remembered position survives close/reopen and reset-by-centring clears the file. `hyprctl configerrors` empty after reload.
- **Not verified (needs real input):** SUPER+LMB drag on these windows, header double-click reset.
- **Caveats:** they no longer close on click-outside, no longer register with `Popups` (click on another monitor / opening a bar popup leaves them open; opening one calls `Popups.closeAll()`), and are ordinary windows: they stay on their workspace, a fullscreen window hides them, and they open on the focused monitor (Settings opened from another monitor's popup is only sized for that monitor). Positions are only saved on close (not if Hyprland dies with one open). Menu.qml could follow if it gets a fixed size.

### Follow-ups (modal windows)

- **Settings is resizable and tileable:** `minimumSize` 760x480, no maximum; the card fills the window, so `togglefloating` and SUPER+RMB work (`qs-modal` only sets float at map). Qt keeps the last size of a hidden window (e.g. the tiled one), so `Settings.open()` pins min = max = default for 150 ms (`pinSize`) to open at the default size. The Screenshot / Record menus stay fixed-size. `modal-windows.lua` remembers position only while floating (closed tiled = keeps the last floating entry) and, when the user resized, the size too (`title<TAB>dx<TAB>dy[<TAB>w<TAB>h]`); the saved size is applied 220 ms after open (after the pin), so a window with a saved size flashes at the default size first. The header double-click also restores the default size. Verified with hyprctl dispatches (resize, togglefloating, close, reopen) and screenshots; real SUPER+RMB resize not tried.
- **Reset by double-click (replaced SUPER+double-click):** SUPER+LMB cannot be observed from Lua (the `bindm` drag claims the press and Hyprland blocks every other bind on that chord until release: `shadowBinds()` / `canInvokeNow()` in `src/keybinds/Manager.cpp`; three Lua attempts logged nothing useful). Instead a plain double-click on the header (Settings title row; `ModalReset.qml`, a MouseArea behind the buttons) runs `hyprctl dispatch '(function() qs_modal_reset("<title>") ... end)()'`, i.e. the global `qs_modal_reset(title)` in `scripts/modal-windows.lua`: centre on its monitor, back to the opening size, saved position forgotten; ignored while tiled. Verified by running that dispatch on moved/resized windows and by screenshots; the double-click itself needs real input. The TEMP logging and the release bind are gone.
- **Scratchpad terminal:** SUPER+R (`binds.lua`, `toggle_scratchpad`): spawns `kitty --class scratchpad` if no window of that class exists, else `toggle_special("scratchpad")`. Rule `scratchpad-terminal` (`rules.lua`): float, centred, 60% x 60%, `workspace = "special:scratchpad"`; shown/hidden with the `specialWorkspace` animation set to `fade` in `animations.lua` (was `settings.lua`) (workspace animations only accept slide/fade styles, `popin` gives "unknown style"; a real pop-in would mean closing/reopening the window, e.g. kitty attaching to a zellij session — user chose fade) (a non-silent workspace rule shows the special workspace when the window opens). SUPER+TAB stays `group.next` (user uses it). Verified by dispatching the spawn and toggles and a screenshot; closing the kitty (exit) makes the next press spawn a new one.

- **Corners:** the `qs-modal` rule must NOT set `rounding = 0`: a window's glass (solid_glass) is cut to the window's own rounded box, not to the card's blur region, so a square window showed square dark corners outside the card's rounded border. The window now keeps the global rounding (= the Appearance radius = `Theme.radius`, power 3), which lines up with the card. Checked floating and tiled for Settings and floating for the Screenshot menu (zoomed crops in the scratchpad, `k_all.png`, `t_tiled_corners.png`). A tiled single window on a monitor with smart gaps off gets `rounding 0` from the managed smart-gaps rules, so its corners can still look square there (not checked).

## Firefox translucent header (2026-10-19)

- **What changed:** `config/firefox/userChrome.css` + `config/firefox/user.js`, symlinked by `install.sh` into the install-default profile (`zgxo9dkp.default-release`, found via profiles.ini; also linked by hand). Prefs: `toolkit.legacyUserProfileCustomizations.stylesheets`, `widget.wayland.opaque-region.enabled=false`, `widget.transparent-windows=true`, `browser.theme.toolbar-theme=0`. CSS sets the real 158 variables `--toolbox-background-color(-inactive)` (`--glass-tint`, transparent: tab strip untinted), `--toolbar-background-color` transparent, `#nav-bar` (address bar row) 5% white via `--navbar-tint`, `--toolbar-field-background-color(-focus)`, `--tab-background-color-selected` (red tint), plus content forced opaque. Tabs: `.tab-background` radius = `--window-radius` (12px, hard-coded copy of the Appearance radius) minus `--tab-edge-gap` (6px: measured gap above the tabs, tab margin 4 + strip 2); `#TabsToolbar` gets 4px inline-start padding so the first tab is also 6px from the left edge (was 2px). Checked with a throwaway profile + grim crop: tab corner concentric with the window border. Not synced: update `--window-radius` when the radius changes. No Hyprland change.
- **Targeted:** Firefox 158. Every variable and id was grepped in that build's omni.ja (browser.xhtml, browser-colors.css, browser-shared.css, tab.tokens.css). Dead pre-redesign names removed (`--toolbar-bgcolor`, `--lwt-accent-color`, `#titlebar`, `#appcontent`, `#urlbar-background`).
- **Verified:** throwaway `--no-remote` profile, floating Firefox over a floating kitty with `background=#ffcc00`: header pixels go from (5,5,5) to (19,16,5)/(23,19,4) with the yellow window behind, so the header is translucent and blurred (solid_glass darkens heavily, so the shift is faint; crops in scratchpad `firefox/w_before.png`, `w_after.png`, `w_cmp.png` levels-stretched).
- **Caveats:** restart Firefox needed; real profile not launched. Not checked: menus/popups, private windows, fullscreen, light theme. Selectors may break on Firefox updates.
- **Resume:** edit the CSS in the repo, restart Firefox. Test with `firefox --no-remote --profile <scratch dir with user.js and chrome/userChrome.css>`.

## GTK4 / libadwaita glass (Nautilus etc., 2026-10-21)

- **What:** libadwaita apps get translucent backgrounds with the compositor glass behind them, matching the shell's glass cards. `~/.config/gtk-4.0/gtk.css` links to `theme/out/host/gtk4.css` (rendered from `theme/templates/gtk4.css`, link set in `theme.py deploy()`), which `@import`s the shared `out/gtk.css` and overrides the libadwaita named colours with alpha. GTK3 user CSS: see **Other toolkits** below; the named `Wall` theme (Brave dialogs, Chromium) stays opaque. No Hyprland or Quickshell change.
- **Mapping to Settings > Glass (user request: follow tint / darkening):** the compositor already applies tint, darkening, saturation, refraction to the glass behind GTK windows with the same shader and values as behind the shell's cards. Measured with an isolated test card (layer `qs-adwtest`, same blur-region mechanism, fill bg at 0.3) above a GTK window with the same fill over the same backdrop: identical pixels ((2,65,3) vs (1,64,2), (76,71,2) vs (77,72,3)); `qs ipc call glasstune set darkening 0.5` darkened both equally ((2,47,3) vs (1,46,2)) with no CSS change. So the CSS must NOT repeat tint / darkening (that would apply them twice). It paints only the shell's card fill: window base = bg at `Theme.cardFill` (0.3 when `decoration:blur:variant` is solid_glass; `theme.py hypr_solid_glass()` uses the same test as Theme.qml, keep both in sync). Over plain blur the base is `bar_opacity` (0.55), NOT the shell's `bar_opacity + 0.3` card fill (see the stock-Hyprland entry under Other toolkits); `theme.toml gtk_glass_fill` overrides the base on any host. Per-part `theme.toml` offsets are added to that: `gtk_glass_{chrome 0, header .05, sidebar .05, view .15, popover .5, dialog .55}` (effective opacity = base fill + offset; stacked layers get `1 - (1 - eff) / (1 - chrome)`), `gtk_glass_control` 0.07 = fg fill of Nautilus' path bar, entries in header / tool / search bars and the window-control circles (the shell's Btn fill; hover x2). `gtk_glass` false = opaque, no blur request.
- **Live update:** tint / darkening / every other glass slider reaches RUNNING GTK apps immediately, through the compositor (verified: a running test Nautilus followed a tint change). No regeneration hook on glass.json is needed. The CSS only changes with the palette, `theme.toml`, or the blur variant (glass on/off); then re-render (`theme.py apply`) and restart the app: GTK4 does not watch the user gtk.css (theme.py's color-scheme flip makes it re-read for some apps; not tested for this).
- **Findings:** (1) libadwaita 1.10 / GTK 4.24.1: `:root { --window-bg-color: @window_bg_color }`, so `@define-color` overrides work. Do NOT also set the `--*-bg-color` variables at user priority: ghostty sets `:root --window-bg-color` itself. (2) **Blur needs `backdrop-filter`:** GTK >= 4.22 creates an `ext-background-effect-v1` object for every surface and leaves the blur region empty; Hyprland treats that as "no blur" (`CWindow::shouldBlur`, `m_hasBackgroundEffect`). `window { backdrop-filter: blur(12px) }` and `popover > contents {...}` make GTK send the window box / rounded popover box (WAYLAND_DEBUG: `set_blur_region`). (3) NautilusWindow carries `.view` (whole window = view colour, sidebar on top): `window.view` gets the chrome fill, Nautilus' file views (`.nautilus-grid-view`, `-list-view`, `-network-view`, `placesview`) get the view layer. (4) User CSS outranks the theme regardless of selector specificity: a state-less rule also overrides `:hover` etc., so every state that changes a fill must be listed.
- **Covered:** every GTK4 app: Nautilus, Calculator, Papers, Zenity, Epiphany, Bottles, Kooha, LACT, the GNOME file-chooser portal, pavucontrol / geeqie (plain GTK4), ghostty (gets glass like kitty; its `background-opacity` is unchanged).
- **Verified (2026-10-21):** isolated instances only (`XDG_CONFIG_HOME` scratch dir without bookmarks; Nautilus under `dbus-run-session` so it cannot attach to a running user instance: a plain `nautilus --new-window` DOES open its window inside the user's process), floating, no focus, over an opaque mpv `testsrc2` backdrop window, grim crops of the test area only. Checked: Nautilus grid next to the test card, Calculator, Zenity, a PyGObject probe (headerbar, boxed list, text view, popover, AdwAlertDialog), ghostty; a tint change through `glasstune` IPC, restored, glass.json byte-identical.
- **Caveats / not verified:** Nautilus list view, the file-chooser portal, Papers, Epiphany, light colour scheme, tiled windows, performance of the glass pass per GTK window. Linked / non-flat buttons outside the listed selectors keep libadwaita's currentColor fills. `backdrop-filter` needs GTK >= 4.22. A blur-variant switch needs a re-render. `render_all()` once rewrote `out/hyprpaper.conf` with `~` TV-wallpaper paths (pre-existing, reverted). `out/host/` (gtk3/gtk4 CSS, kvantum.kvconfig, brave-glass.css) is git-ignored and rendered per host (see **Stock Hyprland**).
- **Resume:** edit `theme/templates/gtk4.css` / `theme.toml`, re-render (`theme.py apply`), restart the GTK4 app. Test recipe: mpv `--wayland-app-id=<test id> av://lavfi:testsrc2` backdrop; `dbus-run-session -- env XDG_CONFIG_HOME=<scratch> nautilus --new-window /usr/share` via `hl.dsp.exec_cmd(cmd, {float=true, monitor=..., no_initial_focus=true, no_dim=true})`; place with `hl.dsp.window.move/resize({x=,y=, window="address:..."})`, picking the window by exact pid; reference card: a `quickshell -n -p <scratch>` PanelWindow (namespace `qs-...`, `BackgroundEffect.blurRegion: Region { item: card; radius: 12 }`, fill bg at 0.3).

### Other toolkits (GTK3, Qt / KDE, Brave; 2026-10-21)

- **GTK3: done.** `~/.config/gtk-3.0/gtk.css` -> `theme/out/host/gtk3.css` (template `templates/gtk3.css`, link in `theme.py deploy()`; imports the opaque `out/gtk.css`). Same `gtk_a_*` values as GTK4 (card fill + offsets), plus header-bar entries / raised buttons in the shell's button fill. GTK3 has no background-effect protocol, so Hyprland blurs/glasses any GTK3 window with alpha by default (like kitty): no window rule. GTK3's Adwaita inlines its colours, so the template uses explicit selectors, not `@define-color`.
  - **Scope = `window.csd` and descendants only.** Gecko (Firefox, Thunderbird) reads its system colours from unrealized / orphan style contexts without `.csd`, so it is excluded: a throwaway Firefox profile with the repo's user.js + userChrome.css rendered pixel-identical before / after (only the animated backdrop differed). Chromium / Brave / Electron never read the user gtk.css (named theme `Wall` stays opaque). Menus (`window.popup`) stay opaque.
  - **Limit:** GTK3 windows WITHOUT a header bar are server-side decorated on Hyprland and carry no `.csd` class (no "ssd" class exists to select them), so they stay opaque (e.g. nwg-look). Verified translucent: a PyGObject GTK3 probe and Evince (document page stays opaque).
- **Qt / KDE: done with Kvantum (installed 2026-10-21: kvantum 1.1.8, kvantum-qt5).** A palette colour with alpha alone stays opaque (Qt's Wayland backing store needs `WA_TranslucentBackground`, measured with a PyQt6 probe); Kvantum's `translucent_windows` sets it. `theme.py write_kvantum_theme()` (run by deploy) writes `~/.config/Kvantum/Wall/` = KvFlat's SVG + kvconfig recoloured to the palette (`kv_recolor`: greys by lightness onto bg..fg, KvFlat's blue onto the accent) with `out/host/kvantum.kvconfig` (template `templates/kvantum.kvconfig`) on top: `reduce_window_opacity` = 100 - card fill (70), `reduce_menu_opacity` from `gtk_glass_popover`, views `base.color` at the GTK view layer alpha (Qt `#AARRGGBB`), Kvantum blur off (Hyprland draws the glass, no window rule: alpha windows are blurred by default), `opaque=` video apps (vlc, obs, kdenlive, ...) plus quickshell / qs / qrenderdoc. `~/.config/Kvantum/kvantum.kvconfig` selects Wall. Style switch: `theme.toml qt_style = "kvantum"` -> kdeglobals `[KDE] widgetStyle` (what `QT_QPA_PLATFORMTHEME=kde` honours) and qt6ct `style=`; no `QT_STYLE_OVERRIDE`, env.lua unchanged; `"Fusion"` reverts. Verified isolated (`XDG_CONFIG_HOME` copy with kdeglobals widgetStyle=kvantum, own D-Bus): Dolphin on /usr/share and Ark translucent with glass, next to Nautilus and the shell test card; a test quickshell with `QT_STYLE_OVERRIDE=kvantum` rendered unchanged (the shell uses no QtQuick.Controls / palette). Limits: KvFlat's SVG frames (text edits, push buttons) stay opaque (grey push buttons, not the shell's Btn fill); Qt5 apps not checked; the running shell / apps pick the style up on restart. Backups of kdeglobals / qt6ct.conf from before the switch: scratchpad only.
  - **Design match: libadwaita is the design source (user, 2026-10-21).** Qt (Kvantum) and GTK3 copy libadwaita's MEASURED metrics (allocations dumped by a libadwaita 1.10 gallery with our gtk4.css): header bar 46-47 px, header / tool buttons 34x34 flat (hover fg 7 %, pressed 16 %), 6 px between them, 16 px symbolic icons; text buttons 34 px high, 17 px side padding, bold, fg 10 % (hover 15 %, pressed / checked 30 %), 9 px corners; entries / combos / spin boxes 34 px (spin = [value][-][+]); sidebar rows 36 px + 2 px gap, inset 6 px, 6 px corners, selection fg 10 %, icon 16 px with 12 px to the label; check / radio 20 px with a 2 px fg 15 % ring, checked = accent + accent_fg (= palette bg) mark, 6 px to the label; switch 46x26, knob 20; scale trough 4 px, knob 20; progress 8 px; tabs 34 px pills (selected fg 10 %); popover menu: 12 px corners, 6 px padding, items 32 px with 6 px corners and 12 px text inset, separator 1 px + 6 px margins; boxed-list rows 50-57 px. Files: `kvantum_svg.py` (ALPHA fills, shapes), `templates/kvantum.kvconfig` (geometry), `templates/gtk3.css` (GTK3, all scoped to `window.csd`).
  - **Kvantum findings (source read: tsujan/Kvantum style/*.cpp):** push buttons get +4 px over frames + text (so 34 px needs frame.top/bottom 6, entries 7); round 9 px corners on those come from `frame.expansion=18` plus `expand-<element>` SVG twins (`ebox()` in kvantum_svg; theme.py sets `frame.expansion=0` for every other inheriting section). A default push button is drawn as an OVERLAY with status `default` (`button-default-*` frame / interior, accent) and `button-default-indicator` (empty); its label colour cannot be set separately (stays fg). Item views: frames AND text margins are capped at 6 px (ThemeConfig.cpp), so rows cannot be inset like libadwaita's sidebar; unfocused windows request `itemview-<state>-inactive` (generated, else Kvantum falls back to a square palette fill). QtSvg leaves fully transparent shapes out of an element's bounds (spacer rects: opacity 0.001). Spin `indicator.element=spin` -> `spin-up/down-*` drawn as +/-; menu separator = `menuitem-separator`; `[Focus] frame=false` removes dashed focus rects; menus: padding 6 + `frame.expansion=24` (`expand-menu-normal`); combo popups `hide_combo_checkboxes`.
  - **Qt icons (`theme.toml qt_symbolic_icons`, default true):** `theme.py gen_qt_icons()` (called by gen_icons on `apply`) writes `~/.local/share/icons/Wall-Adwaita-Qt`, used by Qt / KDE only (kdeglobals `[Icons] Theme`, qt5ct / qt6ct `icon_theme`; GTK keeps Wall-Adwaita): Adwaita symbolic actions / ui / legacy under their plain AND -symbolic names, KDE aliases (`QT_ICON_ALIASES`: application-menu -> open-menu, view-list-icons -> view-grid, ...), Breeze Dark's monochrome actions/16 for every other name (recoloured), symbolic places / devices as a Scalable 16-24 px dir listed before the colour dirs, then symlinks to Wall-Adwaita's colour dirs; index.theme sets MainToolbar/Toolbar/Small defaults 16 and FollowsColorScheme. `deploy() -> apply_qt_layout()` writes kdeglobals `[MainToolbarIcons]` / `[ToolbarIcons] Size=16`, `[Toolbar style] ToolButtonStyle(OtherToolbars)=NoText` (libadwaita header bars are icon-only) and dolphinrc `[PlacesPanel] IconSize=16`. Per app (verified nested): Dolphin toolbar = Adwaita symbolic 16 px (back / forward / view mode / split / search / menu), places panel symbolic 16 px; Ark toolbar monochrome (Breeze Dark fallbacks: archive-* names have no Adwaita equivalent), icon-only. Trade-off: one name serves sidebar and view, so Dolphin's details view at <= 24 px also shows symbolic folders.
  - **GTK3:** header bar buttons flat 34 px (rules carry a `:not(.x-specificity)` chain to outrank the content-button fill), window controls 24 px circles with the control fill, content buttons / entries as above, `.sidebar` / `placessidebar` / `stacksidebar` / `.navigation-sidebar` rows 36 px inset 6 px, CSS check / radio / switch / scale / progress shapes, notebook tabs as pills, `list.frame` as cards, popover menus. Resets keep tree-view column headers / tab close buttons small. Verified nested: a GTK3 gallery (same widgets) and Evince.
  - **Verified (2026-10-21, nested Hyprland only, images in the session scratchpad):** galleries side by side (libadwaita / Qt / GTK3), Qt QMenu and QComboBox popups (they only map after the nested pointer has entered the window: `hl.dsp.cursor.move` in the NESTED instance before `showMenu()`), Dolphin vs Nautilus, Ark, Evince. Measured Qt: toolbar 46, tool buttons 34x34, buttons / entries / combo / spin 34, check 20, rows 39 (Kvantum layout, libadwaita 38). **Not matched / limits:** Qt icon-only QPushButtons stay 52 px wide (text margins apply without text), default-button label stays fg on accent, no switch widget in Qt, Dolphin's places panel / breadcrumb / status bubble draw their own geometry (icon 6 px from the edge, not 14), Qt list rows are not inset, boxed-list cards only for QGroupBox, Qt text views keep a darker base layer. Not verified: hover / pressed states (no pointer clicks), light colour scheme, Qt5 apps, GTK3 apps other than Evince, the running user session (apps pick the theme up on restart).
  - **Test harness (nested):** scratchpad `design-match/`: `up.sh` / `down.sh` (nested Hyprland on DP-3 at 40,40, 1250x1000, own config copy with autostart stubbed and `dm.lua`: monitor scale 1 + a float rule for `dm.*` / KDE classes), `run.sh` / `app.sh NAME PNG CMD` (app inside the nested socket, own D-Bus, isolated XDG dirs; refuses wayland-1), `shot.sh` (grim on the nested socket), `tools/render.py` (renders the templates + Kvantum into the scratch config, not the live one), `tools/{adw,qt,gtk3}_gallery.py` (dump allocations / Qt pixel metrics as JSON). The first nested launch sometimes never gets an xdg configure from the live compositor (window never maps); down + up again fixed it.
- **Brave header: tab strip feasible in GTK mode, toolbar row not (corrected 2026-10-21).** Brave 155 in GTK mode (`extensions.theme.system_theme: 1`, throwaway `--user-data-dir`, `GTK_THEME=<test theme>:dark`) paints the TAB STRIP / frame with the GTK `headerbar` / `.titlebar` background INCLUDING its alpha, and Hyprland puts the window glass behind it (no rule needed; pixels match the shell card over the same stripe). The first test looked opaque because the test theme set `headerbar` without `:backdrop`: Wall's more specific `headerbar:backdrop` won while the window was unfocused (the user saw it translucent while it was focused). With `headerbar, headerbar:backdrop, .titlebar, .titlebar:backdrop` alpha it stays glass focused and unfocused. The toolbar / omnibox row stays opaque whatever the theme sets (`window.background`, `toolbar`, `.toolbar` alpha are dropped; Chromium keeps the toolbar opaque). Recorded with wf-recorder: opaque for ~2.7 s until the GTK theme applies. **Applied to the real Brave (2026-10-21, user approved):** `theme.toml brave_mode = "gtk"` (default now; `"seed"` = the old seed-colour theme). `templates/brave-glass.css` -> `out/host/brave-glass.css` (header bars incl. `:backdrop`, bg at base fill + `gtk_glass_header` = `gtk_eff_header`; needs `browser.custom_chrome_frame = true`, see the stock-Hyprland entry below); `write_gtk_theme()` writes `~/.local/share/themes/Wall-Glass` (= Wall + that file); the `bin/brave` shim exports `GTK_THEME=Wall-Glass:dark` when `brave_mode = "gtk"` and the theme exists, else `Wall:dark`; `apply_brave()` (run by the shim only while Brave is closed) writes `extensions.theme.system_theme = 1` in GTK mode (no seed colours), `0` + seed in seed mode. Written once on 2026-10-21 while Brave was closed (backup of `Default/Preferences` kept outside the repo). Turn off: `brave_mode = "seed"`, close Brave, start it through the shim (or `theme.py brave`). Not yet checked on the real profile by the user. **Tab / toolbar colours in GTK mode (2026-10-21, measured with a distinct test colour per GTK node, throwaway profiles):** `window.background` background = toolbar row AND active tab (alpha dropped: solid `bg`); `label` colour = active tab title AND toolbar icons (one colour, `theme.toml brave_tab_text` = "fg" default or "accent"; Chromium mutes it while the window is unfocused); `.titlebar label` / `headerbar label` = inactive tab titles (`fg_dim`); `.view` / `textview text` background = omnibox pill (`surface` ~ bg + 7% fg); GtkEntry is NOT the omnibox. All set with `:backdrop` too, in `templates/brave-glass.css`. The active tab title alone cannot be the accent through GTK, and a theme extension would switch GTK mode (and the glass) off. Leftover seed prefs (`browser.theme.color_scheme2` etc.) did not change these colours in a test. **Omnibox focus ring (2026-10-21):** NOT from GTK: selection / `*:selected`, `@theme_selected_bg_color`, `entry:focus` border / outline, `*:focus` outline and `.suggested-action` all left it at Brave's blue #5b67e8 (focused throwaway window on brave://newtab). It comes from Chromium's seed colour pipeline, which still runs in GTK mode while toolbar / tab colours stay GTK: `browser.theme.user_color2` = accent with `color_variant2` 3 (vibrant) gives a red ring (#ee002e for #e7313c; variant 2 = grey-red, 1 = muted red), and also tints Brave UI accents (info-bar buttons, links, NTP search box). Code path: `theme.toml brave_gtk_seed` (palette key, "" = off, the default until the user approves the extra Preferences keys) + `brave_gtk_variant`; `apply_brave()` writes them in GTK mode. Focus tests: `focus_cap.py` lifts the `no_focus` prop, focuses the test window ~1.2 s, captures, focuses the previous window again. Launch tests WITHOUT `dbus-run-session` (with it, the exec rule missed and the window tiled on the focused workspace) and with `no_focus` (Brave activates itself and took focus once).
- **Hyprland 0.56 fork, branch more-blur-rebased-20261021** (`layer_alpha_ref` 0.3): GTK4 / GTK3 windows still get the window glass (edge refraction, matching the shell test card); `layer_alpha_ref` only affects layer surfaces.
- **Stock-Hyprland workstation: Brave strip pitch black, Nautilus near-opaque (2026-10-21, fixed).** Two separate causes, both reproduced and verified in a nested `/usr/bin/Hyprland` 0.56.2 (live blur values: size 4, passes 2, vibrancy 0.1696, new_optimizations; headless output `TEST`, `grim -o TEST` on the nested socket, opaque checkerboard GTK backdrop, throwaway Brave `--user-data-dir`s and a `dbus-run-session` Nautilus with scratch HOME / XDG_CONFIG_HOME / `GSETTINGS_BACKEND=memory` / `ADW_DEBUG_COLOR_SCHEME=prefer-dark`). (1) **Brave:** the real profile had `browser.custom_chrome_frame: false` ("Use system title bar and borders"). Then Chromium makes the window surface opaque and the strip shows the headerbar rgb solid (measured exactly bg (8,9,11) at alpha 0.55); same profile with `custom_chrome_frame: true` = blurred backdrop through the strip. The alpha was NOT the cause (the earlier lowering to `bar_opacity` did nothing). Fix: `apply_brave()` in GTK mode also writes `browser.custom_chrome_frame = true` (idempotent, tested on a copy of a profile). Side effect: Chromium draws its own window buttons in the strip, here only close (`button-layout` 'appmenu:close'). The running Brave's `/proc/<pid>/environ` is useless (Chromium overwrites it for the process title), and `brave` from Hyprland exec resolves to the shim (checked via an exec'd env dump). (2) **Nautilus / libadwaita:** the base fill on plain blur was the shell card fill `bar_opacity + 0.3` = 0.85, so the file view (+0.15) was fully opaque (`view_bg_color` alpha 1.000) and sidebar / header 0.9. Now the plain-blur base is `bar_opacity` (window 0.55, header / sidebar 0.60, view 0.70 effective; popover / dialog still clamp to 1.0), `theme.toml gtk_glass_fill` overrides it; solid_glass hosts unchanged (0.3; checked: same per-layer alphas as before). Same base feeds GTK3 CSD and Kvantum (`reduce_window_opacity` 15 -> 45 on stock). Brave's strip alpha is `gtk_eff_header` again (base + `gtk_glass_header` = 0.60 on stock, 0.35 on glass), `theme.toml brave_glass_alpha` overrides. Nested screenshots: Nautilus 0.85 (view opaque) vs new (blurred backdrop through grid, sidebar and header, text readable). **Caveats:** `decoration:dim_inactive` (0.5) darkens unfocused windows incl. the glass; over a dark wallpaper 0.55-0.70 still looks dark (tune `gtk_glass_fill` / offsets). Not verified on the user's real windows (not touched). **Resume:** the user must quit Brave fully and start it via the shim (writes the pref while closed), and `nautilus -q` + reopen. Harness notes: put the nested window on a hidden special workspace with a host rule on class `aquamarine`; the nested socket path must be short (symlink the runtime dir into /tmp).
- **Brave toolbar top corners (2026-10-21, open, no change made):** the small coloured triangles at both ends of the toolbar's top edge are NOT the glass tab strip and NOT Hyprland. Chromium paints the horizontal-tab-strip toolbar with `CustomCornersBackground` corners of type `kRoundedWithBackground` (hard-coded in `browser_view_tabbed_layout_impl.cc`; no flag), filled with the FRAME colour (`kColorFrameActive` / inactive). The transparent variant only exists for Chromium's glass frame, which is macOS 26 only. In GTK mode that frame colour does NOT come from GTK: an opaque magenta `headerbar`, `window.background` or `*` left it unchanged, while the GTK headerbar area ends 1 px above the corners. It comes from the seed pipeline: #37160B with `brave_gtk_seed = "accent"` (variant 3; 0/1 #311918, 2 #291C1C, 4 #311A00), Brave's #1F1F23 with no seed, #000 with `brave.darker_mode` (but then the toolbar is forced to lightness ~7.5 % (#101215), which no longer matches the GTK-coloured active tab, and the omnibox turns black). Same tiled, floating and maximized; `BraveHorizontalTabsUpdate` off keeps it. Measured in a nested Hyprland with a solid green wallpaper and throwaway profiles. The only fixes are user choices: no seed (grey corners, blue focus ring), or a toolbar colour equal to the frame colour. Seed = toolbar bg #08090B does NOT work either: the frame is a fixed dark tone of the seed's hue (v0/1 #15202F, v2 #1C2026, v3 #121F3B, v4 #241A39; even #000000 gives #092327 / #182123), and the focus ring follows the hidden hue (blue / grey / teal). Toolbar, active tab and omnibox (GTK) stay the same with any seed.
- **Test harness lesson:** the active workspace on DP-3 can change under you (the user switches); a plain `grim -g` then captures their windows. Capture only through a guard that checks the active workspace, moves the test windows there, checks that the backdrop covers the region and no foreign floating window / overlay layer overlaps, and re-checks after the shot. Brave launched through `dbus-run-session` ignored the exec rule (tiled on the focused workspace): float + move it by exact pid right away.

## Wallpaper on a host with other monitors (2026-10-19)
- `templates/hyprpaper.conf` matched monitors by `desc:` of the dev machine's displays plus `HDMI-A-1` = TV (`tv_wallpaper`, nier.jpg, not in the repo). On the work machine (DP-2 Philips, HDMI-A-1 Dell) no block matched DP-2 and HDMI-A-1 pointed at a missing file, so nothing visible changed on apply. Fix: catch-all `monitor =` block, and `render_all` falls back to the main wallpaper when `tv_wallpaper` does not exist. Only one image is in `~/Pictures/wallpapers`, so the picker has nothing else to switch to. Not verified live (hyprpaper not restarted; run `theme.py apply <img>` or restart hyprpaper to check).

## Per-monitor wallpapers (2026-10-21)

- **Request:** per-monitor wallpapers chosen in Settings > Wallpaper & theme; the colour theme comes from the primary monitor's wallpaper.
- **Data model:** `${XDG_STATE_HOME:-~/.local/state}/theme/wallpapers.json` (`THEME_WALLPAPERS_FILE` overrides on the theme.py side, `QS_WALLPAPERS_FILE` on the QML side), host-local, owned and written only by `theme.py`: `{"version":1,"primary":"<key>","monitors":{"<key>":"~/img.png"}}`. Key = the usual monitor key (description as Hyprland reports it, else the connector; `valid_key()` = `managed.monitor_key` rules). Unplugged monitors' entries are kept; entries are never pruned.
- **Primary = theme source:** its wallpaper is `wallpaper` in `theme.toml` (unchanged: palette, templates, apps, `Theme.wallpaper`); it never has an own entry. `primary` unset (or not connected) = the connected monitor at 0,0 (same "main display" rule as the launcher; there is no Lua/QML primary marker, `monitors.primary` in monitors.lua is host Lua only), else the first. The first per-monitor write pins the resolved primary into the file so a layout change does not move it. A monitor without an entry shows the primary's wallpaper (= `theme.toml`'s).
- **CLI (theme.py):** `apply <img>` = primary, as before. `apply <img> --monitor <key>`: key is the primary = the normal apply; else sets the entry, re-renders ONLY `out/hyprpaper.conf` and live-sets that output with `hyprctl hyprpaper wallpaper "<selector>,<path>,cover"` (hyprpaper 0.8.4 IPC; works for images that were never preloaded, no restart, no palette, no app reloads). **The selector must match the config block's:** `desc:<key>` for a description key, the connector only for a connector-name key. Live bug (2026-10-21): with the connector (`DP-3,...`) the IPC exited 0, printed nothing and `listactive` did not change, because the live config has a `desc:` rule for that output; the nested test only had connector-name rules and so missed it. `live_set_wallpaper(key, conn, path)` now polls `listactive` (up to ~3 s) and restarts hyprpaper (reads the new conf) if the output did not switch, printing a note to stderr. Verified live: the `desc:` call switched DP-3 (listactive); the restart fallback was not exercised live. `clear <key>` drops the entry (live-set back to the primary's image). `primary <key>`: the old primary gets an entry = the current `wallpaper`, the new primary's entry becomes `wallpaper`; if that image differs it is a full apply, else only the hyprpaper conf is re-rendered. `render` (post-merge hook) is unchanged without a file: `out/` byte-identical to HEAD's theme.py except the host-CSS import paths of the scratch dir (checked old vs new in a scratch HOME).
- **hyprpaper:** `templates/hyprpaper.conf` got `{{monitor_preloads}}` (end of the last preload line) and `{{monitor_blocks}}` (after the last block): both empty strings without entries, so the file is unchanged. Blocks are appended last: for the same monitor the LATER block wins (tested: catch-all < specific, duplicate `HB` blocks -> the last). Selector = connector name if the key is one, else `desc:<key>`. hyprpaper does not hot-reload its config; a full apply still restarts it (`reload_apps`), which reads the new blocks.
- **Settings page** (`SettingsWallpaper.qml` + `WallpaperMonitorCard.qml`, `WallpaperTile.qml`; rebuilt 2026-10-21 with Images / Videos tabs). Top to bottom: title row `headerItem` = theme source name (the primary's VIDEO name when it plays one, not the cached still) + swatches. With 2+ screens: "Monitors" cards (min 200 px, so 3 fit across at the 920x600 minimum; under 250 px the thumbnail shrinks and PRIMARY becomes the palette glyph only): thumbnail (play glyph over a video's still), model (connector only when it differs from the title), sub line = file, `video.mp4 · loop|once`, or "same as primary" for a monitor without its own entry. Clicking a card = the monitor being edited. Under the cards a box says what that means, wrapping (no truncation): primary = "its wallpaper also sets the colour theme of every app"; other = own wallpaper vs "shows the primary's wallpaper", naming the primary, with "Follow primary" (`clear`, only with an own entry) and "Make primary" (`primary`). Then "Wallpaper for <monitor>" (one screen: "Wallpaper", no monitor section at all) with a `Segmented` tab pair "Images N" / "Videos N" in the head's trailing slot; the grid shows only that library. Videos tab only: a "Playback on <monitor>" box with Play once / Loop, an "Optimize" `Toggle` (status text; progress while encoding) and, for Play once, "Play for" (0..D whole seconds, D = the video's usable length; the maximum position is "Full length" = no timing, wind disabled; 0 s = start winding down at once) and "Wind down" (0.5 s steps, 0..2 x (D - play) = `(D - play) / windAvg`, recomputed live as Play moves and clamped visibly, dimmed with the hint "lower Play for" at Full length, label "3 s / max 22 s") sliders that follow the playing clip's `play` / `wind` and re-apply it the moment the slider is RELEASED (nothing is applied while it is held; keys / wheel settle 250 ms; `apply <video> --mode once --play S --wind S`; restarts the clip, cancels a stale bake and starts the new one; the dragged value is kept until the state file shows it, 4 s at most; picking a new video uses the slider values; sliders get no hover glow), and a wallpaper that is a cached video still with no `primary_video` in the state (older state / hand-edited theme.toml) is mapped back to its video by name for the cards and header (`stillVideo()`; the hash comes before the optional `-t<ms>`): shows the edited monitor's current mode when it plays a video (own or the primary's it follows), picking the other mode re-applies that same video (`apply <video> --mode m [--monitor k]`; for a follower that creates its own entry; for the primary it is a full re-theme since the still changes: last frame vs middle frame), otherwise it sets the mode for the next video clicked. The playing video's tile has a "▶ LOOP/ONCE" badge (no badge on other videos). Tab choice: opens on the tab matching what the edited monitor shows; a tab picked by hand is remembered per monitor for the shell session, as are the edited monitor and the next-video mode (`memory` page hook). Keys: arrows / Home / End / Enter in the current tab's grid, Tab / Shift+Tab switch tabs (selection resets). Test hook (documented, kept): `qs ipc call wallpaperpage tab images|videos`, `qs ipc call wallpaperpage monitor <key>` (only while the page is open). It reads the state file with a FileView (plus a 300 ms reload poll while a command runs, since a not-yet-existing file is not watched). "Applying..." clears when the target shows the file (and mode). Verified 2026-10-21 in an isolated Quickshell (scratch HOME/XDG, scratch wallpapers.json, generated images + the user's mp4s symlinked) in a nested Hyprland floated on the secondary monitor, screenshots via grim on the nested outputs: primary images / hand-picked videos, non-primary with a loop video (auto Videos tab), follower of an image and of a primary once-video, own-image monitor, session memory across close/reopen, single monitor (both tabs), 920x600 and a 2560x1440 output; no QML warnings. Not verified: real clicks / key presses (no synthetic input; tabs and targets switched over the IPC hook), an actual mode switch or apply (theme.py was blocked in the rig), the empty-folder texts, the live page on screen (live shell reloaded clean, but the page was not opened live).
- **Verified (nested Hyprland, 3 outputs, scratch HOME, stubbed `reload_apps`, nothing live touched):** render without a file identical to old; non-primary apply/clear/primary swap/plain apply update the json, toml and conf as described; the live IPC changed a running hyprpaper's output; a hyprpaper started on the generated conf showed the right colour per output (grim, 1 px samples); isolated Quickshell screenshots of the page: primary target, non-primary target, after a primary swap (theme recoloured, badge moved). **Not verified:** real clicks (no synthetic input), real monitor descriptions (`desc:` selector and keys with spaces; the nested outputs have none, so keys were connector names), the live hyprpaper, real multi-monitor hardware, the `primary` full-apply on the live desktop.
- **Video entries:** an entry may also be a video object and the primary has `primary_video`: see **Video wallpapers**.
- **Links:** `links` (monitor groups that always change together) in the same file: see **Monitor links**.
- **Caveats:** identical monitors without a serial share one key; if the stored primary is unplugged the 0,0 monitor acts as primary meanwhile (its own entry is ignored, not deleted); a connector-name key of an unplugged monitor gets a harmless `desc:` block; `tv_wallpaper` (HDMI-A-1 block) still comes before the generated blocks, so an entry for that monitor overrides it. Test hazard: running `theme.py` from a scratch copy with the REAL `HOME` rewrites links in `~/.config` and `~/.local/share/themes`; always set `HOME` in the same command.

## Monitor links (2026-10-22)

- **Request:** a link toggle between monitor cards on the Wallpaper & theme and Bar pages; linked monitors always change together.
- **UI (shared):** `MonitorCardRow.qml` lays the cards out (absolute x/y from `xOf(i)`/`yOf(i)`, `cols` from `minCardW`, gap derived from the notch, 30) and puts a `MonitorLinkButton.qml` (26 px round, opaque, HoverGlow; accent chain = linked, muted broken chain = unlinked) in the gap between card i and i+1 (`linkCenter(k)`); across a wrap (row break) it sits centred in the gap between the rows. The page gives `linkedAt(i)` / `linkToggled(i)`. Cards in the picked card's group get a softer accent border (`grouped`); headings name the group ("Wallpaper for A + B", "Layout · A + B", info box "Linked with ...").
- **Link notches (2026-10-22, user: the chain circle overlapped the card edges; follow-up: 3 px gap too small, use the config's spacing scale):** each card gets a round cut-out around every link button that reaches it, radius = button/2 + `notchGap`, so the button sits in a concave gap with no overlap. `notchGap` = new `Theme.controlGap` 6: the Settings spacing scale is 6 (between adjacent controls: rows, button groups, = `chipGap`), 8, 12 (between blocks, page column spacing), paddings 9-12 inside cards. The card `gap` is derived, not tuned: the cut may bite only as far as keeps `notchGap` to the card content, `gap = btnSize + 4 x notchGap - 2 x inset` = 26 + 24 - 20 = 30 (`inset` 10 = smallest card content margin, WallpaperMonitorCard's left; the Bar card's 12 just gets more room); the button itself no longer touches the cards, the bite is 4 px. `MonitorCardRow.notchesOf(i)` returns `[Qt.vector3d(x, y, r)]` in card coordinates for every link whose notch circle reaches card i's rect (up to 4; also corners at a row-break link with square corners); the cards set `layer.enabled: notches.length > 0` and `layer.effect: NotchMask {...}` (`NotchMask.qml` + `shaders/notchmask.frag(.qsb)`, build line in the .frag): the card is drawn into its layer as before (fill, border, HoverGlow, content) and the shader cuts the circles and draws a stroke of the card's border colour along each cut (SDF stroke centred on the cut, width `borderWidth` + 0.3, smoothstep AA over +-1 device px via `fwidth`; the hole edge, stroke and card fill share the ramp, so no seam), clipped to the card's rounded rect (`radius` = the card's). Used by the Bar page's card (`SettingsBar.qml`, `mon`) and `WallpaperMonitorCard.qml`. Why a shader mask, not `Shape` paths: one SDF handles notches on edges and corners at any card size / radius with no path boolean, and also clips the hover glow; the cards are flat fills on the Settings glass card (not `isGlassItem`), so the compositor glass region is unaffected.
  - **AA fix (2026-10-22, user: the cut border looked jagged on the live screen, scale 1):** first version used a 1 px linear ramp on an exact 1 px band (coverage(dc) - coverage(dc - 1)). Measured on nested captures (per-row red-excess profiles): the stroke alternated between one crisp pixel (~0.95) and two half pixels (~0.5 / 0.5) per row, i.e. visibly stepped, while Qt's own AA'd rounded border at the card corners spreads ~2 px per row and carries ~1.3x the ink of its straight edge. Fixed as above; 8x nearest crops now look like the Qt corner. Card positions checked integer in the nested scene (temporary `mapToItem` log in the test copy), so layer resampling was ruled out there; not checked on the live geometry or when the page is scrolled to a fractional offset. `.frag` / `.qsb` changes do not trigger Quickshell's hot reload; the live shell needs a restart to load a rebuilt shader.
  - **New type = live restart:** `NotchMask` is listed in `qmldir`; the live quickshell does NOT re-read qmldir on hot reload (Settings > Bar / Wallpaper went blank with "NotchMask is not a type" until restarted). Settings pages load lazily, so such errors only show in `qs log` when the page is opened.
  - **Verified (nested `/usr/bin/Hyprland` 0.56.2, own `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR`, headless outputs as the extra monitors, isolated HOME with a shell copy, solid `misc.background_color` backdrop, `grim -o <headless>` on the nested socket, zoomed crops):** both pages, linked and unlinked, in one row and wrapped (row-break link over a card's bottom edge and over a 4-way gap), Appearance radius 12 (`radiusPage` 0, square) and 32 (`radiusPage` 14); notchGap 3 / gap 20 first, then 6 / 30. Even gap, AA edge, border follows the cut, thumbnail clears the cut by ~6 px, text unchanged. Headless outputs only rendered while the nested window was visible (floated on the second monitor); hidden on a special workspace grim hung.
  - **Not verified:** hover glow inside a notched card (no pointer in the rig; it is in the layer, so it is cut by construction), fractional scale, light palette, the fork's solid_glass. If the .qsb fails to load the effect logs `NotchMask shader failed`.
- **Wallpaper:** links are `"links": [[keyA, keyB], ...]` (edges; chains = one group) in `wallpapers.json`, written only by `theme.py` (omitted when empty; unknown/duplicate/invalid pairs dropped on load; keyed by monitor key so they survive re-plugging, unplugged members are still applied to). CLI: `apply <file> --monitor k1 --monitor k2 ...` (repeatable; ONE call: entry made once, state + hyprpaper conf written once, each output live-set, players synced once; the primary among the keys = the others' entries are saved, then the normal full apply/re-theme runs), `clear k1 k2 ...`, `link <a> <b> [--from <key>]` (adds the edge, then the whole group takes `--from`'s wallpaper: its entry, or the primary's if it is the primary; a source that just follows the primary makes the group follow it), `unlink <a> <b>`. The page sends every apply / mode / play / wind / ease / Follow primary for the whole group of the edited monitor (`applyCmd`, `monitorCmd`); "Make primary" stays per monitor (links persist). Linking from the page uses `--from <edited monitor>` (the left card's when the edited one is on neither side). Bakes/optimizes are queued per monitor as before. Replay-on-empty-workspace is per monitor: linked monitors replay independently.
- **Bar:** links live in `bars.json` under the reserved key `"_links"` (same pair format; old files without it and old readers are unaffected; `pip-reserve.lua`'s regex skips it, checked by reading the pattern). Linked monitors share on/off, edge and layout: `BarConfig.set` writes the field to every key of the group; `BarWidgets.store` (so move / hide / show / copy / reset) writes the layout to every key of the group (also unplugged ones). On/off is linked on purpose ("they act as one bar"); the last-bar rule is about the group: `BarConfig.canDisable(s)` is false when no enabled bar would remain outside the group (the card's toggle dims, `set` refuses). `BarConfig.link(a, b, from)` copies `from`'s on/off + edge to the group (keeps bars on when `from` is off and nothing is enabled outside), and `BarWidgets.syncGroup(from)` copies its layout (or the default). "Copy from" lists only monitors outside the group; Reset applies to the group.
- **Verified:** theme.py in a scratch HOME/XDG with shim `hyprctl` / `gsettings` / `pkill` on PATH and fake monitor descriptions: group apply of an image and a video, primary inside the group (full apply, theme follows), link / unlink / clear, source following the primary, swapped connectors (keys), old state files, junk links. BarConfig / BarWidgets through rig-only IPC hooks on copies (not in the repo): old file, linked edits (edge, hide), last-bar refusal, link from an off monitor, reset / copyFrom on a group, junk `_links`. Screenshots of both pages with 2 and 3 monitors, linked and unlinked, at the forced 920x600-class card (1280x720 nested outputs) in a nested Hyprland floated on DP-3 (`grim -o` on the nested socket).
- **Not verified:** real clicks on the link buttons (no synthetic input; link state set through the state files), the live apply through the Settings page, real monitor descriptions, hyprpaper reaction to a group apply on the live desktop.
- **Caveats / ideas:** a link across a row break is a centred button between the rows (ambiguous with 4+ monitors); links apply to what the page shows as one group even when an unplugged member exists; replays are not linked (open question).

## Video wallpapers (2026-10-21)

- **Request:** video wallpapers (mpvpaper 1.9, libmpv from mpv 0.41; research in the session notes, claims below are the verified ones) per monitor or all monitors, mode **once** (plays, then holds the last frame: the login animation) or **loop**, chosen in Settings > Wallpaper & theme.
- **Source folder:** `video_dirs` in `theme.toml` (default `~/Videos/wallpapers`; unset = `xdg-user-dir VIDEOS`/wallpapers, and `$HOME` as answer counts as unset, then `~/Videos`). Written to `out/palette.json` as `video_dirs` (`Theme.videoDirs`, QML falls back to `~/Videos/wallpapers` while an old palette.json lacks it: run `theme.py render` once). Images still come from `wallpaper_dirs`; videos are NOT searched there. Extensions mp4/webm/mkv.
- **Data model** (`wallpapers.json`, version still 1, old files valid, no video entries = behaviour exactly as before): a monitor entry is an image string OR `{"video": "~/Videos/wallpapers/x.mp4", "mode": "once"|"loop", "still": "~/.cache/theme/video/<name>-<mode>-<hash>.png"}`. The primary's video is the top-level `"primary_video"` (same shape); `wallpaper` in theme.toml is then its still, so palette / templates / hyprpaper stay image driven. A monitor WITHOUT entry follows the primary: image or video (so a primary video plays on all monitors unless a monitor has its own image/video entry; "all monitors" = no special mode).
- **Stills:** `video_still()` (ffmpeg, cached in `$XDG_CACHE_HOME/theme/video`, keyed by path+mtime+size+tag; once uses tag `once2`, so the old black-tail stills are not reused): `once` = frame at the **hold point** (`-ss <hold> -frames:v 1`), or without a black tail the last frame (`-sseof -0.5 -update 1`, fallback full decode); `loop` = middle frame. **Hold point (2026-10-21, fixes once-videos ending on black, e.g. nier-automata: black from 22.70 s of 24.37 s, so the held wallpaper and the palette were black):** `video_hold()` runs `blackdetect=d=0.05:pix_th=0.10:pic_th=0.98` on the last 5 s scaled to 160 px (then 30 s, then the whole file if the tail is all black; ~0.2-0.5 s even for 4K); a black run only counts when it reaches the end of the file; hold = black_start - 0.15 s (nier: 22.55 s), `None` when there is no black tail. Thresholds: a frame is black when 98 % of its pixels have luma under 10 % of range, so a fade-out is cut where it drops under 10 %; 0.05 s minimum run ignores flicker. Cached as `<stem>-once2-<hash>.json` (`{"hold": s|null}`) beside the still. `vp_start()` appends `end=<hold>` to the once options (`keep-open=yes` then pauses on that frame). Loop mode untouched. Used for the palette (primary) and as the hyprpaper image UNDER the player, so login never shows black and a vanished player reveals the identical frame. `video_thumb()` = 640 px JPEG for the Settings grid.
- **Players:** one `mpvpaper -o "<opts>" <connector> <file>` per output (connector name, not the description), started by `video_sync()`; pid files `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR/theme-video/<connector>.json` ({pid, video, mode}), logs `mpvpaper-<connector>.log` there. Stop = SIGTERM then SIGKILL to the recorded pid ONLY if `/proc/<pid>/comm` is `mpvpaper` (tested: a pid file pointing at an unrelated process is just dropped). A flock serialises sync runs (login script + monitor.added can race). Options: once `keep-open=yes loop-file=no no-audio hwdec=auto-safe panscan=1.0` (+ ` end=<hold>` when the clip ends in black), loop `loop-file=inf no-audio hwdec=auto-safe panscan=1.0` (panscan 1 = cover like hyprpaper). No `-p` auto-pause (research: workspace detection unreliable on Hyprland, #110).
- **CLI** (`theme.py`): `apply <file> [--monitor <key>] [--mode once|loop]` (file may be a video; no `--monitor` = primary, full apply from the still; image applies clear the video of that target and stop its player), `clear <key>`, `primary <key>` (video entries move with the swap; the old primary keeps its video as an entry), `video start|stop|restart|sync [key]` (start/sync wait up to 15 s for hyprpaper, start only missing players, stop unwanted/changed ones; `restart` forces; `stop` is temporary, the next sync restarts), `video status`, `video thumb|thumbs <files>` (prints `<video>\t<jpg>`).
- **Hyprland:** `scripts/start-hyprpaper.sh` runs `theme.py video start` after starting hyprpaper (the player layer must map after hyprpaper's); `hyprland/autostart.lua` re-runs it 3 s after `monitor.added` and 1 s after `monitor.removed` (reaps the dead player, starts the new output's). `restart_hyprpaper()` (a full apply restarts hyprpaper) spawns a detached `video restart` when videos are wanted, because the new hyprpaper surface would otherwise map above the old players; `main` then skips starting in its own sync (`video_sync(start=False)`).
- **Settings:** the grid lists images then videos; videos get a cached still thumbnail (async: one `theme.py video thumbs` Process, results streamed per file) and a "VIDEO" badge; the current-check follows the video path. Since 2026-10-21 (later) images and videos are separate tabs and the Play once / Loop control lives only in the Videos tab: see **Per-monitor wallpapers** > Settings page.
- **Verified (nested Hyprland 0.56 fork build, isolated HOME/XDG, scratch copy of theme.py with deploy/apply_brave/reload_apps stubbed and hyprpaper killed by pid; WAYLAND-1 window output + 2 headless; test clips from ffmpeg testsrc2: 720p30 h264, 720p30 vp9 webm, 4K60 h264, all tagged bt709/tv; nested window floated on the secondary monitor):**
  - **No MPRIS (2026-10-22):** `MPV_OPTS` carry `load-scripts=no`, so the system `mpv-mpris` plugin (/etc/mpv/scripts/mpris.so, installed for the Jellyfin player) does not autoload into mpvpaper; otherwise every wallpaper shows up as a player and takes over the bar now-playing widget. Explicit `scripts=winddown.lua` still loads (verified with a probe script; playerctl count unchanged with the flag, +1 without).
  - **Layer order (fixed 2026-10-22):** players run on the `bottom` layer (`mpvpaper -l bottom`), hyprpaper on `background`, so the video is always above the still and below every window. Before, both were `background` and the order depended on who mapped last: hyprpaper decodes its 4K still slower than mpvpaper starts, mapped later and hid the playing video behind the still (user: "does not move at all" while the player log showed bursts playing; `hyprctl layers` listed `[mpvpaper, hyprpaper]`). Check with `hyprctl layers -j`: mpvpaper must be in level 1 of each monitor.
  - **End of play-once:** wf-recorder lossless capture of the output (580-632 frames at 60 Hz) through start and end: no black or flash frame (min mean luma 99-124 = the images/frames shown, black would be ~0-16), the held frame is the video's last frame (frame 149/149 by the on-screen counter), identical to the still frame by eye; PSNR 28 dB against the still only from hyprpaper's vs mpv's scaling of fine test-pattern lines (colours match, red bar fc0000 vs fb0000). Video start under the still: no black either. mpvpaper stays alive after the end (keep-open).
  - **Idle after the end:** 10 s windows, nested compositor CPU 0.0 %, mpvpaper CPU 0.2 %, GPU busy (whole RX 9070 XT, card1, shared with the live desktop so noise is ~3 %): held 4.2 % avg vs image-only 3.4 % (glass popup open on all 3 outputs), i.e. idle.
  - **Cost while playing** (glass popup over each output, solid_glass; nested Hyprland CPU / mpvpaper CPU / GPU busy avg): 720p30 loop 0.8 % / 2.1 % / 8.0 %; 4K60 loop 1.2 % / 3.0 % / 13.4 %. Without glass: 720p30 0.5 % / 2.4 % / 6.3 %; 4K60 0.8 % / 2.5 % / 13.1 %. So glass re-blur on one 1280x720 output adds about +0.3 to +1.7 points GPU and under 1 % of a core; a play-once video costs nothing after it ends. Nested outputs are 720p60, not 2560x1440@360 Hz: the live blur cost will be larger (blur area is much bigger), unmeasured there.
  - **Real files** (3 mp4s from the user's folder, symlinked read-only into the isolated HOME, stills/thumbs in the isolated cache; 4K60 baseline-profile h264 30 s, 4K30 h264 24 s, 1080p25 h264 120 s; all bt709/tv except the 4K60 one, untagged): mpv log `Using hardware decoding (vaapi)`, `VO: [libmpv] 3840x2160 vaapi[nv12]`, mpvpaper RSS 244 MB. Numbers (10 s windows, nested compositor CPU / mpvpaper CPU / GPU busy avg, glass popup open unless noted; idle baseline 2.6-3.4 %): 4K60 once PLAYING 1.4 % / 2.8 % / 15.6 % (max 60 % spikes); 4K60 once HELD after the end (30 s clip) 0.0 % / 0.1 % / 3.3 %, again 0.0 % / 0.0 % / 4.9 % (idle); 4K60 loop 1.3 % / 1.8 % / 14.5 %, no glass 0.8 % / 1.9 % / 13.1 %; 4K30 loop 0.7 % / 2.2 % / 9.2 %; 1080p25 loop (no glass) 0.4 % / 1.9 % / 5.8 %. Last-frame still of the 4K60 file took 0.6 s; held frame equals the still (the PSNR of the grim vs still is only low because the test glass popup covers part of it, see `video-wp/real-10221-held-vs-still.png`).
  - **Unreadable files:** `.mlw` is not a listed extension; a corrupt `.mp4` (random bytes) gets no thumbnail (ffmpeg stderr is silenced, `video thumbs` prints an empty path), the Settings cell is dimmed with "unreadable: <name>" and clicks on it are ignored (no "Applying..." state); `apply` of such a file exits 1 with one stderr line and changes no state. Screenshot `video-wp/settings-real-videos.png`.
  - **Shutdown:** monitor -> image stops that player only (others kept); primary -> image stops all followers; `kill -9` of a player then `video start` respawns it; `primary` swap with a video moves it (`primary_video` <-> entry); no mpvpaper left behind (`pgrep` empty, live hyprpaper untouched).
  - **Settings:** isolated Quickshell screenshots (session scratchpad `video-wp/settings-*.png`): cards with video stills and `test-a.mp4 (once)`, toggle row, badges, current ring, theme recoloured from the video's last frame (accent red).
