- **Not verified:** real clicks (no synthetic input; target switching and the toggle buttons were not clicked: the primary-target screenshot was produced by `theme.py primary`), real monitor descriptions (keys with spaces; players use the connector so only `entry -> connector` mapping is involved), the live hyprpaper / live outputs / VA-API decode on the real session's outputs (VA-API decode WAS verified in the nested compositor, log line above; the live session's log line is the check), the real 360 Hz blur cost, mkv, `start-hyprpaper.sh` and the Lua hooks beyond `bash -n` / `loadfile` (running them would pkill the live hyprpaper), HDR / 10-bit sources, hypridle idle inhibition by mpvpaper (research issue #113).
- **Caveats:** a play-once clip's player restart (any full apply / hyprpaper restart, primary swap, monitor re-plug) resumes its replay STATION when a baked file exists (held on the last burst's end frame); only login / a new bake / a changed entry replays the first play (unbaked: replays from 0). Memory growth on long loops (mpvpaper #117/#116/#101): prefer once, or restart players now and then (`theme.py video restart`). Players keep decoding when a window covers the output (no auto-pause); a looping video forces the glass re-blur every video frame (prefer 24-30 fps; a 60 fps 4K source is the worst case). Monitor disconnect can segfault mpvpaper (#97): the pid file goes stale and the next sync / `monitor.removed` hook reaps it. Untagged videos can get a different colour matrix in the ffmpeg still than in mpv; tagged (bt709/tv) ones match. The theme's palette for a primary `once` video comes from its hold frame (last non-black frame). Verified in a nested Hyprland (isolated HOME/XDG): `end=22.549` + `keep-open=yes` pauses mpv at 22 s (log `(Paused)`), mpvpaper stays alive at ~1 % CPU, grim of the nested output shows the held frame (luma 107.7, PSNR 40 dB vs the still); the two clips without a black tail hold their true last frame (log 100 %, PSNR 36 / 45 dB vs the last-frame still, scaling only). Existing live entries keep their old (black) still until re-applied: `theme.py apply <video> --mode once`.
- **Try it live:** (1) `python3 ~/.config/theme/theme.py render` (writes `video_dirs` into palette.json), `hyprctl reload` is not needed. (2) Settings (SUPER+T) > Wallpaper & theme: pick the monitor card, choose "Play once" or "Loop", click a video (thumbnails appear a moment after opening; the first apply extracts the still). (3) Check `python3 ~/.config/theme/theme.py video status` and `hyprctl layers`. (4) Back to an image: click an image for that monitor (stops its player). At login the players start from `start-hyprpaper.sh`. Logs: `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR/theme-video/mpvpaper-<connector>.log`.
- **Timed play-once (2026-10-21, "play 3 s at full speed, wind down over 3 s, stay still"):** entry fields `play` / `wind` (seconds). **Semantics changed 2026-10-21 (user: "wind down with play for 0, a ~3 s slowdown without ever playing full speed"):** `play` is literal, 0 = the wind starts at the first frame (t_stop = wind / 2); NO `play` key (`play` None in code, `--play` absent on the CLI) = full length (the whole clip up to the hold point, wind 0), which is also the migration: every entry written before only had `play` when it was > 0, so old entries keep their meaning with no rewrite. `play >= D` (D = `video_usable()` = black-tail hold or duration - 0.05, rounded DOWN to whole seconds, also what the sliders use) = full length. **Limits (`clamp_timing(play, wind, usable)`):** play + wind * `WIND_AVG` <= D, i.e. wind <= 2 (D - play) (the wind consumes wind * 0.5 s of media: `WIND_AVG` = mean of the raised cosine, one constant for theme.py, `winddown.lua` and the QML, which gets it as 4th column of `theme.py video thumbs`: `path, thumb, usable s, WIND_AVG`); only absurd values are capped (`PLAY_MAX` 600, `WIND_MAX` 120). `video_plan` still shortens a wind that does not fit the real (unrounded) limit with the same curve. Note the wind could technically be longer than the UI allows (it would just be shortened to the clip). `video_plan` returns t_stop >= 0.1. The script gets `winddown-timed=yes|no` (a play of 0 needs a flag now), `-play`, `-wind`, `-stop` (t_stop from theme.py). The Settings sliders get D for the playing clip from the scan's `video thumbs` run, or from `video thumb <file>` when the clip is outside the scanned folders (`loadUsable`). CLI: `apply <video> --mode once [--play S] [--wind S] [--monitor k]` (a re-apply is the way to change them; `video_sync` compares video, mode, play, wind, so it restarts the player). Runs INSIDE mpv: `mpv/winddown.lua` is passed through mpvpaper's `-o` (`input-ipc-server=<runtime>/theme-video/<conn>.sock scripts=<repo>/mpv/winddown.lua script-opts=winddown-play=S,winddown-wind=S[,winddown-smooth=yes][,winddown-loop=yes]`; mpvpaper forwards them, the script loads and logs `[winddown] ...` into the player log; paths with spaces are skipped because mpvpaper splits on spaces). **Curve:** speed 1 until `time-pos >= play`, then a 60 Hz timer sets `speed = max(0.01, (1 + cos(pi*u)) / 2)`, u = elapsed / wind (raised cosine: smooth start and end), then pause. Media time covered by the wind = integral = wind / 2, so **t_stop = play + wind / 2** (measured: play 3 / wind 3: speed 1.0, 0.967, 0.877, 0.742, 0.578, 0.404, 0.242, 0.111, 0.027, 0.01 at t = 0, .35, .68, 1.02, 1.35, 1.69, 2.02, 2.35, 2.69, 2.99 s; integral of the logged speed over wall time 1.484 s vs 1.5 planned). `video_plan()` clamps t_stop to the black-tail hold / clip end (the wind is shortened with the same curve, `play >= limit` = whole clip), t_stop is mpv's `end=` (keep-open holds it) and the still / palette time. **mpv's `end=` is exclusive: it holds the LAST FRAME BEFORE t_stop** (30 fps, t_stop 4.5: held frame 4.467, shown by a frame-counter test clip), so `video_still()` now takes that frame (`-ss t-1 -t 1 -update 1`, last frame wins) for timed AND black-tail holds; before it took the first frame >= hold (one frame later; the old 40 dB PSNR). The script only seeks to t_stop when the timer ended more than 1.5 frames short. Still cache name gets `-t<ms>` (key = source + t_stop, so play/wind changes re-extract). Measured: held grim == still frame (same counter), mpvpaper CPU 0 ticks / 10 s after the stop. **Smoothness:** at low speed frames are held for many display frames (looks steppy); `winddown-smooth=yes` (on, `WINDDOWN_SMOOTH`) sets `video-sync=display-resample`, `interpolation=yes`, `tscale=linear` for the wind only. Gotcha: libmpv under mpvpaper gets no display timing (`display-fps` unavailable) and interpolation did NOTHING until `display-fps-override=60` was set (display frames changing 1.0 with it, 0.1-0.5 without, by recording the output) - **BUG FIXED 2026-10-21 (user: "the wind down speeds it up"):** the override is NOT a render cap. mpvpaper presents at the output's frame callbacks, mpv paces by the override, so playback runs at (callback Hz / override) x speed: override 60 on a 360 Hz output = 6x faster (nested headless 2560x1440@360, 30 fps clip with burned-in counter, play 3 / wind 10: time-pos 3.13 -> 6.13 in the first 0.5 s, reached end= after ~1 s; override 240 on 360 = 1.5x, 360 on 144 = 0.4x and stopped short at 6.17). Correct override = real refresh: 360 on 360 Hz, 144 on 144 Hz, 60 on 60 Hz all follow the analytic curve (time-pos 6.533 at wind t = 4.02 s vs 6.527 computed, stop 7.967 = end=). Smoothing off at 360 Hz also correct. **Now:** `theme.py` passes `winddown-fps=<round(refresh of the connector, hyprctl)>` and only enables `winddown-smooth=yes` when 24 <= Hz <= `WINDDOWN_SMOOTH_MAX_HZ` (145); above that (the 360 Hz OLED) the wind is the plain speed ramp. `winddown.lua` smooths only when `winddown-fps` >= 24 (no silent 60 default). Why not smoothing at 360: measured on a nested 360 Hz output, with one glass popup, 10 s wind: GPU busy 10.9 % -> 18.0 % (+7 points, best of 3, the shared live GPU makes the other pairs noisy), board power 45.9 -> 53.4 W, nested compositor CPU 0.4 -> 3.6-9 %, mpvpaper CPU 1.4 -> 3.3-7 % (no glass: GPU 9.2 -> 12.9 %, 45.8 -> 51.2 W); a render per vsync, and the real bar glass re-blurs on each. Caveat: the refresh is read when the player starts; changing a monitor's refresh later needs `theme.py video restart` or smoothing speed is off. Turn smoothing off entirely with `WINDDOWN_SMOOTH = False` in theme.py. Live check: DP-1 restarted, IPC samples of time-pos/speed every 0.25 s: monotonic, speed 1 -> 0.01, time-pos 7.483 at the stop (end 7.5). Not verified: look of smoothing at 100-144 Hz outputs on screen (only the time-pos curve), VRR monitors.
- **Baked wind-down (2026-10-21/22, user: "the wind down is choppy, interpolate so the slow down is still smooth"; `theme.py` `bake_*`, on with "Optimize for monitor", `THEME_BAKE=0` = off for tests):** the runtime ramp steps through SOURCE frames: at 0.3x a 30 fps clip shows 9 distinct frames/s (measured on a 360 Hz output: 30, 25, 16, 7, 1 distinct frames in the five seconds of a play 6 / wind 5 wind) and runtime interpolation is too costly at 360 Hz. So once clips with a wind get a per-monitor baked file in the opt cache (`<stem>-<WxH>-bake-p<play>w<wind>-<hash>.mp4`, same LRU): **play part** (source frames before `play`, source timestamps, the optimized scale / fps, own encode) + **wind part** (the source range [play, t_stop] retimed to the wall-clock wind at a constant F fps, in-between frames synthesized), stream-copy concatenated; mpv then plays it at speed 1 (`winddown-timed=no`, no `end=`, keep-open holds the last frame = the still; a play of 0 = the file is only the wind part). **F** = largest of 25..60 that divides the refresh (`opt_fps(BAKE_FPS_MAX=60, hz)`: 60 at 360 / 60 Hz, 30 when none fits or unknown); 60 costs only 5 s of render per replay; measured wind 2.9-3.7 % mpvpaper CPU vs 0.8-1.8 % for the runtime ramp, versus the runtime smoothing's +7 points / +7.5 W at 360 Hz.
  - **Retiming math:** wind curve s(x) = (1 + cos(pi x)) / 2, x = w / W; media offset m(w) = play + (last - play) * 2 mu(x), mu(x) = x / 2 + sin(pi x) / (2 pi) (mu(1) = 1/2: the wind covers W / 2 of media); `last` = pts of the source frame before t_stop (mpv's `end=` is exclusive, this is the frame the still shows, `bake_last_pts`, from packets: no decoding); W is rounded up to N = ceil(W F) output frames; output frame k (0..N) is the source at m(k / F) on a 64-piece linear version of the curve (`bake_breaks`; k = N is exactly `last`).
  - **Interpolators** (`BAKE_INTERP` / `$THEME_BAKE_INTERP`: auto | rife | minterpolate | blend | dup): **auto = RIFE** (`rife-ncnn-vulkan` from the AUR `rife-ncnn-vulkan-bin`, models in `/usr/share/rife-ncnn-vulkan/<name>`, default `rife-v4.14` (user pick 2026-10-22: lite/heavy/large rejected by eye; v4.14 vs v4.25 A/B after a ground-truth score where v4.14 had the lowest gap-4 flicker, 1.50 vs 1.96, quality otherwise tied), then v4.25, v4.6; `$THEME_RIFE_MODEL` overrides; interp string and model are in the cache key) when installed, else ffmpeg `minterpolate`. RIFE runs in the bake's **RIFE stage** (below: a few long-lived directory-mode `rife-ncnn-vulkan` processes, NOT one process per frame); **GPU 0 = the RX 9070 XT, 1 = the iGPU** (`-l`; `RIFE_GPU`); step 0 = a plain copy of the source frame, so the last frame is the still. **Colour:** the PNG round trip must convert back with the SOURCE's matrix and tags (`video_color`, `scale=out_color_matrix`, `-colorspace ...`): untagged conversion shifted a bt709 clip by 21 dB. If RIFE fails the job falls back to minterpolate for the same file.
  - **Quality, ground truth** (a clip decimated to half rate, the dropped frames re-synthesized at t = 0.5, PSNR against the real frames, per frame): the user's 2b clip (real content, 48 frames): dup 37.6 dB, blend 42.0, minterpolate mci 42.9, RIFE v4.25 43.9 (v4.14 43.8, v4.6 43.5; RIFE better in 48 of 48 frames); synthetic fast clip (box at ~1400 px/s over a fractal): dup 16.6, blend 19.1, minterpolate 21.0, RIFE v4.14 24.6, v4.18 24.4, v4.25-lite 24.2, v4.25 24.1, v4.6 24.0, v4 21.9. Close-ups of the same wind frames (`closeups_mi_vs_rife_2b.png`, scratchpad): the two are within 41 dB of each other, RIFE has cleaner edges and less ghosting. Distinct frames per 60 in the baked wind: plain stepping 28, 21, 9, 3; blend / mci / RIFE 60 flat. **Do not trust `psnr` between two ffmpeg files of different frame counts for such comparisons** (a misaligned first run showed minterpolate ahead).
  - **Speed / load:** minterpolate is single-threaded, so `bake_wind_minterp` splits the wind part into <= 16 chunks (`BAKE_CHUNKS`) that start on source-frame boundaries (`bake_chunks`; the still's own pair stays in the previous chunk), each its own ffmpeg + H.264 part, joined by stream copy. Each chunk's source frames get timestamps WARPED onto the wall clock (`bake_chunk_filters`: flat `setpts` expression, a clone of the first frame prepended at pts 0 so minterpolate's fps=F grid anchors on the global output grid, three clones appended, `select` by frame index: `trim` end counts frame DURATIONS and lost the last frame), so the warp is continuous across joins (frame steps 1/60 s everywhere, checked with ffprobe). Chunked vs single pass: same frame count and timestamps, PSNR 46.8 dB mean, 35 dB worst (the first frames of a chunk, no predecessor; with one context frame 48.2 / 38.9: not worth the wasted interpolation), decoded output independent of the worker count (idle vs busy encodes were bit-identical). **Workers (2026-10-22, user: "I will never change wallpaper videos while gaming": NO throttling by load, fullscreen or GPU busy any more; `bake_busy_state` / `bake_gpu_free` / the "waiting for a free GPU" state are gone):** chunk workers = every core the process may use (`bake_workers`: `os.sched_getaffinity`, so cgroup / taskset aware; `$THEME_BAKE_LOAD=<n>` forces n, tests), every encode / RIFE process at `nice -n 5` (`BAKE_NICE`, the desktop stays responsive), ffmpeg additionally `ionice -c2 -n4`. RIFE: see **RIFE stage** below (the per-frame-process design, its worker rules and its speed profile are gone).
  - **RIFE stage (2026-10-22, replaces one `rife-ncnn-vulkan` launch per output frame: every launch reloaded the model and rebuilt the Vulkan pipelines, GPU busy swung 100/11/8 %, the DP-3 raycast bake took 754 s):** `RifeStore` (theme.py) plans EVERY interpolated frame of the whole bake up front (`bake_rife_plan`: first wind, per new burst its ease-in and wind-down, the ease-in again per fade, in the order `bake_encode` consumes them; part ids `w1`, `("e"|"w", i_s, n)`, `("f", j)`), and a producer thread renders them in batches while the encoders consume earlier batches. **Frames** (`rife_frames`): output frame = source pair (i, i+1) at step j / `RIFE_G` (64: max timing error 1/128 source frame); where the output steps >= `RIFE_NEAREST` (1.0) source frames per frame (full speed, the fast end of a curve: e.g. a 120 fps source on a 60 Hz bake above speed 0.5) the NEAREST source frame is shown (no RIFE; error <= 1/2 source frame there, the usual frame-rate conversion stepping). Frame indices are the ORIGINAL clip's (`"orig"` = (i_s, loop length) maps a burst's own index, wrap included), so bursts no longer need their lossless `src.nut` (made lazily only for a full-speed stretch, minterpolate or the fallback). **Arbitrary steps in ONE process (the trick, verified bit-identical to single-pair `-0 -1 -s` runs):** directory mode (`-i dir -o dir -n T`, N files sorted by name) makes output i = RIFE(L[x], L[x+1], fx - x), fx = i N / T; with N / T = (2G + 1) / G output i = qG + j lands on slot x = 2i + q at step j / G, so every output owns a slot pair: the list is HARD links (symlinks crash the tool, rc 139) to the wanted source PNGs, unused outputs get a 64x64 black dummy pair (~1.5 ms each; mixed sizes work), j = 0 is a copy without GPU. `RIFE_C` = 40 outputs per step bucket per run keeps i * N < 2^24 (the tool's float index math stays exact). `-s` is ignored in directory mode (checked). **Batches** (`RIFE_BATCH` 320 RIFE frames, the first a quarter of that so the GPU starts after ~1 s): source PNGs of batch k + 1 are decoded (ffmpeg, runs of <= `RIFE_DEC_RUN` 240 frames, 4 decoders, `-compression_level 1`, only the frames needed are kept) while batch k is on the GPU; at most three batches live, each within a third of min(40 % MemAvailable, 80 % of free /dev/shm) (`/dev/shm/theme-video-<uid>/<pid>/rife-<n>`, else `opt/.rife-<pid>-*` on disk); a batch is deleted once its parts are encoded. Items are dealt round-robin over the workers (buckets stay even); each worker run is one process. Encoders: one `image2pipe` ffmpeg per part (`rife_encode`, PNG decode `-threads 4`), rgb -> yuv with the source's matrix / tags as before. **Workers** (`rife_worker_count`): `RIFE_ARGS` = `-j 2:1:2` (ONE proc thread: each proc thread holds ~1.1 GB VRAM at 1440p, measured 1.16 / 2.26 / 4.46 GB for 1 / 2 / 4), count = floor((free VRAM - margin) / 1.3 GB scaled by pixels) clamped 1..`RIFE_WORKERS` = 4, `THEME_RIFE_WORKERS` forces; a failing process (Vulkan OOM) halves the count for the rest of the bake and its run is retried; a run failing alone twice = the RIFE stage fails, every part falls back to minterpolate (`THEME_BAKE_RIFE_OOM_ABOVE=1` with 4 workers: 4 -> 2 -> 1, bake finished). **Cancel:** the consumer's `job_stale` raises `JobCancelled`, `bake_encode` closes the store (kills only its own processes, removes its dirs): measured 0.3 s, no child process and nothing in /dev/shm left. **Source decode:** software (dav1d) by default: 240 4K AV1 frames -> 1440p PNG in 1.55 s, VA-API (`-hwaccel vaapi`, frames downloaded) 3.6 s, so VA-API is opt-in (`THEME_BAKE_HWDEC=1`, only when `hwdec_ok`, software fallback per run). **Knee** (whole DP-3 raycast bake, 16 bursts, isolated rig, idle machine; RIFE frames/s, GPU busy during RIFE, total): 1 worker 15.4/s 83 % 114.8 s; 2 16.6/s 81 % 106.0 s; 3 18.0/s 85 % 98.4 s; **4 18.9/s 87 % 95.0 s (default)**; 6 19.8/s 90 % 91.3 s; 8 20.1/s 91 % 92.0 s; 4 workers with `-j 2:4:4` (13.7 GB VRAM) thrashed: 5.7/s, 290.8 s. Raw ceiling (dir mode alone, no decode / encode): ~23 RIFE frames/s at 1440p for v4.14. **Before / after** (raycast-hero-4k120-av1, DP-3 = 2560x1440 at 60 Hz, play 0 / wind 3 / ease 0.5, 16 bursts as before): 754 s -> 95 s (1631 RIFE frames instead of ~3700: the nearest-frame rule). With the adaptive burst count (64 bursts, 6239 RIFE frames, 229 s of slow motion): 348 s, 18.6 RIFE frames/s, GPU busy 86 % during the RIFE stage (isolated rig, H.264 then; the encode is not the bottleneck). DP-1 (F 120, 21 segments, 7874 RIFE frames planned) was not baked in full: estimate ~7.5 min from the same rate. Small-clip runs: 960x540 120 fps ~100 RIFE frames/s. Test hooks: `THEME_RIFE_WORKERS`, `THEME_BAKE_KMAX`, `THEME_BAKE_RIFE_OOM_ABOVE`, `THEME_BAKE_VRAM_GB` / `_FREE_GB`, `THEME_OUT_CODEC`.
  - **Compatibility:** this machine RX 9070 XT (RDNA4, 16 GB, AV1 decode): 4 workers. Work machine RX 5700 (RDNA1, 8 GB, no AV1 decode): 3 workers by the VRAM rule (8 GB, ~6.5 free), the AV1 source still decodes in software for the bake (dav1d) and the player waits for the H.264 copy (`hwdec_ok` / `video_wait_for_copy`); RIFE speed there unmeasured (expect a slower GPU).
  - **Numbers** (user's 1080p25 clip, play 6 / wind 5 = 301 wind frames, F 60, machine not idle: load average 2.5-6 from other work): minterpolate single worker nice 19 (busy tier) 148 s; minterpolate idle tier (8 workers) 55 s; RIFE 55 s (before chunking the single pass took 95 s). GPU during the RIFE bake: mean busy 33 % (10 % for the CPU bakes), board power mean 63 W, spikes to 322 W (the ordinary desktop baseline here is 45 W); live 1440p bakes of 4K60 clips with RIFE: 78 s for a 6.5 s wind (391 frames). Sizes 0.9-17 MB.
  - **Cancel (user: "move the slider and it should stop when I let go, immediately, and bake the new file"):** the Settings sliders apply on release (`down` falling edge, `flushTiming`; while held nothing is applied). `apply` saves the state and calls `video_optimize_poke()` right after (BEFORE the slow palette / reload work): monitors whose player runs something else go first in the queue (`optimize.prio`), and the encode runner (pid in `optimize.pid`, checked against /proc cmdline) gets SIGUSR1. Its encode loops (`job_stale`: re-evaluates `opt_wanted()`, all files the monitors want now, memoized ffprobes) kill the process groups of a job whose file is no longer wanted (`proc_kill` / `procs_kill`: SIGKILL, only unreaped Popens whose /proc comm is ffmpeg / nice / ionice / rife), raise `JobCancelled`, delete the partial output (`.tmp-`, `.bake-` dirs), and the next job (the changed monitor's first) starts. Other monitors' jobs are not cancelled (verified: a running job of another monitor survived and finished, the new one queued next). A finished job re-checks `opt_wanted()` before swapping, so a stale file never swaps in (verified by changing the state without a poke: the player stayed on the runtime path); a newer bake replaces an older baked file. Runner SIGTERM / SIGINT kill its ffmpegs (no orphans). Measured in the nested rig: ffmpeg chunks killed 0.2 s after the command started (0.07 s of that is the still extraction + state save; the poke to dead processes ~0.13 s), first new process 0.3 s; RIFE job: killed 0.06 s after the poke, new process 0.02 s later; `optimize.json` shows the new label at once. A cold still extraction of the new t_stop adds 0.2-1 s before the state is even saved.
  - **Bugs found on the way (fixed):** mixed MP4 timescales (a 25 fps clip's 12800 vs 15360) made the stream-copy concat produce a 1.24 s freeze and 50 fps wind: every part is now encoded with `-video_track_timescale 90000` (divisible by 24, 25, 30, 40, 45, 50, 60); `BAKE_VERSION` is "b2" so older bakes are rebuilt. `ff_encode` with cfr rounding next to a source pts dropped a frame (wind parts use passthrough).
  - **Swap / cache:** until the bake is ready the player runs the runtime ramp from the original / plain copy; the swap is `script-message swap <file> baked`: at once while the clip is in its play part (`loadfile ... start=time-pos`, verified at 2.03 s), else pending until the next replay (never replaces a held or winding frame), clears `end=` and the timer. A finished bake only reaches players whose entry still has that play / wind. Cache key = path, mtime, size, size, A fps, scale, F, play, wind, t_stop, last, interpolator (+ RIFE model) + its options, segment count, `BAKE_VERSION`; `opt_trim` also deletes stale `.bake-` / `.rife-` / `.tmp-` leftovers older than a day.
  - **Verified** (nested Hyprland on DP-3's workspace, isolated HOME / XDG, headless 2560x1440 at 360 and 60 Hz): baked file plays at speed 1.0 the whole time, 60 distinct frames per second through the wind at both refresh rates, ends at play + W and holds, replay restarts it; last frame vs the source frame / still: minterpolate 43.3 dB, RIFE 41.5 dB (held output vs the still 27 dB: only hyprpaper-vs-mpv scaling of fine lines); play 0 / wind 3 runtime: wind starts at 0.000, stops at 1.467 (end 1.5). Live (2026-10-22): the user's 10221 clip (play 0, wind 6.5) baked with RIFE in 78 s, both players on it: speed 1.0, duration 6.517, held at 6.5, 391 frames with constant 1/60 steps, last frame vs the still 42.6 dB; `qs log` shows no SettingsWallpaper errors. **Not verified:** a pointer drag of the sliders (state-driven screenshots only), RIFE under a really running game (the busy rule is unit-checked with CPU hogs only; GPU busy / fullscreen detection are simple reads), scene cuts inside the wind (minterpolate duplicates there; RIFE untested), the real 360 Hz panel's power with the glass bar.
- **Replay bursts (2026-10-22, user: "I want just really short wind-downs when switching workspaces", "advance along the loop"; replaces the hard cut back to frame 0, then the full-cycle / loop-point-search / fade-at-start designs, all removed):** after the first play holds on H (media `t_stop`), every workspace switch plays one short baked BURST and holds again. Burst = ease-in from rest (speed 0 -> 1 over `ease`, per monitor `--ease`, default `replay_ease_in` = 0.5 s) immediately followed by the cosine wind-down (1 -> 0 over `wind`), NO full-speed part in between (both mirrored cosines, symmetric), so a switch advances d = ease / 2 + wind / 2 media seconds (ease 0.5 + wind 3 = 1.75 s) and the clip holds on that frame; the next switch continues from there. A burst is at least `BURST_MIN` = 1 s on screen (both 0 = one plain second; a missing wind is lengthened to the minimum). **File:** `[first play: A1 + W1 (ends on H)][burst 0][burst 1] ...`, sidecar `<bake>.json` = `{t2 (= start of burst 0), bursts [{start, end, i_s, n, fade}], cycle_from, d, text, loop, tag}`. The first frame of burst i IS the source frame burst i-1 held (same frame, its own encode): joints measured 51.5 dB (lucys, every joint and the cycle wrap), 53-54 dB (non-loop, 45.6 dB where a fade burst starts), 138 dB (the 120 fps clip). Every burst is baked from its own lossless source NUT (`bake_src_clip`: frames i_s .. i_s + n at the output size, exact CFR timestamps, the source's colour tags), so a burst that crosses the loop's wrap just continues through frame 0 and the retiming math (`bake_breaks`: wind curve, or curve "in" for the ease-in; RIFE v4.14 / minterpolate unchanged) works on plain virtual times from 0.
  - **Stations (`bake_bursts`):** S_0 = H (frame `last`), S_{i+1} = S_i + n_i. **Loops** (`loop_wrap`: last -> first step is a normal step, cut <= 6 x the median step or <= 0.3 mean luma difference; of the user's 13 clips loops are 10221, 2b-graceful-power, 2b-sword-flowers, 2e961817, 7af08f74, f465cfd1, fba523c3, frieren-dark, frieren-fallen-citadel, lucys, NOT nier, A8B4A45F, frieren-and-fern): stations wrap around the clip (an identical last == first frame is dropped from the loop), `K = round(L / d)` bursts cover exactly one loop with d = L / K, only K segments are baked and the cycle repeats (K bursts return to station 0). K is capped by `burst_kmax` (bake cost: every burst is RIFE-interpolated): as many bursts as `BURST_RIFE_BUDGET` = 8000 RIFE frames allow, clamped to `KMAX_MIN` 16 .. `KMAX` 64 (16 without RIFE; `THEME_BAKE_KMAX=n` forces n, tests). Raycast 4K120 at play 0 / wind 3 / ease 0.5: 98 RIFE frames per burst on the 60 Hz monitor = 64 bursts (112 s of the 120 s clip), ~400 per burst at 120 fps on the 360 Hz one = 20 bursts. A loop that needs more than the cap (e.g. 2b-sword-flowers, 140 s at 1.75 s per step = 80) is NOT covered round and round, it is walked like a non-loop clip over the cap's bursts and then restarts at S_0 with the cut / fade rule below. There is never a full-speed part except for ease + wind < 1 s (`BURST_MIN`). **Non-loop clips** advance until the next burst would cross the end, then START OVER from S_0 (cleaner than going on to the start in the middle of a burst: no burst has to cross the end): the jump from the last station back to S_0 is measured (cut = mean absolute luma difference at 640 px vs the median step after it): clean cut (`CUT_ABS` 0.6 / `CUT_RATIO` 10) = burst 0 again (`cycle_from` 0), else an extra segment R = burst 0 whose ease-in part is a FADE from the held frame (`blend`, eased weight, frame 0 = the held frame; the clip already moves under it; the plain / wind parts are burst 0's, shared) and the cycle continues at 1 (`cycle_from` 1): bursts 0, 1, ..., m-1, R, 1, 2, ... (verified live in the rig on frieren-and-fern: 0,1,...,8 then 1,2). When H is within one burst of the end (full length), the stations start at frame 0 and the first replay is a fade burst from H. Non-loop clips get an ease-in of at least 0.4 s (a fade needs motion under it).
  - **Player (winddown.lua):** `winddown-bursts` = "t0/.../tK" (burst starts + file end), `-cyc`, `-station` (`<runtime>/theme-video/<conn>.station` = "<tag>:<index of the NEXT burst>", written at every burst start; the runtime dir is per login, so it resets on login/reboot where first play holds at S_0 again), `-tag` (the bake's hash). First play = `end=t0` (holds on H). `winddown-restart` = `burst_play`: `end` = the burst's end (the next burst's start; `none` for the last), seek to its start, unpause, advance the index (after the last: `cycle_from`). A swap that waited for the replay (`swap <file> baked <bursts> <cyc> <tag> <station file>`) plays the burst from the swap; a mid-first-play swap keeps the position and sets `end` to t0. **A player restart resumes the station** (`vp_start`: station file with the same tag and index > 0 -> `start=<burst start - 2 ms> pause=yes`: held on the frame the last burst ended on, no first play; verified: resumed at 16.867 s, the next switch played burst 4); a new bake or a changed entry starts with the first play. **The still and palette stay the first play's hold (S_0): no re-theming per station.** Before the bake is ready the runtime path is unchanged (a replay = hard seek to 0 and the speed script; bursts only exist baked).
  - **Trigger (`scripts/video-replay.lua`):** EVERY `workspace.active` change on a monitor triggers its replay (the emptiness check and window counting are gone), except special workspaces (scratchpad toggles); `config/theme/mpv/replay.py <conn>` (= `theme.py video replay`) ignores it while the previous burst plays (the debounce). A workspace moved to another monitor fires the event only for monitors whose active workspace changed. Verified in the nested rig (kitty windows on two workspaces): every switch between two OCCUPIED workspaces plays a burst, a special-workspace open + close plays none, 12 rapid flicks play exactly one.
  - **Settings:** the Ease-in slider (Off .. 1.0 s, per monitor, release applies at once, cancels and re-bakes) now sets the ease-in of every burst; the read-only line (`theme.py video loopinfo <video> <play|full> <wind> [ease] [monitor key|connector]`; Settings passes the edited monitor, whose fps / size decide the burst count, and refreshes when the monitor card changes) reads "each switch advances ~1.7 s (5 steps per loop)" or "... (8 steps, then back to the start with a fade)".
  - **Measured (RIFE v4.14, nested 360 Hz):** lucys (loop, play 0 / wind 3 / ease 0.5): K = 5, d = 1.73 s, 17 s of slow motion, 83.6 MB, bake 292 s (4K source); motion per 1/60 s of a burst 0.0 -> 1.1 -> 0.0 (no spikes beyond the 30 fps cadence), 7 consecutive switches: stations 1,2,3,4,0,1,2 (the 6th burst starts exactly where the first did: wrap); frieren-and-fern (non-loop, 4 s, full length): 9 segments, d = 0.5 s, bake 154 s, motion of the fade bursts ramps like a plain one. Bake cost grows with K (see **RIFE stage** for the current numbers).
  - **120 fps rule:** F = 120 (the bake fps and the optimized copy's) ONLY when the source is >= 119 fps AND the refresh is a whole multiple of 120 (`fps120`: the 360 Hz panel, not the 60 Hz one); 60 fps sources stay at 60 (`opt_fps` and the bake's F). None of the user's clips is 120 fps (max 60: 10221 and others); a generated 120 fps clip verified the path on a 360 Hz output: F = 120, every frame step 1/120 s (797 frames), avg frame rate 120/1, joints identical, the runtime fallback (original played with the speed script) winds down and stops at 1.742 (planned 1.75) and a replay seeks 0; no optimized copy is needed there (same size, same rate).
  - **Colour bug fixed on the way:** the default yuv -> rgb24 PNG conversion came back 1.7 luma levels DARKER through the round trip (a luma step at every seam between interpolated and direct frames); extraction uses `RGB_UP` (full range, accurate) and the encoder `bake_rgb_down` (the source's matrix / range / tags): bias -1.68 -> -0.01.
  - **Caveats / not verified:** the very first `theme.py video sync` after a theme.py change sometimes did not start the encode runner (a second call did; the cause is unknown, `opt_jobs` was non-empty); the runtime fallback does not do bursts; the bursts on the live display (numbers and player state only, no screenshots of the wallpaper).
- **Replay on every workspace switch (2026-10-22, was: only on switches to an EMPTY workspace):** `scripts/video-replay.lua` (required with `pcall(require, ...)` in hyprland.lua) subscribes to `workspace.active` (subscription kept in `_G.__video_replay_sub` and removed before re-subscribing, so a reload does not stack handlers). Every active-workspace change on a monitor except special workspaces runs detached `exec python3 -I -S "$HOME/.config/theme/mpv/replay.py" <connector>` (exec_cmd itself is `/bin/sh -c`; connector validated against `^[%w_.-]+$`, <= 40); no emptiness check. **Hyprland Lua API objects (monitors, workspaces, windows) are userdata:** `type(x) == "table"` is false, use nil checks and pcall. `mpv/replay.py <conn>` (the one implementation; `theme.py video replay` loads it via importlib) checks the pid file (`mode` once, `/proc/<pid>/comm` mpvpaper) and talks mpv JSON IPC on `<runtime>/theme-video/<conn>.sock`: `get_property pause` must be true (= holding; playing = ignored, which is the debounce: a replay unpauses at once), then `script-message winddown-restart` (= the next burst, see Replay bursts; without a baked file: speed script from 0). Loop / image monitors have no socket or no player: nothing happens. A monitor following the primary's video is a once player of its own and replays too.
- **Replay latency (2026-10-22):** *When does the event fire?* Hyprland fork `src/output/Monitor.cpp` `CMonitor::changeWorkspace`: `startAnimation()` OUT/IN at :1392-1393, then (same function, same main-loop iteration, before any animated frame is rendered) socket2 `workspace`/`workspacev2` :1428-1429 (written synchronously, `ipc/s2/Unix.cpp` addEvent -> flush) and `bus workspace.active` :1430 -> Lua `hl.on("workspace.active")` dispatched synchronously (`LuaEventHandler.cpp:151`). No earlier hook is needed. `hl.exec_cmd` = fork + `execl /bin/sh -c` (`Executor.cpp` spawnRawProc). *Measured* (socket2 workspacev2 arrival -> mpv `pause` false, real `focus({workspace=8})` on DP-3, Optimize encoder running): before 52 ms (python startup 6 + compiling 4.3k-line theme.py ~32 + json/re/subprocess imports ~10); after (mpv/replay.py: `-I -S`, only `_socket`, no json: substring checks on theme.py's own json.dumps output and mpv's compact replies) 4.6-7.1 ms. The rest is mpv: `winddown-restart` seeks (`absolute+exact`) before playing, the first new frame (`seeking` false) lands 8-20 ms after the unpause (decode from the keyframe). Verified: debounce (switch back at +0.5 s: no restart), loop pid file / dead pid / missing file / bad names -> no-op (fake runtime dir with a symlinked socket), `theme.py video replay` still works (~90 ms), players held paused at the same time-pos afterwards. Further cuts would need a persistent listener (Quickshell Socket to the mpv sockets, saves the ~5 ms spawn) or skipping the seek in winddown.lua when the burst starts at the held frame (contiguous bursts; unverified that mpv resumes past `end=` without one).
- **Replay on launcher open (2026-10-22):** `Launcher.qml` `onOpening` (every open path) calls `replayWallpaper()`: when the focused monitor's active workspace has no toplevels (`Hyprland.focusedMonitor.activeWorkspace.toplevels`), a one-shot `Process` runs `python3 -I -S theme/mpv/replay.py <connector>` (argv, connector validated like video-replay.lua). Verified live only the negative case (focused workspace with 1 window: mpv `time-pos` stayed 64.349989 across `launcher toggle`, no QML errors on reload); the empty-workspace case was not run live (no empty focused workspace; no workspace switching allowed).
- **Optimize for monitor (2026-10-21, default on; `theme.py video optimize [on|off]`, stored as `optimize` in wallpapers.json, toggling restarts the players):** per (video, monitor) copy in `$XDG_CACHE_HOME/theme/video/opt/<stem>-<WxH>-<fps|src>-<hash>.mp4`, key = path, mtime, size, target size, fps, scale flag, codec, `OPT_VERSION`. Size = the monitor's PHYSICAL pixels from `hyprctl monitors -j` (width/height swapped for odd `transform`), scale-to-cover (lanczos) + centre crop so mpv does no scaling / panscan; only when the source is bigger than the monitor (smaller sources are not upscaled, mpv does that). fps = largest of 25/30/40/45/50/60 that is <= the source (+0.5 %) and divides the refresh within 1.5 % (59.95 Hz ok); frames are dropped by the `fps` filter, never blended; none fits (24p on 60 Hz, 60 fps on 143.98 Hz) = keep the source rate; the same rate as the source = no fps filter (timestamps untouched, `-fps_mode passthrough`). Examples (checked): 60/360 -> 60, 30/360 -> 30, 50/59.951 -> 30, 60/59.951 -> 60, 24/60 -> source, 30/75 -> 25, 60/50 -> 50. No copy when nothing would change. **Codec: HEVC since the 2026-10-22 rework (see Output codec HEVC), H.264 as the fallback; the H.264 history:** (probed per machine since 2026-10-22, see **Encoders, decode and H.264 level**; here `h264_vaapi -qp 20 -profile high -bf 0 -g 120 -flags +cgop -level 5.2`, render node `renderD128` = the RX 9070 XT, `THEME_VAAPI_DEVICE` overrides, libx264 fallback), 1440p60 test clip, whole-GPU power (noisy, live desktop shares the GPU) 4K60 original 57.0 W / 17.1 % busy vs 1440p copy h264 54.5 W / 15.3 %, hevc 54.6 W / 15.2 %, av1 54.8 W / 15.2 %: codecs are indistinguishable, so the most compatible one; size of the 10 s test clip h264 2.56 MB, hevc 2.06 MB, av1 3.52 MB. The `drm-engine-enc` (= decode on VCN) fdinfo counter was too erratic to use (80 % vs 22 % on identical runs); mpvpaper CPU 2-3 % in all cases. av1_vaapi's width padding was not hit (1440p is a multiple of 8, the plan rounds sizes to even). **Quality** vs the lanczos reference (10 s): 4K60 -> 1440p PSNR 52.7 dB / SSIM 0.996, 4K30 nier -> 1440p 46.2 dB (min 40.5) / SSIM 0.989; colour tags and frame count / duration identical (731 frames, 24.367 s). **Encode** (VA-API, nice 19 + ionice idle): 30 s 4K60 56.7 MB -> 7.7 MB in 5.7 s; 24 s 4K30 45.6 MB -> 17.5 MB in 2.8 s; cache capped at `OPT_CACHE_MB` (4096) by LRU mtime (`opt_cached` touches). **When:** apply plays the ORIGINAL at once; `video_sync` then spawns the detached runner `video optimize-run` (flock: one at a time, loops until no copy is missing, `.fail` marker beside a failed copy), progress in `<runtime>/theme-video/optimize.json` (`{state, label, pct}`, shown in Settings), and on each finished copy sends `script-message swap <file>` to the players that run that video: loop = at the wrap (position jumps back; verified `swap ... at 0.000`), once = at once while it plays and is not winding down (verified at 5.8 s of a 10 s + wind clip, wind then ran on the copy), else at the next replay (the held frame is never touched). Later starts use the copy directly. Chosen over "encode first behind an Optimizing state" because the first play must not wait for an encode and the swap points are invisible. Same timeline as the source (no trim) so hold, t_stop and the black-tail scan stay valid; **stills and the palette still come from the ORIGINAL** (one still serves every monitor an entry shows on, and the palette must not shift when a copy finishes), so the hyprpaper underlay is not pixel-identical to a copy's frame (cover-scaled by hyprpaper; held-vs-still PSNR 36.5 dB on the swapped 1440p clip). Not done: pixel-matched per-monitor stills.
- **Optimize speed (2026-10-22, user: "do it slowly in a background (by default)", then "an in-between: take 60% of resources, but keep my desktop fully responsive"):** Settings > Wallpaper > Videos box, row "Speed" under Optimize (only while Optimize is on): 3-way `Segmented` Background (default) / Balanced / Fast; `theme.py video optimize-speed background|balanced|fast` (prints the value), stored as `optimize_speed` in wallpapers.json (absent = background; `save_wp_state` omits the default). Changing it never applies / restarts anything: it saves the state and SIGUSR1s the runner. Spawned processes get `job_prio()`: **Background** = `chrt -i 0 nice -n 19 ionice -c3` (SCHED_IDLE), ffmpeg decoder `-threads 2`, `-filter_threads` / `-filter_complex_threads 2`, chunk workers cores // 4, PNG decoders 1, RIFE workers 1. **Balanced** = `chrt -b 0 nice -n 19 ionice -c2 -n7` (SCHED_BATCH: still a small guaranteed share while the desktop is busy, never preempts a waking interactive thread; IDLE would stall it whenever the desktop uses CPU), every count = 60 % of fast (`BALANCED_FRAC`, `speed_scale`; threads >= 2, RIFE >= 1). No cgroup CPUQuota (a per-process scope does not add up to a job-wide cap; a runner-wide scope would need its own unit + `set-property` per switch). **Fast** = the previous behaviour (nice `BAKE_NICE` 5, ffmpeg encodes `ionice -c2 -n4`, every core, VRAM-sized RIFE count). Output identical in all modes (encoder args untouched). **Live switch:** SIGUSR1 sets `repri`; `job_stale()` (encode loops, ~0.1 s) / the job loop call `opt_speed_refresh` -> `opt_prio_apply` (`speed_prio`: policy + nice of every thread in the runner's child process groups, `ionice -P <pgid>`); thread / worker counts change only for processes spawned afterwards (bake chunks re-evaluate every second). Raising priority back needs RLIMIT_NICE >= 15 (`ulimit -e` = 31 here); failures ignored. **Measured** (16 cores, scratch XDG dirs, functions called directly, testsrc2 clips): 4K60 -> 1440p hevc_vaapi copy, idle machine: background 15 ffmpeg threads 4.0 s, balanced 39 threads 2.1 s, fast 57 threads 2.2 s; while a nice-5 RIFE bake loaded the machine (load ~39): 12.6 / 12.1 / 5.8 s (low modes yield, as intended). 1080p30 RIFE bake, idle machine: background 39.5 s (1 RIFE, GPU busy 82 %), balanced 27.9 s (2 RIFE, 91 %), fast 27.3 s (4 RIFE, 92 %): GPU-bound, so balanced costs ~nothing here. Decoded md5 identical across all three (copy and bake). Live switches checked on a running copy: bg->fast, bal->bg, fast->bal, bg->bal (policy / nice / ionice flipped within 1 s). **Caveats:** the GPU is not throttled in any mode beyond the RIFE count (one RIFE alone ~82-93 % busy; amdgpu has no userland priority for another process's Vulkan compute, RIFE tiling would change the output, fewer ncnn threads only slow the CPU side). A runner started by older code keeps its behaviour until it exits. Not verified: real clicks (state set via the CLI, the page followed the file), a live switch during a chunked bake, desktop frame times under load.
- **Encoders, decode and level (2026-10-22, user: the work machine may have no AV1 / maybe no HEVC hardware, never assume; HEVC output since the RIFE-stage rework, see **Output codec HEVC** below):** H.264 is the FALLBACK output codec (when this machine cannot hardware-decode HEVC Main or encode HEVC at all); the H.264 chain below is unchanged. `ff_tries` order = the encoders that work on this machine out of `h264_vaapi` -> `h264_nvenc` -> `h264_qsv`, then `libx264` (always last = the runtime fallback when a hardware encoder exits at startup: `ff_encode` walks the list). Which work is PROBED once (`enc_probe`: a real 3-frame 720p test encode with the actual argument set to `-f null`) and cached as `<runtime>/theme-video/probe-enc-<encoder>-<node>` = yes|no (delete to re-probe; `THEME_FORCE_ENCODER=<name>` forces one + libx264). Every encode sets `-level 5.2` (verified: ffprobe `High,52` on the copy and the bake; bake logs `H.264 profile,level`). **Level guard:** H.264 level 5.2 allows 2,073,600 macroblocks/s and 36,864 per frame; `h264_fps_cap(w, h, fps)` returns the allowed rate (2560x1440@120 = 1.73M ok, 3840x2160@120 = 3.89M NOT: capped to 60; the size alone over 36,864 MB returns None = no cap possible). It is applied to the optimized copy's fps (`opt_plan`) and to the bake's F (`bake_plan_base`, `f_cap`, logged `exceeds H.264 level 5.2: baked at 60 fps`); the cap is in the cache keys through fps / F. **Hardware decode of the SOURCE** (`hwdec_ok`, cached `probe-hwdec-<codec><depth>-<node>`): `vainfo --display drm --device <node>` must list the codec's profile with `VAEntrypointVLD` (H.264 High/Main/CB, HEVC Main / Main10, AV1 Profile0, VP9 Profile0 / 2), or nvidia-smi present (NVDEC: h264 / hevc / vp9 always, av1 from RTX 30xx); anything else, unknown codecs and a missing vainfo = NOT decodable. `THEME_FORCE_NO_HWDEC=av1,hevc` (or `all`) simulates. A source that cannot be decoded gets an H.264 copy even when nothing needs scaling or an fps change (`opt_plan`). **Playback gate (`video_wait_for_copy`):** Optimize on + no copy yet + original not hardware-decodable = the player is NOT started with the original (4K 120 fps AV1 in software would hammer the CPU); the still stays (hyprpaper), `video sync` logs it and kicks the runner, whose label reads "... (playback starts when ready)" in Settings, and when the copy is done the runner calls `video_sync(only=conn)` which starts the player on it (the bake swaps in later as before; a failed copy = `.fail` marker = the original plays after all). Optimize OFF + not decodable: the original plays anyway, `theme: warning ... playing it in software (high CPU)` on stderr and Settings > Optimize shows "Off: this video has no hardware decoding here, so the original plays in software (high CPU)." (`theme.py video hwdec <file>` prints `yes|no <codec>`). **Verified** in the nested rig with `THEME_FORCE_NO_HWDEC=av1 THEME_BAKE_VRAM_GB=8 THEME_BAKE_VRAM_FREE_GB=6.5` and the raycast 4K120 AV1 clip: no player started, label "(playback starts when ready)", 49 s later the 1080p120 H.264 copy (High, level 52) played (mpv `path` = the copy), then the bake started with `RIFE workers 4` (1080p; at 1440p the 8 GB card gets 2). Not verified: nvenc / qsv encoders (no such GPU here; the probe protects), NVDEC mapping, a real RX 5700.
- **Output codec HEVC (2026-10-22, user: smaller files, then "no visible quality loss at all, visually lossless"):** optimized copies and bakes are HEVC Main 8-bit when `out_codec()` says so: `hwdec_codec_ok("hevc", 8)` (vainfo `VAProfileHEVCMain` VLD / NVDEC) AND one HEVC encoder works (probed like H.264: `hevc_vaapi` -> `hevc_nvenc` -> `hevc_qsv` -> `libx265`), else the H.264 chain; `THEME_OUT_CODEC=h264|hevc` forces (tests); `THEME_FORCE_NO_HWDEC=hevc` -> h264 (checked). Codec and QP are in both cache keys (`opt_path`, `bake_path`), so the next sync rebuilds every H.264 copy / bake. **Level** (`enc_job` per job from size and the file's HIGHEST rate, so every concat part shares encoder / profile / level / timescale): 5.1 up to 534,773,760 luma samples/s (1440p60, 1440p120, 4K60), 5.2 up to 1,069,547,520 (4K120), max 8,912,896 samples/frame (`hevc_fps_cap` caps the fps beyond, replacing the H.264 level guard via `enc_fps_cap`). `-tag:v hvc1`. **Gotcha:** `hevc_vaapi` + `hvc1` gave UNDECODABLE files at any QP but 26 (`slice_qp -2 is outside the valid range`: the hvcC header came from the driver's default PPS, init_qp 26); `-bsf:v extract_extradata` puts the real parameter sets into hvcC (0 decode errors). **Quality target:** p5 (worst 5 % frames) PSNR >= 50 dB and p5 SSIM >= 0.995 against the lanczos-scaled source, and against the UNCOMPRESSED RIFE output for interpolated frames; measured on 2 s segments (raycast 4K120 -> 1440p60, 2b-sword-flowers 1080p, 30 RIFE frames of 2b at t = 0.5), `hevc_vaapi` CQP, p5 PSNR / p5 SSIM / Mbit/s: raycast q12 54.6/0.9970/9.8, q14 54.0/0.9967/8.1, **q16 53.2/0.9962/6.5**, q17 52.9/0.9960/6.0, q18 50.2/0.9926/3.0 (fails), q20 49.9/0.9922/2.5; 2b q16 59.0/0.9995/4.6; RIFE q16 59.4/0.9995/4.4. The previous H.264 (`h264_vaapi` qp 20) for comparison: raycast 50.4/0.9930/3.3 (fails the SSIM target), 2b 57.0/0.9992/2.3, RIFE 57.4/0.9993/2.3. Chosen **`OPT_QP_HEVC` = 16** (18 is a cliff on raycast: the driver's rate steps), libx265 fallback **crf 14** (`-preset fast`, keyint 120, no B-frames; raycast 53.0/0.9960/7.4, 2b 54.7/0.9982, RIFE 54.7/0.9984; crf 16 is borderline at 0.9950). Files are ~2x the old H.264 ones (user: quality first). Dark-gradient crop (raycast lower left, 512x384, 5x contrast boost, looked at): source and HEVC q16 keep the grain, no banding; H.264 qp 20 visibly smooths / blocks it (crop PSNR 48.2 vs 45.2 dB).
- **Verification of the rework (2026-10-22, small inputs: the first 10 s of raycast as `ray10.mp4` (AV1 4K120, stream copy), a 960x540 120 fps cut, a 4 s 960x540 ping-pong loop of 2b; isolated HOME / XDG; nested Hyprland floated on DP-3's visible workspace, headless TEST 960x540@360 + the window output):** plan level (`plancheck.py`, scratchpad): every burst starts on the frame the previous one held, positions monotone, speed 0 -> peak (end of the ease-in) -> 0, last frame = i_s + n at step 0, loop wrap (lucys: 5 bursts, the last crosses 259 -> 0 and ends on station 0). Rendered (HEVC, 120 fps): `hevc,Main,hvc1,level 153`, 0 decode errors, every frame step 1/120 s, joints 53-60 dB (same source frame encoded twice), first play's held frame vs the source frame 53.5 dB, loop wrap held-vs-station-0 55.8 dB. Nested: the 120 fps clip on the 360 Hz output bakes at F 120 (60 Hz monitors F 60); a source without hardware decode (an H.264 High 10 cut, and `THEME_FORCE_NO_HWDEC=av1` on the AV1 cut with `THEME_BAKE_VRAM_GB=8`) shows the still until the copy is ready, then plays the HEVC copy with `Using hardware decoding (vaapi)`; first switch swapped the bake in at burst 0, two spaced switches = 2 bursts, 6 flicks in 0.3 s = 1 burst, special workspace open + close = 0; `video restart` resumed paused at 7.525 s (burst 3 starts 7.533), the next switch played burst 3, station file `<tag>:4`; the Settings line reads "each switch advances ~1.0 s (4 steps per loop)" (screenshot of the nested output). **Not verified:** a full live re-bake (user runs it: `python3 ~/.config/theme/theme.py video sync`), the RX 5700, nvenc / qsv / libx265 in a real bake (only probed / measured standalone), HEVC level 5.2 at real 4K120 output (only the level math).

- **How to test (nested):** harness copies in the session scratchpad `video-wp/rig/` (`up.sh`, `nh.sh`, `t.sh`, `hp.sh`, `qs.sh`, `meas.sh`, `endtest.sh`; they expect `/tmp/vw`): nested `Hyprland -c` with its own `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR`, `hyprctl output create headless` x2, window floated on the secondary monitor with `hyprctl dispatch "hl.dsp.window.move/float/resize({window='address:0x..', ...})"`, isolated HOME with a copy of `config/theme` and `quickshell` and a fake `~/Videos/wallpapers`; `theme.py` copy patched so it never runs `pkill -x`, gsettings or kwriteconfig; `grim -o <output>` on the nested socket; end-of-video check with `wf-recorder -o <output> -D -c libx264rgb` + ffmpeg `signalstats`.

## Corne keyboard access (per host, 2026-10-19)
Per-host rule files live in `hosts/<Host>/etc/udev/rules.d/`. `install.sh` does not install them (the agent may not use sudo); copy by hand with the command in each file's header, then replug the keyboard. Each host only gets its own files.
- **Workstation (`hosts/CrossWorkstation`): Corne with Vial firmware.** The Corne (foostan, USB 4653:0001, serial `vial:<id>`) exposes hidraw1 as the Vial raw HID interface (report descriptor starts `06 60 ff 09 61`). hidraw nodes are `root:root 0600` with no uaccess tag, so the Vial web app (WebHID) hangs on "connecting". Fix: `50-vial.rules` (uaccess + MODE 0660). Install: `sudo cp hosts/CrossWorkstation/etc/udev/rules.d/50-vial.rules /etc/udev/rules.d/ && sudo udevadm control --reload-rules && sudo udevadm trigger --subsystem-match=hidraw`. Not yet verified after install; check with `getfacl /dev/hidraw1`. Use Chromium or Brave (WebHID), not Firefox. `vial-gui` is not in the official repos (AUR/AppImage).
- **Battlestation (`hosts/CrossBattlestation`): Corne with VIA firmware**, same USB id 4653:0001. `61-corne-nojoystick.rules` clears `ID_INPUT_JOYSTICK` on its input nodes (no joystick device for games); `62-corne-via.rules` tags its hidraw node `uaccess` so VIA (WebHID) can connect. Installed on this machine as copies in `/etc/udev/rules.d/`. Install elsewhere: `sudo cp hosts/CrossBattlestation/etc/udev/rules.d/6*-corne-*.rules /etc/udev/rules.d/ && sudo udevadm control --reload-rules && sudo udevadm trigger --subsystem-match=input --subsystem-match=hidraw`.
- The Vial rule must not be installed on the battlestation, nor the VIA rules on the workstation.

## Zellij claude-tabs plugin (standalone package, 2026-10-20)
The claude-tabs tab bar (Claude Code status, WC3 races/voice lines, icon font) is its OWN package, checked out at `~/code/crossatko/zellij-wc3cz-claude` (to be published as a separate repo). All of its code, scripts, sounds, icon font, docs (`README.md`) and history/notes (`AGENTS.md`) live there; read that file before working on the plugin. Nothing of it is in this repo any more.
- **Dotfiles side:** `config/zellij/config.kdl` alias `claude-tabs location="file:~/.config/zellij/plugins/claude-tabs/claude-tabs.wasm"` with options; `default_layout "claude"` uses the dotfiles' own `config/zellij/layouts/claude.kdl` (copy of the package example; `layouts/nvim.kdl` also uses the plugin). `config/zellij/plugins/claude-tabs` is a host-local symlink to the checkout, created by the package `setup.sh`, git-ignored (`.gitignore: config/zellij/plugins/`). Kept that path so the permission grant and running sessions stay valid. `config/kitty/kitty.conf` keeps `symbol_map U+1F7E0-U+1F7E6 WC3 Icons`.
- **install.sh** runs `$CLAUDE_TABS_DIR/setup.sh` (default `~/code/crossatko/zellij-wc3cz-claude`) if it exists, else prints a hint (without the checkout use `default_layout "compact"`). setup.sh links, installs the font, pre-grants permissions and sets the Claude Code hooks.
- **Live files outside the repo:** `~/.claude/settings.json` hooks call `~/code/crossatko/zellij-wc3cz-claude/claude-hook.sh <event>` (backup before the move: `settings.json.bak-claude-tabs-move-20261020`).
- `.gitattributes` (binary flac/wav/wasm/ttf/webp/png) stays; most of those file types now live in the package.
- **Install check 2026-10-20 (supersedes the `~/.claude/settings.json` hooks line above):** the hooks come from the Claude Code PLUGIN `zellij-wc3cz@zellij-wc3cz` (enabled in both `~/.claude` and `~/.claude-personal`; no `claude-hook.sh` entries in any settings.json, so no double registration), the bar wasm is the checkout via the host-local `plugins/claude-tabs` symlink (identical to a fresh `cargo build`), permissions are pre-granted for that path, WC3 Icons font + fontconfig installed. kitty needs BOTH `symbol_map` lines (U+1F7E0-1F7E6 race fallbacks, U+1FA70-1FAD6 character icons; restart kitty after changing). Verified by screenshot of an isolated kitty+zellij session (coloured portraits, tinted tabs, no permission prompt). The plugin cache copy of `claude-agents.sh` had a newer uncommitted version than the checkout; it was copied into the checkout (uncommitted there).

## Raycast-style launcher (`Launcher.qml`, `launcher/`, 2026-10-20, UI milestone)
- **What:** the launcher (SUPER+D via apps.lua `menu`; the old `Menu.qml` was removed 2026-10-20). `qs ipc call launcher toggle` (also `hide`; `show` is defined but the `qs ipc call launcher show` CLI prints the target help instead of calling it, use `toggle`). A `ModalMenu` (kind `launcher`, layer `qs-launcher`, covered by the generic `qs-.*` blur rule, no rules.lua change), 640px card, 8 visible rows (scrolls), rows = icon, name, subtitle, source label, `KeyBadge` 1-9.
- **Per-host launcher screen (2026-10-20):** `~/.config/launcher/config.json` (host-local, not in the repo) `{ "screen": "<text>" }` picks the launcher monitor by case-insensitive substring of its model (or exact connector name); unset or no match = the screen at 0,0. Set on the multi-monitor desktop to the primary OLED monitor model (the default would pick the monitor at 0,0 above it). Verified: with the other monitor focused the `qs-launcher` layer is on the primary one (`hyprctl layers -j`). Not screenshotted (the layer is full-monitor, would capture windows).
- **Screen + reserved space (2026-10-20):** `ModalMenu` got two opt-in properties, used only by `Launcher.qml` (Menu / modals unchanged): `fixedScreen` (open there instead of the focused monitor; `toggle()` on an open launcher on another screen moves it instead of closing) and `respectReserved` (`exclusiveZone` 0 instead of -1, so the full-anchor surface is laid out in the usable area: inside BarReserve's zone and the monitor `reserved_area`, e.g. the pinned-video strip; the card is `centerIn` that area). Main display = the screen at 0,0 (Hyprland `monitors.primary` is at 0x0 on workstation/VM hosts; QML has no primary marker; on the default desktop host monitor1 is at 0x1440, so there it resolves to the Dell, adjust `fixedScreen` if wrong). Measured on DP-2 (3440x1440, reserved right 866, test strip bottom 40): launcher layer 0,0 2574x1400 (before: full 3440x1440 by code, not measured). The click-away area shrinks to the usable area too. Not verified: visual screenshot, toggle-move between screens, real BarReserve (scratch used a stand-in strip).
- **Keys:** Up/Down, Ctrl+J/N down, Ctrl+P up (Ctrl+K is the action panel, so it is not "up" here), Enter runs, Esc / Ctrl+C closes, Ctrl+1..9 runs visible row N, Right arrow (`keyRight()`, only when it would not move the caret: empty filter or caret at the end without selection) opens the actions of the selected item (Run, Pin/Unpin, Hide, Open .desktop location for apps), Ctrl+K is the alias; Esc/Ctrl+K/Left closes the panel (Left = `keyLeft()`); footer hint shows "→ options" ("→ broadcasts" on Twitch channel rows, `rightHint`), Up on an empty search at the first row recalls previous queries (Up older, Down newer), PageUp/PageDown, Ctrl+U/W.
- **Sources (pluggable):** `launcher/AppsSource.qml` (DesktopEntries; keywords = genericName + comment + Keywords; incl. appending to `~/.cache/qmenu/drun-history`), `CommandsSource.qml` (kind `command`: power menu, sleep/shutdown/restart/exit power commands, wallpaper/theme = `qs ipc call picker toggle`), `WebSource.qml` (Google / DuckDuckGo rows appended after the ranked results while a query exists, `xdg-open`). A source is a QtObject with `items` (array), optional `dynamic(query)` and `run(item)` returning true when it handled the item; add it to `sourceList` in `Launcher.qml`. Item ids are `app:<desktop id>`, `cmd:<name>`, `web:<engine>` (web rows are never recorded).
- **Logic:** `launcher/js/Ranker.js` + `Frecency.js` (other agent; see `launcher/js/README.md`). Launcher calls `Ranker.rank(allItems, query, usage, now, {limit: 50})`, `Frecency.record` on each launch (not for web), `setPinned`, `setHidden`, `prune` on load.
- **State:** `~/.local/state/launcher/usage.json` (pure usage map, written 300 ms debounced after each launch/pin/hide), `~/.local/state/launcher/history.json` (last 20 queries, newest first; a separate file so usage.json stays the plain map the JS expects). Optional `~/.config/launcher/config.json` = `{"aliases": {"<item id>": ["alias"]}}`, missing = ignored. Dirs are created with `mkdir -p` at start.
- **Verified (isolated `quickshell -n -p` copy on a short `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR`, solid backdrop window behind it, grim of the card only):** opens/toggles via IPC, empty-query list with app icons/subtitles/badges, query "scr" ranks the two commands first, apps next, web rows last, action panel for an app, Pin persists to usage.json, qmllint clean. **Not verified (no synthetic input allowed):** real typing, keyboard navigation, Ctrl+1..9, query recall, Enter-launch of any item, the power/sound-output commands (the sound/power ones call the Lua menus via `hyprctl dispatch` + `require`, untested), Open .desktop location (nautilus --select), Hide (no unhide UI yet; edit usage.json), a live-session run.
- **Sound input (2026-10-20):** now the launcher `input` section (`SectionsSource.qml`; `sound-input-menu.lua` removed in the cleanup). Bind SUPER+SHIFT+M (free); "Sound input" command in `CommandsSource.qml`. Sources minus `.monitor`, default marked, `set-default-source` + `move-source-output`.
- **Script commands (2026-10-20):** `launcher/ScriptSource.qml` (kind `script`, source "Script", id `script:<filename>`). Scripts: executable regular non-hidden files in `~/.config/launcher/commands` (repo `config/launcher/commands/`, `install.sh` links only that subdir so `~/.config/launcher/config.json` stays; the live symlink is not created until install.sh is rerun or `ln -sfn` by hand). Override dir with `$LAUNCHER_COMMANDS_DIR`. Rescanned on every launcher open (`active: lc.shown`) by `launcher/bin/scan-commands` (bash + awk, one process, prints `@<TAB>file` then `key<TAB>value` lines; missing dir = empty). Header in the first 30 lines: `# @launcher.<key> <value>` or `// @launcher.<key> <value>`; keys `title` (default file name sans extension), `subtitle`, `icon` (freedesktop name, else a code/terminal glyph), `aliases` (comma list), `mode` = `silent` (default) | `toast` (first stdout line, or `failed (rc): <first stderr line>`, via `notify-send -a Launcher`, so it goes through Notifs/Toasts) | `terminal` (`kitty --title <name> -e script`, closes when the script ends), `argument` (presence = ask for text; value = prompt label, default "Argument"; passed as `$1`). Unknown mode falls back to silent; first occurrence of a key wins. Launcher.qml changes: `ScriptSource { active: lc.shown }` + sourceList entry, and the argument prompt: `runItem(item, arg)` stores `argItem`/`argQuery` and clears the input when `item.argument` is set and no arg was given; the placeholder shows `<name>: <label>`, Enter runs with the typed text, Esc/Ctrl+C restores the previous query, `refresh()` is skipped meanwhile; sources now get `run(item, arg)`. Examples: Copy IP address (toast, wl-copy), Open dotfiles in terminal, Say hello (argument demo).
- **Script commands verified:** isolated `quickshell -n -p` copy with `LAUNCHER_COMMANDS_DIR`, rows appear with Script label and glyphs (screenshot of the card over a solid backdrop), rescan on open, qmllint clean, scan script on missing dir / spaces in names / non-exec files / bad mode; the toast shell snippet run directly against a fake `notify-send` (args, failure message). **Not verified (no synthetic input):** typing a query, Enter on a script, the argument prompt UI, terminal mode launch, real notification display, `icon` header resolving a theme icon (edit-copy did not resolve here, glyph fallback shown).
- **Next:** an unhide/manage view.
- **Clipboard history (2026-10-20, integrated):** `launcher/ClipboardSource.qml` (kind `clip`, source "Clipboard", id `clip:<cliphist id>`), registered in Launcher.qml (`ClipboardSource { id: clipSrc; active: lc.shown; onUpdated: lc.refreshKeep() }` + `sourceList`). Exclusive prefix mode on the RAW untrimmed query (`refresh()` now passes `query` as 2nd arg to `exclusive(q, raw)` / `dynamic(q, raw)`): `clip`, `clip <text>`, `v <text>` incl. the single space after `v` (a bare `v` stays a normal app search); only clipboard rows show. Lists `cliphist list` newest first (max 50, filter = substring of the one-line preview), images "[image]" + size, no thumbnails; loaded on each open, `updated()` signal redraws keeping the selection. Rows are `transient` (no usage.json, no Pin/Hide) and `private` (Launcher.runItem skips history.json for the query text). Enter = `sh -c 'cliphist decode "$1" | wl-copy' sh <id>`. Ctrl+K "Delete entry" (`clip-delete`): `ClipboardSource.action()` runs `cliphist delete` for that id, drops it from the view and returns `"keep"` (Launcher.doAction: launcher stays open and refreshes; any other truthy return closes it). IPC: `qs ipc call launcher query '<text>'` opens the launcher prefilled (`Launcher.openWith`; on an already open launcher it just sets the text); `show`/`toggle`/`hide` unchanged. `binds.lua`: SUPER+SHIFT+V = `qs ipc call launcher query 'v '`. Watchers: `autostart.lua` runs `wl-paste --type text|image --watch cliphist -max-items 500 store` (db `~/.cache/cliphist/db`); `cliphist` in `install.sh`. Caveats: cliphist ignores password-manager sensitive hints, so such copies are stored until rotated out (use Delete entry); typing `v ` while searching apps starting with "v " flips to clipboard mode. Verified (isolated `quickshell -n -p` copy, HOME and PATH shim with a fake cliphist, solid backdrop, grim of the card): `query 'v '` shows clip rows only; `v` = app results; `v ban` filters; `clip ` lists; Delete entry removes the row and the fake db entry and the list stays open; action ids `[run, clip-delete]` (no Pin/Hide); qmllint clean; `luac -p binds.lua`. Not verified: Enter on a row (would write the real clipboard), history.json/usage.json really untouched after Enter (code path only), real typing / Ctrl+K keypress (the delete was driven by a scratch-only IPC hook), the live bind (no hyprctl reload).

### Launcher utility sources (2026-10-20): calculator, windows, emoji, files
- **Sources:** `launcher/CalcSource.qml`, `WindowSource.qml`, `EmojiSource.qml`, `FileSource.qml`, data `launcher/data/emoji.tsv` (1910 rows `char TAB name TAB keywords`, generated once from GTK4's bundled CLDR English annotations: `gresource extract /usr/lib/libgtk-4.so /org/gtk/libgtk/emoji/en.data`, parsed with PyGObject as GVariant `a(ausasasu)`; no CLDR/unicode-emoji files exist on the system). `install.sh` package list gained `libqalculate` (qalc). `fd` is not used (plain `find`).
- **Launcher.qml changes (small, generic):** `sourceList` now `[apps, win, cmd, script, calc, emoji, file, web]` with a marked `// CLIPBOARD SOURCE` spot for the clipboard source; `refresh(keep)`: a source with `exclusive(q)` true owns the whole list (prefix modes), `dynamic()` items with `top: true` go above ranked results; async sources emit `updated()` and `refreshKeep()` redraws keeping the selection; items with `transient: true` (windows, calc, emoji, files) are not recorded in usage.json and get no Pin/Hide; item `actions: [{id,label,glyph}]` are appended to the Ctrl+K panel and delegated to the source's `action(id, item)`; placeholder text mentions `:` and `/`.
- **Calculator:** query must look like math (digit + operator, `func(...)`, or `<num><unit> to|in|as <unit>`; plain words never spawn qalc). Debounce 250 ms, `timeout 2 qalc -t -- <expr>` as argv (no shell), `in/as/into` are rewritten to `to` (qalc reads "in" as inches). Row "= result" (subtitle = expression). Enter: `wl-copy` + `notify-send -a Launcher`. Quirk: qalc prints mixed units for lengths (`5km in miles` = `3 mi + 188 yd + 2.39 in`); `-s "mixed units 0"` is not accepted by this qalc, so unresolved.
- **Windows:** `hyprctl clients -j` reread on every open (`active: lc.shown`), title + `class · workspace N`, icon = lowercase class; Enter = `hyprctl dispatch 'hl.dsp.focus({window="address:0x.."})'` (address regex-checked). `LAUNCHER_CLIENTS_JSON=<file>` replaces hyprctl (tests). Titles are never logged.
- **Emoji:** query starts with `:`; empty = first 50; matched with `Ranker.rank` on name+keywords, 50 rows; Enter `wl-copy -- <char>`. Ranking is the generic Ranker (e.g. `:smile` lists names containing the word first, not "smiling").
- **Files (name search, since 2026-10-22 only `f x`; `/x` and `~/x` now browse folders, see "Launcher: file browser" below):** query `f x` (needs >= 2 chars after the prefix); 200 ms debounce, process stopped on each query change; `timeout 3 find $HOME -maxdepth 6` pruning .git, node_modules, .cache, .steam, .wine, drive_c, compatdata, .cargo, .rustup, .npm, `.local/share/{Steam,lutris,wineprefixes,Trash}`; `-iname *x*` (`-ipath` if x has a slash), glob chars escaped; at most 400 collected, shallowest 30 shown. Enter = `xdg-open`; Ctrl+K: Copy path, Open containing folder (`nautilus --select`).
- **Verified (isolated `quickshell -n -p` copy, `HOME` = scratch dir with fake files, fake clients JSON, solid backdrop window, grim of the card only; a test-only IPC hook in the scratch copy set the query and dumped `results`):** qmllint clean; result lists for window rows (fake titles), `:smile`, `12*(3+4)` = 84, `5km in miles`, `12 usd to eur`, `sqrt(2)`, `hello world` (no calc row), `f report` and `/report` (.git/.cache hits pruned); screenshots of the window row, calculator top row, and the file action panel (Run / Copy path / Open containing folder). **Not verified:** Enter on any of them (it would write the real clipboard / open files / focus windows), real typing, `~/` prefix, depth cap and the Steam/wine prunes against a real tree, Ctrl+K action execution, live-shell reload of the final Launcher.qml.
- **Folders open in Nautilus via xdg-open (2026-10-22):** with no user default for `inode/directory`, xdg-open took the first entry of /usr/share/applications/mimeinfo.cache, `kitty-open.desktop` (folders opened a terminal). Fixed outside the repo: `xdg-mime default org.gnome.Nautilus.desktop inode/directory` (~/.config/mimeapps.list, not tracked). A new machine needs the same.

### Launcher: file browser (2026-10-22)
- **Behaviour:** a query starting with `/` or `~/` browses that folder (`FileSource.browse()`: everything up to the last `/` is the folder, the rest filters it, case-insensitive substring; a filter starting with `.` shows hidden entries by prefix, otherwise dotfiles are hidden). First row "Open folder" (`xdg-open`; with no trailing slash only when the typed text exactly names a folder), then folders, then files (numeric-aware name sort, prefix matches first when filtering), max 500 rows, unreadable / missing folder = an info row. Enter on a folder row descends (`item.descend` = path + "/", handled at the top of `Launcher.runItem`: sets the input text, nothing recorded; `~` style kept if typed). Enter on a file = `xdg-open`.
- **Listing:** one one-shot `timeout 3 find -H <dir> -mindepth 1 -maxdepth 1 -printf '%Y\t%s\t%f\0'` (argv, NUL records so newlines in names are safe, `%Y` follows symlinks so a link to a folder is a folder), collected with `StdioCollector` (SplitParser cannot split on NUL; the `\0` must reach find as backslash-zero, a real NUL char in argv truncates it). Cached per folder for 2 s (re-listed in the background when older, shown stale meanwhile); one Process at a time, a newer folder request is queued. Filtering / sorting happens in QML on the cache. Max 20000 entries parsed.
- **Actions (Ctrl+K or Right, the existing `item.actions` mechanism; `actionsFor(cat)`):** category from the extension (`category()`; no `file` calls). All files: Show in folder (`nautilus --select`), Copy path. Image: Copy image to clipboard (`wl-copy --type <file --mime-type -b>`), "Set as wallpaper (full theme apply)" = `python3 -I ~/.config/theme/theme.py apply <file>` after a notify-send. Video: Play in mpv, "Set as video wallpaper (full theme apply)" (same command). Text / code (and README, Makefile ...): Open in editor (`kitty --directory <dir> -e ${EDITOR:-nvim} <file>`), Copy contents to clipboard. Folders: Browse (Enter), Open folder, Open terminal here (`kitty --directory`), Copy path. No delete / destructive actions. "Open with..." skipped (no CLI chooser).
- **Verified (nested Hyprland on the live compositor, own `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR`, isolated HOME with fake files incl. spaces, newline and quote in names, dangling symlink, symlinked folder, chmod 000 folder; checkerboard Background-layer backdrop; test Quickshell with stub `xdg-open nautilus kitty mpv wl-copy notify-send` on PATH logging argv and a stub theme.py, bogus DBUS address; IPC `launcher query` + `hl.dsp.send_shortcut` on the NESTED instance only; grim of the card on the nested socket):** listing, filters (`10221`, `.`, `sub` exact folder), descend by Enter, missing / unreadable folder row, absolute and `~` paths, action panels for image / video / text / folder, and the exact commands of every action (wallpaper actions only reached the stub theme.py, `apply <file>`). Live shell log: no new errors from these files. **Not verified:** real xdg-open / nautilus / kitty / wl-copy behaviour with these args (stubs only), huge folders (100k entries), real theme apply.
- **Caveats:** the old `/x` / `~/x` deep name search is now `f x` only. `Launcher.qml` query via IPC on an already open launcher keeps an open action panel (pre-existing). Info rows need `source: ""` or the badge shows "undefined". Hidden nested-window test trick: nested Hyprland shows only while its window's special workspace is toggled visible on the live compositor (grim hangs otherwise); toggle it only for captures.

### Launcher sections: sound output / input, power, Twitch (2026-10-20)
- **What:** native sub-menus in `Launcher.qml`; `launcher/SectionsSource.qml` (root rows kind `section`, ids `sec:<name>`, source label "Menu", with aliases mic/microphone/speaker(s)/headphones/shutdown/reboot/logout/suspend/stream; rows inside a section are `transient` + `private`, so no usage.json / history.json entries; the section entry rows themselves are recorded). `CommandsSource.qml` lost its "Power menu", "Sound output", "Sound input" rows (the ones that spawned the qmenu popups via `hyprctl dispatch` + Lua require); Sleep / Shut down / Restart / Exit Hyprland rows stay as direct, unconfirmed commands.
- **Navigation:** `Launcher.section` ("" = main list); `enterSection(name)`, `back()`, `keyEscape()`, `keyLeft()` (Esc / Ctrl+C: a section entered from the main list goes back to the main list, a section opened directly by IPC / keybind (`sectionDirect`) closes the launcher, root -> close; Left = back one level in any section, only when it would not move the caret (empty filter, or caret at 0 without selection); Backspace on an empty filter also goes back; footer hint shows `← / Backspace back · Esc close|back`), placeholder "Filter..." (the breadcrumb chip was dropped 2026-10-20: the card header now shows the context, see Header), **Header** (`Launcher.title`/`icon`, bindings so it updates live without flicker: "Launcher" + rocket on the main list; in a section `SectionsSource.header(name)`/`glyph(name)` = Audio output, Audio input, Power, Streams, Screenshot, Record with the section glyph (`header` field, defaults to `title`); prefix modes via `modeTitle`/`modeGlyph` on the exclusive sources = Emoji, Files, Clipboard history; argument prompt = the script name + its glyph; action panel = item name + rocket), typing filters with the ranker (no usage). IPC: `qs ipc call launcher section sound-output|sound-input|power|twitch` opens straight inside the section (`openSection`; Esc closes, Left/Backspace-on-empty goes to the main list; calling it again for the section already shown closes the launcher). binds.lua: SUPER+SHIFT+E power, +S output, +M input, +T twitch now call that; the `show_*_menu` requires were dropped from binds.lua.
- **Sections (replaced the Lua scripts, removed 2026-10-20):** output / input = `pactl list sinks|sources` (LC_ALL=C), same parsing as the awk (Name/Description, short name trimming, sources minus `.monitor`), current default first with a check and "Default" label; select = `pactl set-default-sink|source` + move all sink-inputs|source-outputs, then close. Power = (commands of the removed `power-menu.lua`) (sleep, shutdown, reboot, exit hyprland, switch to gamescope / kde); the script had no confirmation, new: everything but Sleep needs a second Enter (row turns into "Confirm: X" with a warning glyph; moving the selection or typing cancels; Ctrl+1-9/click on another row only arms that row). Twitch = `<twitch-qs-mpv>/bin/twitch-qs-follows --json` (2026-10-20; async, "Loading..." row, exit != 0 shows an error row with the first stderr line; `JSON.parse` -> rows: name, dim subtitle "game · 12k [· stale]", red dot live / grey offline + favorite ("favorite" rows have no ": ", the old text parser mis-named them); kind `login` row runs `twitch-qs-mpv login`, `none` shows an info row. If `--json` exits non-zero or prints non-JSON (older install) the tool is re-run once without it and the old text parser (`parseTwitch`, `__login__`/`__none__` lines) is used), select = `twitch-qs-mpv watch <login>` with `TWITCH_QS_FONT=Paper Mono` like the old script. **Fallback chain (2026-10-20):** tools are probed once per section open (`sh` + `command -v`, files in `$LAUNCHER_TWITCH_DIR` first, then PATH): watch = `twitch-qs-mpv watch` -> else `mpv https://www.twitch.tv/<login>` (a non-zero exit within 15 s raises a notify-send toast, adding "yt-dlp is not installed" when it is missing) -> else `xdg-open` of that URL. Without `twitch-qs-follows` the section shows "Open Twitch in browser" (xdg-open twitch.tv/directory/following) and, for a typed filter matching `^[A-Za-z0-9_]{1,25}$`, a top row "Watch <login>" (`SectionsSource.extra()`, run through the same chain; login only passed as argv). `__login__` row becomes an info row when twitch-qs-mpv is missing. **Past broadcasts (2026-10-20):** Twitch channel rows (live/offline/favorite, not login/none/info/typed) carry the item action "Past broadcasts" (`actions` on the row, handled by `SectionsSource.action()`; Right arrow on such a row runs it directly, `Launcher.keyRight()`; Ctrl+K panel lists it too). It pushes a nested level inside the twitch section (`vodLogin` set; `Launcher.section` stays `twitch`): header "Past broadcasts" with the channel name as dim `titleSub`, `twitch-qs-mpv vods <login> --json` (JSON array newest first: id, title, game, duration, duration_s, created_at, views, url; exit 2 not logged in, 3 network, 4 unknown login) -> rows (title; subtitle "Game · 3h24m · yesterday|N days ago|12 Oct[ 2025] · 1.2k views", missing parts omitted; kind `twitch-vod`, transient/private, filter via the ranker on title/game). "Loading..." row, error row (first stderr line, token-like 30+ char strings masked, per-code fallback text), "No past broadcasts" info row. `back()` (Left, Backspace on empty filter, Esc unless `sectionDirect`) leaves the level to the channel list with the channel re-selected; Esc in a keybind-opened section still closes. Enter plays: `twitch-qs-mpv vod <id>` if the app is installed, else `mpv https://www.twitch.tv/videos/<id>` (same <15 s non-zero toast), else `xdg-open` (`playVod`; id ^[0-9]{1,15}$, login ^[A-Za-z0-9_]{1,25}$, argv only). Without twitch-qs-mpv the action is "Past broadcasts (browser)": xdg-open `https://www.twitch.tv/<login>/videos`. Support for `vods` is decided by the section probe, not by output sniffing: `twitch-qs-vods` must exist next to the (symlink-resolved) `twitch-qs-mpv` (`twVods`); an old app answers an unknown command with usage text and exit 2, the same code as "not logged in", so the command is not even run then ("Update twitch-qs-mpv for past broadcasts" plus an "Open past broadcasts in browser" row). Real exit codes of `vods`: 2 -> "Not logged in" row plus the usual "Log in to Twitch" row (runs `twitch-qs-mpv login`), 3 -> "Could not reach Twitch", 4 -> "Unknown channel", 0 with `[]` -> "No past broadcasts", anything else (1 usage/missing program, 127, killed) -> the update row with the stderr line as note + browser row. Tested with fake `twitch-qs-mpv`/`mpv`/`xdg-open`/`notify-send` shims in a scratch copy (modes ok, empty, exit 1/2/3/4/127, old app without twitch-qs-vods; app, mpv, browser) driven by a scratch-only `dev` IPC hook calling `keyEscape/keyLeft/keyRight/keyBackspace/doAction/runIndex`; real key presses and a real `vods` command (other repo, in progress) not tested. The card header shows a dim "Player: twitch-qs-mpv|mpv|browser" (`ModalMenu.titleSub`, `SectionsSource.headerSub()`). Tested via scratch PATH shims, all 6 tool combinations. Tokens are only handled inside the twitch-qs-* scripts, never seen by QML. `$LAUNCHER_TWITCH_DIR` replaces the bin dir (tests). Live/offline: red / grey circle (item field `iconColor`, small Launcher.qml row change).
