- **Removed 2026-10-20:** `hypr/scripts/sound-output-menu.lua`, `sound-input-menu.lua`, `twitch-menu.lua`, `power-menu.lua`, plus `ScreenshotMenu.qml`, `RecordMenu.qml`, `ModalWindow.qml`, `ModeTile.qml` (recoverable from git 15d1e09). Cleanup pass 2 (same day) removed `Menu.qml`, `bin/qmenu` (+ its install.sh symlink and the `menu` IpcHandler), `config/tofi` + theme template/out, `hyprlauncher.conf`, the `launcher` layer rule. `modal-windows.lua` and the `qs-modal` rule stay for Settings.
- **Verified (isolated `quickshell -n -p` copy, short XDG_RUNTIME_DIR, scratch HOME, PATH shims for pactl mutations / hyprshutdown / shutdown / reboot, fake twitch dir, small solid backdrop, grim of the card; scratch-only `dev` IPC hook drove the same Launcher functions the keys call):** all four sections open via `section`, correct rows (real read-only pactl listing), back -> main list -> close, repeat call closes, filter in a section, aliases rank the section row first (power, mic, reboot, stream, headphones, logout), power confirm arm / cancel on selection change / second Enter ran the shimmed `hyprshutdown ... reboot`, sound select logged the right `pactl set-default-*` (there were no live streams, so the move loop did not run), twitch watch logged `watch bravo`, twitch loading / error / login / none rows, screenshots of Twitch (fake data), Power confirm, Sound output; qmllint clean, `luac -p binds.lua`. **Not verified:** real keypresses (Esc / Backspace / Enter handlers only via the hook, code path reviewed), mouse clicks, the live shell hot-reload and live binds (no hyprctl reload), real Twitch network/auth, moving real streams, real power actions (never run).

### Launcher sections: screenshot / record (2026-10-20)
- **What:** `screenshot` and `record` are sections in `launcher/SectionsSource.qml` (root rows "Screenshot" / "Record", ids `sec:screenshot` / `sec:record`, aliases print, grab, snip, capture / screenrec, video, screencast, recording; the `cmd:screenshot` / `cmd:record` rows were removed from `CommandsSource.qml`). Rows: Region / Window / Screen (kind `shot` | `rec`); record also has "Stop recording" (kind `rec-stop`, first row and preselected while `screenrec.sh status` exits 0, checked async on every entry) and the options Frame rate (30/60), Format (MP4/WebM), Record desktop audio (kind `rec-opt`: Enter toggles, the row refreshes in place and the launcher stays open; `Launcher.runItem` special-cases this kind). Options are saved (400 ms debounce) to `$XDG_STATE_HOME/quickshell/screenrec.conf`, the same `fps=/format=/audio=` file `screenrec.sh` as before. Section reopens on the last used mode (`SectionsSource.startSel`, used by `Launcher.refresh()` for an empty filter).
- **Capture timing:** `Launcher.runItem` hides the launcher (`shown = false`) before `run()`; `SectionsSource.later()` then runs `sh -c 'sleep 0.25; exec "$@"' sh <script> <args>`, so the layer is unmapped before slurp / wf-recorder start (old menus: 200 ms timer). Commands are exactly the old ones: `screenshot.sh region|window|output`, `screenrec.sh region|window|output --fps N --format F --audio|--no-audio`, stop = `screenrec.sh stop`. `$QS_SHOT_SCRIPT` / `$QS_RECORD_SCRIPT` still override the scripts (tests).
- **IPC / binds (decision):** binds and key scripts are unchanged. `qs ipc call screenshot menu|close` and `record menu|close` stay as thin aliases in `shell.qml` for `launcher.openSection("screenshot"|"record")` (menu = toggle like before, close only closes when that section is the one shown). Reason: `screenshot-key.sh` / `screenrec-key.sh` keep their logic (SHIFT+Print stops a running recording without any UI, fallback to a region capture when the shell is down) and need that target to exist. Esc closes (opened directly), Left / Backspace-on-empty go back to the main list. The `ScreenshotMenu` / `RecordMenu` instances in shell.qml are still loaded but no longer reachable (cleanup list in the session notes; nothing deleted).
- **Launcher.qml changes:** `onAllItemsChanged` no longer refreshes while inside a section (windows/clipboard rescans on open reset the selection); the section branch of `refresh(keep)` keeps the selected row by id and jumps to row 0 when Stop recording appears.
- **Verified (isolated `quickshell -n -p` copy, scratch HOME/XDG_RUNTIME_DIR, PATH shims for grim/slurp/wl-copy/notify-send/wf-recorder and a read-only hyprctl, scratch-only `dev` IPC hook for Enter; screenshots of the card over a solid backdrop):** both sections list the right rows; `screenshot.sh` ran for region (`slurp -d`, `grim -g`), window (`slurp -r`), output (`slurp -o -r -f %o`, `grim -o`) with wl-copy + notify-send; record region/window/output reached wf-recorder with `-r 30 ... -c libvpx-vp9` after toggling fps 30 / webm (output = `-o DP-2`); options persisted to screenrec.conf and stay open; Stop row appears while recording and runs `screenrec.sh stop` (recorder gone, state removed); `screenrec-key.sh` stops a running recording, and both key scripts open the matching section through the old IPC; aliases rank the section rows first (print, video, record, scr); toggle/close semantics of the IPC aliases. qmllint clean. **Not verified:** real keypresses and mouse clicks (Enter driven by the dev hook), real grim/slurp/wf-recorder (never run), audio capture (the scratch env has no usable default sink: script printed "Recording without audio"), the live shell hot-reload, the live binds.

### Launcher: Jellyfin section (2026-10-20)
- **What:** `launcher/JellyfinSource.qml` (new; in `sourceList`) + a `jellyfin` entry in `SectionsSource.qml` (root row "Jellyfin", aliases jf/movies/shows/tv/music/media/video/films; header/glyph/enter delegate to the source via `SectionsSource.jf`) + small `Launcher.qml` changes (`jfSrc`, `backJellyfin()`, `afterEnter()`, `keyRight` browses, `Run` label from `item.runLabel`, `jfSrc.track(query, section)` in `onQueryChanged`, `jfSrc.reset()` on open). `qs ipc call launcher section jellyfin`; prefix mode `jf <text>` (needs the space) = search only.
- **How:** talks only to `jellyfin-qs` (`sh -c 'PATH=$PATH:$HOME/.local/bin; exec jellyfin-qs "$@"'`, so a missing PATH entry is covered; exit 127 = not installed). Every request is its own async `Process` with a token (stale exits ignored, newer request kills the older one): `status --json` on entry (caption = user; exit 2/3 replaces the menu by the error rows), search 250 ms debounced, one request per query, no auto retry ("Try again" row), nested levels via `children <id>` (Right / Enter on folders; Left, Backspace-on-empty, Esc one level back; leaving the last level of a `jf ` search returns to it). Enter plays (`play <id>`, `--resume` for videos with resume_s > 0, containers = queue), Ctrl+K: Resume/Play, Play from start, Shuffle (artist/album/playlist), Browse, Recently added (libraries). Failures toast through `notify-send`. All rows `transient` + `private`; only the root "Jellyfin" row is recorded like other sections.
- **Caveats:** exit 4 on a data command shows "Not found on the Jellyfin server" (only 127 is "jellyfin-qs not found"). Search results are not re-fetched when you leave a nested level (cached by text). Verified with a fake `jellyfin-qs` shim in an isolated scratch instance (IPC hook calling keyEscape/keyLeft/keyRight/runIndex), not with real keys or a real server.
- **Ranking tie (fixed):** the Jellyfin Flatpak app is also named "Jellyfin", so at `jelly`/`jellyf`/`jellyfin` the section row and the app row tie exactly (same name, same score) and the order was arbitrary (the app could sit above the menu row, identical looking; only the "Menu"/"Application" label differs). `Ranker.rank` now breaks ties section-first, then name, then input order; regression test in `launcher/js/tests/ranker.test.js`. Verified in a scratch instance (IPC dump of results), not with real typing.
- **Structure (per library, 2026-10-20):** top level = one row per library (`jellyfin-qs libraries`, skipping `collection_type` playlists; subtitle "TV shows"/"Movies"/"Music", glyph tv/film/music/folder), then only "Playlists". The global Continue watching / Next up / Recently added rows were removed (user wants them per category); typing at the top level still searches everything. Libraries are fetched in `enter()` (and in the `jf ` prefix when idle) and cached in `libRows` until the next `reset()`. Entering a library (`pushLibrary`) = a level whose `head` rows are lists scoped to it: Continue watching (`resume --library`, not music), Next up (`nextup --library`, tv/other only), Recently added (`latest --library`), Albums (music: `children <lib> --types MusicAlbum`); each opens a nested level titled with the library name and the list name as `titleSub`. Below the head: all titles by SortName (`children <lib> --types auto --sort name --paged --limit 200`: Series / Movie+BoxSet / album artists, other types direct children); header caption "<type> · N <shows|titles|artists>". Paging: `nearEnd(sel, count)` (Launcher `onSelChanged` + end of `refresh`) loads the next page (`--start <loaded>`) once the selection is within 40 rows of the end; an "N more" / "Loading more..." / error+Try again row ends a partial list. Filter inside a library: local ranker over head + titles when everything is loaded, else head matches + `search <q> --parent <lib> --types <per type>`. Folder / BoxSet rows open a paged `children` level too; series -> seasons -> episodes etc. unchanged. **Selection fix:** `Launcher.refresh(keep)` - when the selected row was a status row (`info` / `jf-retry`) and disappears, the selection goes to the first row of that old status block (Try again -> Loading... -> first loaded row) instead of the old index. **Verified** in an isolated scratch instance with a fake shim (fake names; 3000-item library: 15 pages, scoped search, local filter when loaded; head rows/order per type; Left/Right/Backspace chain with filter + selection restore; lib / level / page failures + retry selection; two card screenshots) via the scratch-only `dev` IPC hook. **Scratch launcher must not take focus:** `ModalMenu` grabs `WlrKeyboardFocus.Exclusive` and a full-screen input surface, so a scratch launcher swallowed the user's keystrokes once; in scratch copies set `keyboardFocus: None` + `mask: Region {}` in ModalMenu.qml and drive it only by IPC. Not verified: real keys, real playback, the live launcher UI with real data.

### Jellyfin helper (2026-10-20)
- `config/launcher/jellyfin/bin/jellyfin-qs` (python stdlib; `install.sh` links it to `~/.local/bin`) + `mpv/jellyfin-report.lua`, README and tests beside it. Server URL in `~/.config/jellyfin-qs/config.json`, token in `~/.local/state/jellyfin-qs/token.json` (0600); the server is reached over Tailscale, so never commit its address. Login once: `jellyfin-qs login` in a terminal. Data commands print JSON; exit codes 0 ok, 1 usage, 2 not logged in/rejected, 3 network ("server unreachable (is Tailscale connected?)"), 4 not found. `play` launches detached mpv with a 0600 include file for the auth header (never in argv/URL). Library flags (2026-10-20): `resume|nextup|latest --library ID`, `search --parent ID`, `children <id> [--types auto|T,..] [--sort name|added|year] [--start N] [--limit N] [--paged]` (`--paged` = `{items,total,start}`; no options = the old output); details in the README. Tests: `python3 -I -m unittest discover -s config/launcher/jellyfin/tests` (fake server with tv/music/250-movie libraries). The list commands (libraries, children with the new flags, resume/nextup/latest, scoped search) were checked read-only against the real server (counts and timings only: every library lists in one page, ~0.2-0.4 s per call); `play` was not run.
- **Single player (2026-10-22):** `play` first runs `stop_players(d)`: for every `<id>.sock` in the session dir it connects, reads the peer PID via `SO_PEERCRED`, sends `quit` over the mpv IPC (so `jellyfin-report.lua` posts Stopped), waits up to 5 s for the PID to exit, then SIGTERM, then SIGKILL after 2 s more. Only jellyfin-qs mpvs have such sockets, so mpvpaper / Twitch mpv are untouched. A refused socket = dead session: its `<id>.*` files are removed; mpv leaves the `.sock` file behind on exit, so it is removed afterwards. Test: `test_play_replaces_running_player` (real mpv + fake server). Caveat: a later play blocks up to ~7 s only if the old mpv hangs; verified with the test suite only, not against the real server/session.

### Launcher: Network and Bluetooth sections (2026-10-21)
- **What:** `launcher/NetSource.qml` (new, in `sourceList` for its Ctrl+K actions) + `network` / `bluetooth` entries in `SectionsSource.qml` (root rows "Network" (aliases wifi, wi-fi, wlan, ethernet, wired, internet) and "Bluetooth" (bt, bluez, pair); `rows()` / `headerSub()` delegate to `SectionsSource.net`). `qs ipc call launcher section network|bluetooth`. Uses the same in-process services as NetPanel / BtPanel (`Quickshell.Networking` = NetworkManager over D-Bus, `Quickshell.Bluetooth` = BlueZ): no nmcli / bluetoothctl processes, nothing blocks.
- **Network rows:** wired devices (Enter connect / disconnect; unplugged or profile-less = info row), Wi-Fi networks sorted connected, saved, signal (subtitle state · WPA2/WPA3/Open · %, source label Connected / Saved), "Turn Wi-Fi off/on" last. Enter: saved or open = `connect()`; new WPA/WPA2/WPA3-Personal = the launcher's own search field turns into a masked password prompt (`argItem.secret`: `echoMode Password`, ImhSensitiveData, key glyph, footer "Enter connect · Esc cancel", 8-63 chars else a red footer note) and hands it to `WifiNetwork.connectWithPsk()` (Quickshell updates the saved profile's psk or AddAndActivates a new one: D-Bus only, never argv / logs / history.json; the field is reset to the old filter right after). Enterprise / WEP networks are info rows ("Set it up with nmtui first"). Ctrl+K "Forget network" on saved ones (not on the only active link).
- **Bluetooth rows:** connected, paired, then named discovered devices (MAC-named noise dropped like BtPanel), type glyph from BlueZ `icon`, battery when available; Enter = disconnect / connect / pair, a successful pair sets `trusted` and connects; "Scan for devices / Stop scanning", "Turn Bluetooth on/off". Discovery starts on entering the section (also once the adapter is powered on there) and is stopped on leave / close only if the launcher started it. Ctrl+K "Forget device".
- **Progress / safety:** rows rebuilt every 700 ms while a section is shown (`updated()` only on a visible change, selection kept by id; `rev` makes headerSub re-evaluate); actions are tracked per row (`pending`): "Connecting..." / "Pairing..." until the target state, red "Could not connect" / "Wrong or missing password" (Wi-Fi `connectionFailed` reason) when it falls back or times out (20-30 s); a failed secured Wi-Fi row asks for the password again. Every disconnect and radio-off needs a second Enter (`confirm` + new `confirmText` / `confirmSub` fields in `Launcher.refresh`, warning when it is the only active link). Launcher.qml: `NetSource { active: lc.shown; section: lc.section; onFinished: lc.close() }`, `runItem` branch `netSrc.owns(item)` (acts in place, launcher stays open), `argNote` / `argSecret`, `argGlyph`.
- **Close on success + Bluetooth audio (2026-10-21, user request):** when a connect started from the launcher reaches its target (Wi-Fi `connect` / password, wired connect, BT connect incl. the pair -> trust -> connect chain; `settle()` collects them in `succeeded`, `poll()` emits `finished()`), the launcher closes. Failures keep it open with the red row / re-prompt; disconnect, forget, power and scan rows still stay open. For a BT device whose icon looks like audio (audio-*, headset, headphone, earbud, speaker, or no icon) `watchSink(address)` then looks every 500 ms for up to 15 s (keeps running after the launcher closed; `PwObjectTracker` binds the nodes so their properties fill in) for an `Audio/Sink` node with that `api.bluez5.address`, sets `Pipewire.preferredDefaultAudioSink` (same as the Audio popup's DevicePicker / `wpctl set-default`) and moves playing streams with `pactl move-sink-input` (same loop as the launcher Sound output section). Only for launcher-initiated connects, never on other connected-state changes.
- **Test hook:** `QS_LAUNCHER_NET_FIXTURE=<json>` swaps both services for fakes from `launcher/js/NetFixture.js` (format in its header; `badPsk` fails, `failConnect` names, delays, BT `sink: true` = a fake sink appears after the connect and the sink setter only logs "audio default sink ...") that log only verb + name (password length, never the text).
- **Verified 2026-10-21** (isolated `quickshell -n -p` copy, scratch HOME, launcher with `keyboardFocus: None` + empty mask, a test shell.qml with only the launcher, a solid backdrop PanelWindow under the card and a scratch-only `dev` IPC hook calling `runIndex` / `runItem(argItem, text)` / `doAction` / `keyEscape`; placeholder fixture names): main-list queries wifi / net / ethernet / blue / bt rank the section row first; Network and Bluetooth lists, password prompt (masked, screenshot), too-short note, wrong password -> failure row -> re-prompt -> Connecting... -> Connected, disconnect confirm, forget, Wi-Fi off / on, BT scan on entry and stop on close, pair -> trust -> connect, BT disconnect confirm; close on success: BT audio connect and pair chain closed the launcher (layer gone) and logged the default-sink switch for that device's fake sink, keyboard connect closed it without a sink call, Wi-Fi wrong password stayed open, right password and wired reconnect closed, wired disconnect stayed open; screenshots of the cards looked at. Real system read-only (no fixture): wired + "No Wi-Fi adapter" on this desktop, BT paired + discovered devices listed, discovery off again after closing. qmllint clean; privacy hook dry-run clean. The live shell hot-reloaded the files without errors (no inline components, no restart needed).
- **Not verified:** the real PipeWire default-sink switch and stream move (needs a real BT audio device connect; the real default sink was never changed), real keypresses / clicks (Esc on the password prompt, typing), any real connect / disconnect / pair / power change (never run against the real network or devices), real Wi-Fi (this host has no adapter), BlueZ pairing that needs a PIN / passkey confirmation (Quickshell registers no agent; such devices probably fail, pair them with blueman / bluetoothctl), SAE via connectWithPsk (NM infers key-mgmt from the AP), the BlueZ "operation already in progress" race BtPanel warns about (start/stop are guarded and only logged).

### Launcher: Install webapp section (2026-10-21)
- **What:** root row "Install webapp" (`SectionsSource` `webapp`, aliases webapp / web app / pwa, words install, brave; `qs ipc call launcher section webapp`), `launcher/WebappSource.qml` (drives the flow, in `sourceList`), helper `bin/webapp-install` (python3 -I, run via `Process`, nothing blocks). Flow: entering the section opens an address prompt by itself (`prompt(item)` signal -> `Launcher.runItem`; argument rows may carry `argInit` (prefilled text), `argVerb` (footer verb), `argGlyph`) -> Enter starts `webapp-install probe <text>` AND opens the name prompt at once (empty, header "Name the web app", row "Looking up host...") -> when the lookup ends, `suggest(item, text)` puts the suggestion (manifest short_name / name, application-name, og:site_name, first part of the title, else the host) into the field ONLY if it is still empty; a typed name is never replaced. Enter with a name while the lookup runs = `pendingName`, installed when it ends. The typed name is used exactly (trimmed; file name = its slug). Enter = `install <url> --name <name>` ("Installing..." row) -> `notify-send` "Installed <name>" (with the icon) and the launcher closes (`finished()`). Bad input: footer note (no letters/dots) or a red "Could not install" row + re-prompt (helper error); Esc on a prompt leaves the rows (Install / Change the address). **Bug fixed 2026-10-21 (user installed "WhatsApp", got "WhatsApp Web" and a second `webapp_whatsapp_web.desktop`):** the name prompt only opened when the (slow, real-site) lookup finished and then set the field to the suggestion, overwriting what the user had already typed into the field meanwhile; Enter then installed the suggestion. Reproduced with a 2.5 s stub page (typed "Example Chat" -> field became "Example Chat Web"); helper and slug were fine. Fix = the flow above; the prompt header no longer shows the suggestion (it is in the row subtitle as "Suggested: ..."), and `refreshKeep()` now also refreshes rows under an open prompt (`refresh()` filters with `argQuery` then). In-place rows: `Launcher.runItem` asks `[netSrc, waSrc].find(s => s.owns(item))`; `activate()` returning a string = keep the prompt with that footer note (NetSource now returns its password note this way).
- **Helper:** normalises the URL (adds https://, only http(s), no credentials, sane host; typed without a scheme and https fails -> tries http), fetches only the page itself, the manifest / icon URLs it declares (also on other hosts: static-asset CDNs, protocol-relative `//host/...`) and the guessed `/apple-touch-icon.png` + `/favicon.ico` of its origin (no favicon service), browser-like Chrome User-Agent (`WEBAPP_UA`), 8 s timeout (`WEBAPP_TIMEOUT`), max 5 redirects and only to http(s), size limits (page 2 MB, manifest 512 KB, icon 4 MB), no cookies. Icon choice: rasters >= 128 px and SVGs first (mask-icon skipped), manifest `purpose: any` beats `maskable` at comparable size, guessed paths last, up to 6 downloads, keeps the biggest; PIL converts (ICO frames, non-square padded, max 512, a full-bleed icon with 4 opaque corners gets rounded corners (22 % radius) like a launcher mask), else rsvg-convert / magick, else PNG passthrough; < 32 px or nothing -> letter tile (hex colour: Qt's SVG renderer shows `hsl()` as black). Installs `$XDG_DATA_HOME/icons/hicolor/<N>x<N>/apps/webapp-<slug>.<sha1-8 of the content>.png` (+ `scalable/apps/webapp-<slug>.<hash>.svg` for SVG icons and tiles) and writes `Icon=<absolute path of that file>` (slugs have no "." any more); `gtk-update-icon-cache` only when an `icon-theme.cache` already exists there (creating one would hide icons other apps add later; there is none in the user's hicolor today). Entry `applications/webapp_<slug>.desktop` (slug = lowercase name, spaces -> `_`, like the old `create_brave_webapp` zsh function): `Exec=brave --app="<url>"` (the `~/.local/bin/brave` shim; `% " $ \` escaped), `Icon=<absolute icon path>`, `StartupWMClass=brave-<host>_<path, / -> _>-Default` (e.g. `brave-example.com__-Default`, `brave-example.com__app_-Default`; derived from Chromium's app-name rule host + "_" + path, matches the classes seen on live app windows for root URLs; `bin/focus-app` keys on `^brave-<host>__`), `Categories=Network;WebBrowser;`, `X-Webapp-Url`, `X-Webapp-Installer=1`, `X-Webapp-Icon-Source` (chosen icon URL or "letter tile": check this first when an icon looks wrong, and the entry's mtime: an entry older than a helper fix was not reinstalled); `desktop-file-validate` errors become warnings, `update-desktop-database`. Reinstalling the same name replaces the entry and every `webapp-<slug>.{png,svg}` / `webapp-<slug>.<hash>.{png,svg}` icon file (older unhashed ones too).
- **Wrong icons fixed 2026-10-21 (three real sites, read-only diagnosis; names not recorded here):** (1) a messaging site answered the old non-browser User-Agent with HTTP 400 and had no `/favicon.ico`, so only the letter tile was left; with a Chrome UA the page lists a manifest whose icons are protocol-relative on a static-asset host (512 px picked now). (2) two design / issue-tracker sites list `purpose: maskable` and plain icons of the same size; the scorer tied and took the maskable full-bleed square. Now `any` wins; one of them only declares full-bleed squares, which now get rounded corners. (3) display: the files were valid PNGs in the right dirs (WhatsApp, the control, had the same layout but a good, transparent-cornered icon), so the difference was the content above; on top of that a reinstall kept the OLD icon in the running shell: AppsSource only rebuilt on `applications.valuesChanged` (list membership), not when a rescan updates an entry in place, and Qt caches theme icons by name (reproduced in an isolated instance: icon replaced in a new size dir, row still showed the old one). Fixes: `AppsSource` also rebuilds on `DesktopEntries.applicationsChanged`, and the hashed absolute `Icon=` path gives every reinstall a new cache key (verified: two reinstalls with different icon colours switched the row immediately). No per-app browser profile: `--app` runs in Brave's Default profile, so site logins / data stay where they are. JSON on stdout, exit 0 / 1 (usage, invalid URL, name or slug) / 2 (could not write / remove) / 4 (no such entry).
- **Uninstall (2026-10-21):** Ctrl+K on an app row whose desktop id is `webapp_<slug>` and whose command has `--app=` with a brave/chrom* browser (AppsSource `webappSlug`; so entries from the old zsh function count too, other apps never get it) offers "Uninstall webapp" (`confirm: true`: the first Enter turns the action row into "Uninstall <name>?" / "Press Enter again to confirm", moving the selection or closing the panel disarms; generic `Launcher.actionConfirm` for any action with `confirm`). `WebappSource.action` runs `webapp-install uninstall <slug>` (async, toast with the result): slug `^[a-z0-9][a-z0-9_.-]{0,63}$` without `..`, the entry must be a regular file (no symlink) resolving inside `$XDG_DATA_HOME/applications` and its Exec must contain `--app=`; removes it and only `icons/hicolor/*/apps/webapp-<slug>.{png,svg}` (regular files inside hicolor), then update-desktop-database. Browser profile / site data untouched. DesktopEntries drops the row by itself.
- **App list:** Quickshell's DesktopEntries watches the applications dirs that existed when the shell started, so a new entry in `~/.local/share/applications` shows up in the launcher with its icon right away (verified in the isolated instance after a restart with the dir present; with the dir created after start it only appeared after a restart).
- **Tests:** `python3 -I -m unittest discover -s config/quickshell/bin/tests` (22 cases; icon regressions: browser-only page (400 for a non-browser UA) + cross-origin protocol-relative manifest icons, any beats maskable, maskable-only still used, full-bleed rounding, reinstall = new icon path + old unhashed files removed; also: typed name wins over the manifest name and replaces an old same-slug entry, name case / spaces, uninstall removes entry + exact icons only and leaves other apps / similar icon names, old-function entry, rejects `../` / bad slugs / non-`--app` entries / symlinks, missing entry = 4; first 12, local stub server on 127.0.0.1, throwaway XDG_DATA_HOME: manifest icon + name, apple-touch-icon only, favicon.ico only, nothing -> tile, SVG, oversized icon skipped, redirect loop, redirect to file:// refused, timeout, reinstall replaces, bad input, Exec quoting). Test hook `QS_WEBAPP_HELPER` replaces the helper.
- **Verified 2026-10-21:** unit tests above; isolated `quickshell -n -p` copy (scratch XDG_DATA_HOME, stub site on 127.0.0.1, notify-send shim, dev IPC hook, backdrop window, screenshots of the card looked at): search webapp / pwa ranks the row first, address prompt opens itself, probe -> "Install “Notes”" with "icon from the site", edited name installed, toast call with the icon path, launcher closed, entry + 256 px icon written, new app rows show the fetched icon and the letter tile; bad input note and "Only http and https addresses" row. qmllint clean, privacy hook dry run clean, live shell hot-reloaded. Fix + uninstall verified the same way: slow stub page, name typed during the lookup kept and installed as `webapp_example_chat.desktop` (Name exact), Enter during the lookup installs after it, empty field gets the suggestion, reinstall of the same name = one entry; Ctrl+K on that app lists Uninstall webapp (not on Firefox), first Enter arms (screenshot), second removes entry + icon, toast, row gone from the app list. **Not verified:** a real site (no network fetch of a real URL was made), launching a created app in Brave (never started against the real profile), the StartupWMClass for URLs with a path / on a non-Default profile and notification click-focus with it, real keys. The user's existing `webapp_*.desktop` entries were not touched; reinstalling them through the section overwrites the entry of the same name.

### Launcher: Tailscale and Mullvad sections (2026-10-22)
- **What:** `launcher/VpnSource.qml` (registered in Launcher.qml with `sw: VpnSwitch`, since the root qmldir singleton is not visible from `launcher/`; SectionsSource delegates enter / rows / headerSub / header / glyph for `tailscale` and `mullvad`). Parsing shared with the network popup in `launcher/js/Vpn.js` (`tsPeers`, `ago`, `mvState`, `mvDescribe`, `mvRelays`, `mvRelayLoc`, `mvChosen`, `mvFirstLine`); TailscaleSection / MullvadSection now call it instead of their own copies. Open with `qs ipc call launcher section tailscale|mullvad` or keywords (ts, tailnet, mv, vpn, ...). No hotkeys yet (SUPER+SHIFT+N / O were free in binds.lua).
- **Tailscale:** row 1 = state + switch (Enter: on = `VpnSwitch.run("tailscale")`, off = plain `tailscale down`; operator refusal adds a copy row with `sudo tailscale set --operator=$USER`), then peers (self left out) online first, green / muted dot, subtitle OS or "last seen". Enter = copy MagicDNS FQDN (wl-copy + notify-send). Ctrl+K: Copy IP, Copy name, SSH in terminal (`kitty --title "ssh <fqdn>" ssh <fqdn>`), Open in browser (`xdg-open http://<fqdn>`). Polls `status --json` every 4 s (1 s while switching) only while the section is shown. Exit-node selection not added.
- **Mullvad:** row 1 = "Connected · City, Country" / Disconnected / Error / "Not logged in" (auth_failed cause) + switch (`VpnSwitch.run("mullvad"|"mullvad-off")`), Reconnect row while on (`mullvad reconnect`), then countries ("Selected" = current `relay get` location). Enter or Right on a country = nested level "Any city" + cities (header = country name; Left / Backspace back, country re-selected; Esc closes when opened by IPC, like Twitch). Picking runs `mullvad relay set location <cc> [city]`, toasts, and calls `VpnSwitch.run("mullvad")` if the tunnel was off; launcher closes. Daemon down = copy row with `sudo systemctl enable --now mullvad-daemon`. Polls `status -j` every 2 s while shown (no `listen`), never shows the disconnected geo location, never runs `account get`.
- **Verified (nested Hyprland with a headless output, scratch copy, fake CLIs on `QS_MULLVAD` / `QS_TAILSCALE`, `VPN_SWITCH_MODE=coexist`, PATH shims for kitty / xdg-open / notify-send, scratch-only `dev` IPC hook):** rows of both sections, peer copy / Copy IP / ssh / web argv, ts off -> `tailscale down`, on -> vpn-switch -> `tailscale up`, operator refusal row, Mullvad off / on via vpn-switch, reconnect error shown on row 1, country -> cities -> back (selection kept), filter "berlin" -> Germany -> Right -> cities, city pick while connected (relay set only) and while disconnected (relay set + connect), daemon down row, error row; popup still renders with the shared parsing; real read-only state (Mullvad list of 50 countries, tailnet peers) in the nested copy; live shell hot reload clean. Screenshots looked at. Test gotcha: the nested window sat in a hidden special workspace, so grim on its output hung; a headless output (`hyprctl output create headless`) + `fixedScreen` on the scratch launcher fixed it. Also pin `HYPRLAND_INSTANCE_SIGNATURE` to the nested instance (`ls -t` picked the live one).
- **Not verified:** any real switch / relay set / reconnect from the launcher, real ssh / browser launch, "Not logged in" against a real logged-out daemon, exclusive-mode switching from the launcher (vpn-switch itself is tested).

### Launcher: VMs section (quickemu, 2026-10-23)
- **What:** `launcher/VmSource.qml` (new, in `sourceList`; SectionsSource delegates enter / header / glyph / headerSub for `vms`, `Launcher.refresh()` takes the rows from `vmSrc.listFor(q)`) + `launcher/bin/vm-ctl` (python helper, argv only, every outcome is a `notify-send -a Launcher` toast). Open with `qs ipc call launcher section vms` or keywords (vm, vms, qemu, quickemu, quickget, virtual machines). No hotkey yet.
- **VM folder:** `$LAUNCHER_VM_DIR`, else `~/.config/launcher/config.json` `"vmDir"` (`~` expanded), else `~/VMs`; `vm-ctl list` creates it. quickget / quickemu always run with cwd = that folder (quickget writes `<os>-<release>[-<edition>].conf` + folder there; the conf's `disk_img` is relative to it).
- **Rows:** "New VM..." then one row per `*.conf` (name `^[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9._+-]*$`): green dot = running (quickemu's own test: `<vmdir>/<name>.pid` holds a live PID, plus `/proc/<pid>/cmdline` is qemu), subtitle guest · disk used (qcow2 allocated blocks) of `disk_size` · running / running headless / running (SPICE) · ssh port (from `<name>.ports`); non-Linux guests say "no GPU acceleration (Linux guests only)". Polled every 2 s (`vm-ctl list`, ~15 ms) while the section is shown. Enter: stopped = `vm-ctl start DIR NAME gl` = `quickemu --vm NAME.conf --display gtk` (GTK + gl=on, so Linux guests get `virtio-vga-gl` = VirGL); running = `vm-ctl focus` = `hyprctl dispatch 'hl.dsp.focus({window="address:..."})'` of the client whose pid is the qemu PID (GTK window class `qemu`, title `QEMU (<name>)`), else the spicy window titled `<name>`, else reopens a viewer (unix-socket SPICE: quickemu; headless = SPICE on 127.0.0.1:593x: `spicy -h 127.0.0.1 -p <port>`, because quickemu's own spicy call for a TCP port lacks the host and shows a connect dialog).
- **Ctrl+K:** stopped: Start with SPICE viewer (`--display spice`: egl-headless VirGL + spicy over a unix socket), Start headless (`--display none`, toast with ssh port), Snapshots (nested level), Open VM folder (xdg-open), Edit config (`kitty -e ${EDITOR:-nvim}`, like FileSource), Delete VM (confirm; `gio trash` of the conf + its VM folder, only if that folder is a direct child of the VM folder). Running: Shut down (`quickemu --monitor-cmd system_powerdown`, the helper waits 90 s and toasts if the guest ignored it), Force stop (confirm; `quickemu --kill`). Stop / kill / delete keep the launcher open with a "shutting down..." marker on the row until the poll shows the change.
- **Snapshots level:** `qemu-img info -U --output=json` (works while running), "New snapshot..." = argument prompt (default `snap-YYYYMMDD-HHMM`, tag `^[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9._-]{0,39}$`, bad / duplicate name keeps the prompt with a note), Enter on a snapshot = restore (row confirm), Ctrl+K Delete snapshot (confirm). All via `quickemu --snapshot create|apply|delete` (offline `qemu-img snapshot`); refused while the VM runs (rows become inert). quickemu prints errors but exits 0 here, so vm-ctl also checks the output for ERROR / NOTE!.
- **New VM:** `vm-ctl oslist <cache>` = `quickget --list-csv` (~4-11 s; cached a day in `~/.cache/launcher/quickget.csv`, stale cache used if quickget fails), "Loading..." row meanwhile. OS -> release (numeric releases newest first) -> edition (if any); Right also descends (`vmNav` rows). Typing a digit or space at the OS level adds every "OS release edition" leaf, so "ubuntu 24.04" / "fedora kde" find them. Picking one first opens a small form (RAM, CPU cores, disk size override, "Download and create"; see RAM / CPU cores below), then runs `kitty --title "New VM: ..." --directory DIR vm-ctl create-run DIR os rel [ed] [--ram R] [--cores N] [--disk S]` (quickget in a terminal: progress visible, Ctrl+C cancels, toast at the end, window closes 5 s after success and waits for Enter after a failure). Chosen over a background process because downloads take minutes, quickget's progress / errors / prompts stay visible and it can be cancelled.
- **Local ISOs (2026-10-23):** first row of New VM is "From local ISO..." -> picker of `*.iso` in isoDirs (`$LAUNCHER_ISO_DIRS` colon list, else config.json `"isoDirs": [...]`, else `~/Downloads`, `~/ISOs`, the VM folder; `vm-ctl isos`, folder + one sub-level, newest first, a real file wins over a symlink to it, filterable). Typing `/...` or `~/...` there switches to path browsing (`vm-ctl isopath`, 120 ms debounce, rows not ranked: `vmSrc.unranked(q)` in `Launcher.refresh`; folder rows use the generic `descend`). Picking an ISO opens a form level: Name (prompt, default from the file name: lowercased, invalid chars -> `-`, made unique), Guest (Enter cycles Linux / Windows / Other, guessed from the file name; Linux = VirGL, Windows adds `tpm="on"` + `secureboot="off"` like quickget's Win11), Disk size (prompt, `^[1-9][0-9]{0,4}[MGT]$`, bare number = G, default 64G), "Create and boot the installer" (`vm-ctl create-local`, then `vm-ctl start ... gl`; the launcher closes via `finished()`), "Create only" (back to the list, the new row selected via `select(id)`). `create-local` writes `#!/usr/bin/quickemu --vm`, `guest_os`, `disk_img="<name>/disk.qcow2"`, `iso="<absolute path>"`, `disk_size` (same shape as a quickget conf) + an empty `<name>/`. The ISO is referenced in place (the form says moving it breaks the installer boot); a "move ISO into the VM folder" action was left out on purpose (it would move files out of ~/Downloads). Paths must be absolute `*.iso` without `"`, `$`, backquote, backslash or comma (the conf is sourced by bash, the path lands in a qemu `-drive file=`). `.img` is not offered: quickemu only attaches `img=` for macOS / Batocera guests.
- **Attach / Detach ISO (Ctrl+K, stopped VMs):** Attach reuses the picker (header "Attach ISO"); `vm-ctl attach` writes `iso=` while the disk is missing or "looks unused" (quickemu: apparent size <= 197632*8 bytes; it boots `iso=` only then and ignores it afterwards) and `fixed_iso=` otherwise (always attached as a second CD-ROM, e.g. virtio-win). Detach removes `fixed_iso=`, else `iso=`. Conf edits replace the `key=` line atomically and keep the file mode. Rows show "ISO x" (fresh VM), "CD y" (fixed_iso), "installer ISO missing: x".
- **quickemu 4.9.9 facts (verified):** `--display` gtk (default) | sdl | spice | spice-app | none; gl defaults to on (glxinfo GLSL ES >= 3, or no glxinfo); Linux guest + gtk/sdl -> `virtio-vga-gl`, spice -> egl-headless + `virtio-gpu-gl`, none -> gl off; Windows gets `virtio-vga(-gl)` but no 3D driver, macOS vmware-svga. GTK chosen over SDL: both render with GL on Hyprland, GTK keeps 1:1 pixels and has the Machine / View menu, SDL stretched the framebuffer. The conf's `display=` overrides `--display` (it is sourced after parsing). A fresh disk without iso/img is an error ("You haven't specified a .iso"). `/dev/kvm` is `crw-rw-rw-` on this host, so no kvm group needed.
- **Verified (nested Hyprland with its own XDG_RUNTIME_DIR and a headless output, scratch copy + test shell.qml with only the launcher and a `dev` IPC hook, fake `quickget` via `QS_QUICKGET`, PATH shims for notify-send / kitty / xdg-open / gio, real quickemu on a 1G blank qcow2 with a 1 MB dummy ISO; screenshots of the card looked at):** list / running state / subtitles (Linux, Windows, not installed), start (GTK + `virtio-vga-gl`, "VirGL (on)", window `QEMU (<name>)`), Enter on running focuses it from another workspace, headless start + Enter opens spicy, SPICE start, Shut down (monitor command sent; OVMF ignores ACPI so the VM kept running, as expected without an OS), Force stop with confirm, snapshot create (bad name note) / restore (confirm) / delete, Delete VM argv to gio (shim), Open folder / Edit config argv, New VM levels + back with selection kept, filter "ubuntu 24" / "fedora kde", OS list failure row, create-run in a real kitty (fake quickget) -> conf appears. Live shell hot reload clean (no new qmldir type: `launcher/` is a directory import). qmllint clean.
- **Local ISO verified (same nested rig, fake ISO dirs with 2 MB dummy files incl. spaces / quote / comma / hidden / too-deep / symlink cases):** picker list and dedupe, path browse + descend, form defaults / bad name / bad size notes / guest cycle / Create only (row selected) / Create and boot (qemu got `media=cdrom,index=0,file=<path with spaces>`, monitor `info block` showed it as ide0-cd0, window shown, the firmware tried the DVD), attach on fresh VMs (iso=) and detach in the UI, attach on an installed disk (fixed_iso=) via the CLI. Real end-to-end with a 6.4 GB Arch-based live ISO from ~/Downloads (read-only, size and mtime unchanged, VM in a scratch folder, 8G disk): created through the section, booted to the distro's installer splash with "VirGL (on)" / virtio-vga-gl (PCI 1af4:1050). Shut down sent the ACPI request but that installer splash ignored it; after 90 s the "still running, use Force stop" toast came, and Force stop worked.
- **RAM / CPU cores (2026-10-23):** form rows "RAM: auto (16G)" and "CPU cores: auto (8)" in the local-ISO form, the quickget pre-download form and Ctrl+K "Resources..." (stopped VMs; Save = `vm-ctl resources DIR NAME RAM CORES`). Enter cycles quick picks (RAM auto/4G/8G/16G/32G, cores auto/2/4/8/16, capped by the host); the row's action "Type a value..." (Right / Ctrl+K) opens a prompt: `VmSource.action` returns a prompt item and `Launcher.doAction` runs an object result with `runItem` (generic). Auto values come from `vm-ctl host`, which mirrors quickemu `configure_ram` (`free --giga` total: >=128 -> 32G, >=64 -> 16G, >=16 -> 8G, >=8 -> 4G) and `configure_cpu` (nproc >=32 -> 16, >=16 -> 8, >=8 -> 4, >=4 -> 2). Rules (QML and vm-ctl alike): RAM `<n>G|<n>M`, >= 1G, <= host RAM - 4G, M not allowed for Windows / macOS guests (quickemu compares `${RAM_VM//G/}` as an integer there); cores 1..host threads, and 1 or even when SMT is on (`cpu_cores` is the vCPU total; quickemu splits it into cores x 2 threads, so odd counts get rounded down). `ram=` / `cpu_cores=` are written only when not auto ("auto" removes the line). quickget flow: `create-run` writes the values into the conf only after quickget succeeded and only if exactly one new .conf appeared (an existing conf is left alone), so it works with the launcher closed. VM rows show "12G · 6 cores" when set.
- **RAM / cores verified (nested rig, fake quickget, dummy 2 MB ISO):** form rows, cycling, typed values with notes (70G -> "At most 60G", 5 cores -> even-number note), Create and boot with 12G / 6 -> qemu `-m 12G -smp cores=3,threads=2,sockets=1`, quickemu log "RAM VM: 12G", row subtitle; Resources Save back to auto removed both lines; quickget form argv `create-run ... --ram 8G --disk 40G`, and that argv in a real kitty with the fake quickget wrote `ram="8G"` / `disk_size="40G"` (replacing the fake's own lines); CLI: create-run with an already existing conf leaves it unchanged, out-of-range values are refused.
- **Clipboard host <-> guest (2026-10-23):** host side: Arch's qemu-ui-gtk is built WITH the GTK clipboard (`gd_clipboard_init` in `/usr/lib/qemu/ui-gtk.so`), so `vm-ctl start ... gl` now adds `--extra_args` with `-chardev qemu-vdagent,...,clipboard=on` + a virtio-serial `virtserialport name=com.redhat.spice.0` (`GTK_VDAGENT`; skipped when the conf has its own `extra_args`, because quickemu sources the conf after parsing and the conf value replaces the CLI one). quickemu wires `com.redhat.spice.0` itself only for spice / spice-app / none, so do NOT put the vdagent port into a conf's `extra_args`: verified that it breaks "Start with SPICE viewer" and "Start headless" ("Failed to start", duplicate port). Guest side (the real blocker on Hyprland guests): spice-vdagent 0.23 forces GDK's X11 backend (`gdk_set_allowed_backends`), so it owns / reads XWayland's CLIPBOARD from an invisible window, and Hyprland's XWM only syncs X <-> Wayland while an X window has keyboard focus (`xwayland/XWM.cpp`: "denying access to write to clipboard because no X client is in focus", "Ignoring clipboard access: xwayland not in focus"; since #6086). Reproduced in a nested Hyprland: an unfocused X owner -> `wl-paste` "Nothing is copied"; a Wayland copy -> `xclip -o` "target STRING not available". Fix = `config/vm-guest/vdagent-clip-bridge`, run INSIDE the guest (needs wl-clipboard, xclip, clipnotify; `exec-once` in the guest's Hyprland): Wayland -> X via `wl-paste --watch` + xclip, X -> Wayland via clipnotify + xclip -o + wl-copy (data-control, no focus needed), last text remembered so its own copies do not echo; TERM stops it and its children only. Verified in the nested Hyprland both ways (multi-line, quotes, `$`, umlaut), no churn, clean exit. Note: `info qtree` "guest on" for the port is not proof the guest agent opened it (a VM without an OS shows it too, before the guest driver negotiates multiport).
- **Not verified:** clipboard end to end through a real guest (bridge + spice-vdagent + qemu-vdagent + host GTK), a real quickget download and a real guest install, VirGL in use inside the guest (no input possible, the guest has no serial getty), a guest that honours ACPI shutdown, real `gio trash` (shimmed), real keypresses (dev hook drove the same Launcher functions), the live launcher UI (only the nested copy was looked at).

## Workspace `on_created_empty` apps (2026-10-22)
- **Cause:** the focus dispatcher (`hl.dsp.focus({workspace=})`, `ConfigActions.cpp findOrCreateWorkspace`) created a missing workspace with isEmpty=false, and `CHLWorkspace::init` only runs the rule's command when it was created empty, so the apps never launched. Verified in the source and in a nested instance.
- **Fix:** in the Hyprland fork (`~/code/hyprland-blur-test`, branch `glass-upstream-20261022`, commit "config/actions: create workspaces empty when switching to them", with a hyprtester test). Installed 2026-10-22. The temporary dotfiles workaround (switch-workspace.lua creating the workspace via `set_workspace` first) was removed again.
- **Caveats:** only fires when the workspace does not exist yet (an empty workspace that is still alive, e.g. the active one on a monitor, never re-fires). On the stock or older builds the apps do not launch; re-add the workaround if you roll back.

Notification timestamps (2026-10-23): `Notifs.times` (id -> `Date.now()` set in `onNotification`, so a replacement with the same id counts as new) plus `Notifs.now`, refreshed every 30 s by one shared `ticker` Timer that only runs while `count > 0`. `Notifs.ago(id)` formats it: "now" (<1 min), "5m", "2h" (up to 3 h), then "HH:mm" (24 h), or "ddd HH:mm" when not from today. `NotifCard` shows it as a muted caption label left of the close button, notification center only (`!toast`, toasts stay clean). Not persisted (history is not kept across reloads anyway). Verified: qmllint clean, nested Hyprland (headless output via `hyprctl output create headless`, own dbus session) screenshot shows "now" on both cards. Not verified: the older formats (m/h/HH:mm/weekday) and the 30 s refresh visually; `times` entries are never pruned (a few bytes per notification until reload).
